3.10.7 According to the Ministry, the other components of Coastal Security, which are funded and
implemented by other Ministries/Departments are as follows:–
/square4The Indian Navy has been designated as the authority responsible for overall maritime
security which includes coastal security and offshore security. The Indian Coast Guard
has been additionally designated as the authority responsible for coastal security in
territorial waters including areas to be patrolled by Coastal Police. The Director General
Coast Guard has been designated as Commander Coastal Command responsible for
overall coordination between Central and State agencies in all matters relating to coastal
Security.
/square4Ministry of Shipping has been mandated to streamline the process of compulsory
registration of all types of vessels, i.e. fishing as well as non-fishing vessels. The
Ministry of Shipping has issued two notifications in June 2009, one for amending the
Merchant Shipping (Registration of Fishing Vessels) Rules along with revised format
for registration and another for notifying the list of registrars. States/UTs are taking
follow-up actions in this regard and DG Shipping is monitoring the progress. The
National Informatics Centre (NIC) has developed an online uniform registration system
in the country. Online registration is in progress in Coastal States/UTs. The Ministry
of Shipping is also working on amendments to the MS Act, 1958 to include penalties
for non-compliance.

38
/square4 Ministry of Shipping has been mandated for installation of Automatic Identification
System (AIS) on all type of boats. The DG Shipping has issued instructions to ensure
that all types of vessels including fishing vessels and vessels of more than 20 MT
category, are installed with Automatic Identification System (AIS) Type B transponders.
Coastal States/ UTs are taking necessary steps for compliance of these instructions. A
Committee headed by DG, Coast Guard has suggested three type of technologies i.e.
Radio Frequency Identification Devices (RFID), MSS Satellite based and Automatic
Identification System AIS(P) for vessels less than 20 mtr. length. Two pilot projects
at Mumbai and Porbander are underway to test the suitability of these three
technologies under the supervision of Ministry of Defence.
/square4Department of Fisheries is taking steps to issue biometric ID cards to all the
fishermen. 18,28,912 fishermen have been identified by D/O Fisheries, M/o Agriculture
for issuance of ID cards. The consortium of Central Public Sector Undertakings has
been assigned the duty of production of cards. Till 06.03.2012, 58,599 cards have
been produced.
/square4The Registrar General of India (RGI) has been mandated to issue Multi-purpose
National Identity Cards (MNICs) to the population in the coastal villages. RGI
nominated consortium of Central Public Sector Undertakings has produced 13 lakh
cards up to 07.03.2012. The project will be completed by March, 2013.
/square4The Coast Guard is creating a chain of radar sensors along the coastline. Trials are
being carried out on the chain of radars at 38 locations on the mainland and 10
locations on the islands by Ministry of Defence. Completion of AIS chain of Radar
Sensor is expected by 2012 end. Coast Guard is also setting up 9 additional coast
guard stations on the western coastline of the country.
/square4Standard Operating Procedures (SOP) for all coastal States/ Union Territories has been
finalized and issued by Indian Coast Guard.
/square4Indian Navy has set up 4 Joint Operation Centres (JOCs) at Mumbai, Visakhapatnam,
Kochi and Portblair under the charge of existing naval C-in-Cs as the C-in-Cs Coastal
Defence for sharing of intelligence among various agencies. The JOCs are jointly
managed and operated by Navy and Coast Guard with inputs from concerned Central
and State Agencies.
/square4Sagar Prahari Bal with a special force comprising of 1000 personnel and 80 Fast
Interceptor Crafts (FICs) for force security protection of naval basis and co-located
vulnerable areas and vulnerable points has been raised by MOD. The procurement of
FICs is in process in MOD.
• Maritime Domain Awareness (MDA) – A number of agencies are working independently
in the Indian Maritime Zone. Indian Navy is undertaking MDA project for integration
of the presently available capabilities, such as National AIS chain, Long Range
Identification Tracking (LRIT) network of static sensors etc. with the following
objectives.The Note for Cabinet Committee on Security (CCS) on MDA prepared by
MOD is at the stage of inter-Ministerial consultation.
/head2rightEnable inter-agency integration
/head2rightEnhance information coalition mechanism
/head2rightIncorporate fusion, analysis, decision making tools
/head2rightacilitate efficient dissemination

39
• Joint coastal security exercises like ‘Sagar Kavach’, conducted biannually, have been
very useful and have ushered in an era of synergy in joint operations. Modalities have
been worked out to disseminate the lessons learnt in each exercise for the benefit of
all other coastal States/UTs. The lessons learnt are being communicated to all stake-
holders, to fill gaps.
• The review of the Coastal Security is regularly carried out by the National Committee
on Strengthening Maritime and Coastal Security against threats from sea (NCSMCS)
headed by Cabinet Secretary, with Naval Chief, Defence Secretary, Home Secretary,
Foreign Secretary, Secretary M/o Shipping, Secretary (BM), Secretary,
M/o Agriculture, Secretary, M/o Petroleum, Dy. NSA, Secretary, Cabinet Secretariat,
Director IB, DG Coast Guard, Chairman, CBEC, M/o Finance and Chief Secretaries/
Administrator of all Coastal States/UTs as Members. NCSMCS also coordinates among
various Central Ministries and agencies and Coastal States/UTs. The Committee has
met on five occasions i.e. on September 4, 2009; January 22, 2010, May 14, 2010,
November 23, 2010 and July 29, 2011.
3.10.8 In view of the 26/11 terror attack on Mumbai where attackers entered the city
through the sea route, the Committee understands the necessity of coastal security
mechanmism. Keeping in view that India has a vast coastline of more than seven thousand
kms, the Coast Guard, the State police and the Navy have major responsibility for coastal
security of the country. The Committee therefore, recommends that foolproof arrangements
may be made for coordination and synergy among different agencies.
3.10.9  The Committee recommends that the Government should step up coastal areas
vigilance by strengthening infrastructure for patrolling and surveillance of the coastal areas.
Executive and technical posts should be filled in at the earliest. The Committee desires that
Coastal Security Scheme, Phase-II should achieve its targets in given time period.
Necessary legal frame work such as amendment to the Merchant Shipping Act, 1958 may
be completed at the earliest.
3.10.10 The Committee impresses upon the Ministry to complete issuance of Bio-metric ID
cards to fishermen as per the target. There should not be any delay in this regard.
Appropriate technology for Automatic Identification system may be selected immediately
after the study of the pilot project. Fast Interceptor Crafts (FICs) may also be procured at
the earliest in a transparent manner.
BORDER MANAGEMENT
3.11 The principal objective of border management is to secure the country’s borders against
interests hostile to the country and to put in place systems that are able to interdict such elements
while facilitating legitimate trade and commerce within an overall strategy for improvement of
border management.
3.11.1  Infiltration from across the border has threatened internal security of India immensely.
India has been facing infiltration from across the border since long which has many negative
consequences such as smuggling of weapons, counterfeit currency and terrorists. The Ministry
was asked about present situation of infiltration from across the border and measures taken to
counter such efforts.
3.11.2 The Ministry in its reply has submitted that the cases of infiltration are reported across
various international borders and the Border Guarding Forces apprehend illegal infiltrators whenever
they come across such cases. The Ministry has furnished the details of number of persons

40
apprehended by the Border Guarding Forces at various international borders during last three years
and the current year which are as under:–
Sl. Border Persons apprehended (including foreigners)
No. 2009 2010 2011 2012
(upto February)
1. Indo-Bangladesh Border 4766 3017 2514 371
2. Indo-Pakistan Border 253 246 187 47
3. Indo-China Border — 13 20 —
4. Indo-Nepal Border 350 1069 577 48
5. Indo-Bhutan Border 11 06 09 —
6. Indo-Myanmar Border 73 157 90 47
3.11.3 In view of the above data, the Ministry contended that there is a general decline in the
number of persons apprehended by Border Guarding Forces at various international borders. The
Ministry has mentioned that the Government have adopted a multi-pronged approach to contain
cross-border infiltration which, inter-alia , includes round the clock surveillance and patrolling on
the borders and establishment of observation posts; construction of border fencing and flood
lighting; introduction of modern and hi-tech surveillance equipment; upgradation of intelligence set
up and coordination with the State Governments and concerned intelligence agencies.
3.11.4 The Ministry has submitted that it was planned to establish Integrated Check Posts at entry
and exit points on the international land borders of the country to check such threats.
3.11.5 The Ministry giving the status of proposed Integrated Check Posts at some border points,
furnished following submissions:–
• Construction of ICPs at Attari, Raxaul, Jogbani and Agartala is in progress. At
Petrapole, mobilization of men, material and machinery has been done, work is at the
start up stage. In case of Attari, 98%, Raxaul 44%, Jogbani 25% and Agartala 8% of
the construction works have been completed.
• For Attari ICP, Technical and Financial bids have been opened and approved by
Empowered Steering Committee for Package III (Signages). Work has been awarded.
The break down schedule for operationalization of ICP at Attari has been prepared.
Work is progressing as per the schedule.
• At ICP Raxaul, building structures and road base layers are complete. Physical
infrastructure is expected to be completed by June, 2012. The physical infrastructure
at Jogbani, is also expected to be completed by June, 2012. Physical infrastructure at
Agartala and Petrapole, is expected to be completed by July, 2012 and January, 2013
respectively.
• DER at Moreh has been cleared by Empowered Steering Committee (ESC). Notice
Inviting Tender (NIT) has been issued. The date of start of sale of tender document
is 27
th February, 2012.
3.11.6  The Indo-Bangladesh border is infested with illegal migration, smuggling, and trans-border
movement of insurgents, which are serious threats to the security of the country. One Member of
the Committee in its sitting held on 4
th April, 2012 raised the issue of trafficking of women, cows,
fake currency and arms near the Sundarbans on the Indo-Bangladesh border. She also raised the

41
issue of molestation of women living in border areas by security forces. The Home Secretary in
his reply submitted as under:–
“.......As far as the border with Bangladesh is concerned, there have been instances in the
past and there are regularly instances of people crossing over taking cattle from here and
driving them across the border. The Central Armed Police Forces have been trying to stop
them. In the process, our Forces have to open fire and there have been casualties. Now,
these casualties cause ripples in Bangladesh. So, we have changed our weaponry. We have
given a mix of non-lethal weapons along with rifles to our Forces so that casualties are
less. But this is happening. This issue has been taken up with Bangladesh border guarding
forces also and they have agreed to check people on their side. I was not aware of the
missing women. That is something which we will get looked into. The BSF has identified
certain areas from where these people cross. I think, there are 23 such areas. In those areas,
the BSF has strengthened the deployment. We propose to strengthen the deployments further.
We propose to increase the number of BOPs. We also propose to strengthen the fencing in
those areas....”
3.11.7 The DG (BSF) further added as under:–
“in order to handle this problem of cattle smuggling across the Indo-Bangladesh border, we
conducted a detailed analysis of the crossings based on data of last couple of years and
intelligence reports emanating from various quarters. We were able to identify 23 border
outposts and a total area of hundred kilometres, which was regarded as highly-sensitive from
the point of view of smuggling, particularly movement of cattle. This falls within the
jurisdiction of South Bengal, Malda and some parts of Assam and Meghalaya. Having
identified these stretches, we held extensive consultations and discussions with our
counterparts on Bangladesh side and we have recently agreed that we will not only
strengthen the measures which we are taking on our side, but the Border Guards
(Bangladesh) has agreed to establish more than 40 outposts exactly opposite this area of
sensitivity in order to prevent the large-scale cattle smuggling and other illegal activities
of smuggling taking place in that area. Besides this, we have sufficiently strengthened the
manpower beyond the usual along with infrastructure, night vision devices, non-lethal
weapons, as mentioned by the Home Secretary, and we have also changed our tactics and
strategy in that area to handle this problem. The consequence of this is being felt now in
the sense that the number of casualties on the border has come down considerably. The
incidents of fence breach have also started reducing and we are getting increasingly active
cooperation from our counterparts across the border.
3.11.8 According to the Ministry, Cattle smuggling across IBB (from India to Bangladesh) over
time, has developed as an organized trans-border crime because there is high consumption/demand
in Bangladesh and ample availability of cattle heads in India. The Ministry has furnished number
of cattle heads seized and persons associated with cattle smuggling apprehended by BSF on Indo-
Bangladesh Border during last two years and the current year which are as under:

3.11.10 In regard to managing Indo-Bangladesh border, the Ministry stated that Border Security
Force (BSF) is the Border Guarding Force on Indo Bangladesh Border and following steps have

/head2rightEffective domination of the border by carrying out round the clock surveillance of
the borders by patrolling laying nakas (border ambushes) and by deploying
observation posts all along the IB. Riverine segments of International Border (IB)
are being patrolled and dominated with the help of water crafts/speed boats/floating
BOPs of BSF Water Wing.
/head2rightErection of fencing on the International Border.
/head2rightFlood lighting of the border to enhance the observation during night hours.

Range Reconnaissance & Observation System (LORROS) Battle Field Surveillance
Radars (BFSR), Hand Held Thermal Imager (HHTI), Night Vision Device/Goggle
(NVDs/NVGs) etc. Constant efforts are being made to procure the latest
surveillance equipments for further enhancing the border domination.

/head2right383 additional BOPs for Indo-BD Border are being constructed.
3.11.11 An amount of Rs 986 crore has been given for border management in this year’s budget
for Indo-Pak and Indo-China border works and erection of barbed wire fencing along the Indo-
Bangla border. The Ministry when asked about the present status on the recent development in
fencing and flood lighting in border areas of the country, submitted that Fencing and Floodlighting
projects have been taken up primarily along Indo-Pakistan Border and Indo-Bangladesh Borders.

43
3.11.12 The Status of Border Fencing and floodlighting along Indo- Bangladesh Border (IBB) as
furnished by the Ministry is as under:
• The Government had sanctioned the construction of fencing along the Indo-
Bangladesh Border (IBB) in two phases.
• The total length of Indo-Bangladesh border sanctioned to be fenced under Phase-I
and Phase-II is 3436.59 km; out of which about 2760.12 km of fencing has so far
been completed.
• In addition, the Government of India has sanctioned a project (Phase-III) for
replacing the entire fencing constructed under Phase-I. So far, 790 km (out of 861
km) of fencing has been replaced under Phase-III.
• 277 km of floodlighting has been completed in West Bengal as a pilot project.
• The Government has decided to undertake additional floodlighting in 2840 km along
Indo-Bangladesh border.
• The work of floodlighting in new border areas for a length of 1015 km has been
completed.
• Additionally, in about 612 km of floodlighting, work is under progress at different
stages i.e. erection of poles, laying of cables, fitting of fixtures and energization.
• As per the present approval, the above works are targeted to be completed by
March, 2012. However, this is likely to spill over as the balance work lies primarily
in difficult stretches and involves issues like land acquisition, public habitation,
forest/wildlife clearance, fencing within 150 yards of international border etc.
3.11.13 The Status of Border Fencing and floodlighting along Indo- Pakistan Border (IPB) as
furnished by the Ministry is as under:
• The Government has sanctioned 2043.63 km of border fencing and 2009.52 km of
floodlighting along the IPB; out of which 1940.72 km of border fencing and 1878.92
km of floodlighting has been completed.
• As per the present approval (accorded on 20.07.2009), the above works are targeted
to be completed by March, 2012 or three working seasons. However, this is likely
to spill over as the balance work involves the difficult areas of the Rann of Kutch
in the Gujarat sector in which most of the pendency is due to flooding in the area.
3.11.14 The Home Secretary in the meeting of the Committee held on 4
th April, 2012 submitted
as under:–
“.....gaps which there are on the Indo-Bangladesh border. The gaps which are there are
mainly because of land acquisition. As far as I can recall, the land acquisition is
pending. Out of 700-odd kms. that are to be fenced, 104 kms. are not fenced because
of problem in land acquisition; fencing in 143 kms. was held up because of public
protests, out of which, they have got now 70 kms. Another stretch of 58.93 kms. passes
through the tiger reserve area. It needs to be cleared by the MoEF . Some 25 kms. is to
be fenced where there is an international border; it is within 150 yards of the border. That
we can do only after the concurrence of Bangladesh. We have got concurrence for 48
stretches. For 160 kms., the concurrence is awaited. The DG , BSF has spoken to his
counterpart. Another stretch of 100.95 kms. is not feasible because it is low lying and
riverine area. Here, the fencing is missing.”

44
3.11.15 The Ministry was asked about collaborative efforts with other concerned Ministries such
as External Affairs, Defence etc. taken by the Ministry of Home Affairs to improve intelligence
gathering and effective Border Management. In its written reply, the Ministry has submitted that
for effective Border Management, the Government has adopted a multi-pronged approach to contain
cross-border infiltration which, inter-alia , includes round the clock surveillance & patrolling on the
borders and establishment of observation posts; construction of border fencing and flood lighting;
introduction of modern and hi-tech surveillance equipment; upgradation of intelligence set up and
coordination with the State Governments and concerned intelligence agencies.  The Ministry has also
submitted that the various bilateral issues with different bordering countries are discussed in
bilateral meetings wherein MEA also participates.
3.11.16 The Ministry of Home Affairs has informed that it has been coordinating with various
Ministries including Ministry of External Affairs, Ministry of Defence etc. and it has been sharing
the intelligence inputs with them in order to collect the real time actionable intelligence and the
collaboration is also being done with other Central Intelligence Agencies as also the State
Intelligence agencies. For any potential security threats various Ministries work in tandem and
coordinated efforts are being made to improve effective intelligence gathering and border
management. The Ministry has further stated that in consultation with MEA and Central Ministries/
Agencies concerned, Treaties, Agreements and MoU have been signed with various countries on
Bilateral Security Cooperation; Mutual Legal Assistance Treaty on Criminal Matters; and Combating
Trafficking in Narcotic Drugs and Psychotropic Substances.
3.11.17 There were media reports that Bangladesh has rejected India’s proposal of border fencing
at the zero line along the Indo-Bangadesh border. The Chief Minister of Tripura reportedly made
a statement in the Assembly that “Border fencing at the zero line, instead of 150 yards inside India,
is crucial to protect many Government installations, markets, roads and human habitations,” The
Ministry in its reply has informed that in terms of Para-8 (e) of Indo-Bangladesh Border (IBB)
Guidelines 1975, fencing is generally constructed beyond 150 yards of the IBB. However, due to
existence of population within 150 yards of the IBB and also topographical constraints, it has
sometimes been necessary to undertake fencing within 150 yards of the IBB. The Ministry further
submitted that this activity has been objected to by the Bangladesh and their Border Guarding Force
[Bangladesh Rifles, now Border Guard Bangladesh (BGB)] of IBB and India has been trying to
persuade Bangladesh Government through meetings at different levels viz. Home Secretary, Foreign
Secretary, DG/BSF that the proposed fencing is for the mutual benefits of both the countries and
will help in curbing illegal cross-border activities.
3.11.18 The Ministry has further informed that during HS level talks held in Dhaka in August
2008, it was agreed that a joint visit could be undertaken by officials of both countries to examine
the on ground situation and reach a solution. To this end, a list of 46 patches was prioritized (7
in North-Bengal, 7 in Shillong, 15 in Tripura and 17 in Assam Man-cachar Sector) and handed over
to Bangladesh Government. According to the Ministry, High level committees have been constituted
by both India and Bangladesh for joint inspection. The Ministry has also informed that the matter
was further discussed in the HS level talks at New Delhi in November-December 2009 and it was
decided that single row fencing without gates may be constructed in these stretches. This decision
was also reiterated in the joint communiqué issued during the visit of Prime Minister of Bangladesh
in January, 2010. The issue of slow progress in joint inspection of prioritized patches and
construction of fencing in these was further discussed in the last HS level talks held at Dhaka on
19-20 January, 2011 and at New Delhi in November, 2011 and it was agreed to expedite joint
inspection of remaining patches.
3.11.19 The Ministry has further informed that out of 46 patches, 42 have been jointly inspected
and 3 have been resolved by realigning the fencing. Besides that, permission for fencing along 35

45
patches has been given while 8 patches are still under consideration with Bangladesh Government.
According to the Ministry, bilateral break-through has enabled steady, though slow progress.
3.11.20 The Ministry has furnished details of 185 such patches (within 150 yards of IBB) with
total length of about 150 Km. identified by Border Security Force (BSF) which is given below:

3.11.22 During the oral evidence of the Home Secretary before the Committee, one Member raised
the issue of status of construction on Indo-Nepal Border. The Secretary, Border Management
submitted that there is a proposal of construction of 1377 Km. road on Indo-Nepal Border out of
which 173 Km., 640 Km. and 564 Km. lie in Uttarakhand, Uttar Pradesh and Bihar, respectively.
The estimated cost of this road construction is Rs.3853 crore. He added that the construction of
said road is on progress in Bihar. He further added that in the case of Uttarakhand, it has been
granted to construct two lane 12 Km. road. He informed that in the case of Uttar Pradesh, in part

upgraded which are of 24.4 Km. and will cost Rs.111 crore. He further informed that in Siddharth
Nagar in UP 15 Km. road at the cost of Rs. 76 crore is to be constructed for which Technical
Committee has given permission. He mentioned that final permission for this road will be granted
in the next meeting of the High Level Empowerment Committee headed by the Home Secretary to
be held on 11
th April, 2012. He also mentioned that final permission has been granted for 24 Kms.
road at the cost of Rs.70 crore in Bihar and construction work has been started on this road. He
apprised that there are six more proposals from Bihar for which Technical Committee has given
permission on which High Level Empowerment Committee will hold consideration.
3.11.23 The Committee feels that though cases of infiltration from across the border and
number of persons apprehended have decreased in recent period, the Government should
not feel complacent. India is already burdened with a large number of infiltrators. The
Committee recommends that security at patches vulnerable to cattle smuggling identified by
BSF should be tightened so that smuggling of the cattle heads from India could be checked.
The Committee also strongly recommends that remaining works of fencing and flood
lighting in border areas may be completed as per targets. The Committee also recommends
that issues such as acquisition of land etc. should be resolved as soon as possible to avoid
delaying fencing and floodlighting works. The Committee also recommends that shifting of
the border fencing within 150 yards along Indo-Bangla Border may be completed soon.

46
3.11.24 In view of the fact that India has 15,106.7 Km. of land border, the Committee
believes that securing it against hostile interests and taking measures to counter threats
such as illegal migration, smuggling, infiltration by terrorists, smuggling of arms, narcotics
and FICNs while facilitating legitimate trade and commerce across the border should be the
main objective of effective border management which is vital for internal security. The
Committee also recommends that construction of 1377 Km. road on Indo-Nepal Border
should be undertaken in a time bound manner.
3.11.25 The Committee has noted that various works are under progress in all the
proposed ICPs. Several targeted dates have been given for completion of various ICPs. The
Committee recommends that there should not be any cost and time overruns. All the ICPs
should be completed and they start functioning as per the targets fixed.

CHAPTER-IV
POLICE FORCES
Central Police Forces
4.1 There are seven CPFs under the Ministry of Home Affairs, namely Assam Rifles (AR),
Border Security Force (BSF), Central Industrial Security Force (CISF), Central Reserve Police
Force (CRPF), Indo-Tibetan Border Police (ITBP), National Security Guard (NSG) and Sashastra
Seema Bal (SSB). AR, BSF, ITBP and SSB are the border guarding forces while CRPF assists the
States in matters related to law and order and is trained and equipped for internal security
management. The Rapid Action Force (RAF) and Commando Battalion for Resolute Action (CoBRA)
are specialized wings of the CRPF to deal with riots and left wing militancy respectively. CISF
provides security and protection to vital installations, Public Sector Undertakings (PSUs), Important
Private Sector Industries and Establishments, airports, industrial buildings, museums and
Government buildings. NSG is a specialized force for counter-terrorism and anti-hijacking
operations. It is also entrusted with the task of securing high risk VIPs.
4.1.1 The Committee took note of a newspaper report stating about 45,000 officers, staff
deserting Central Forces. The Chairman of the Committee raised the issue before the Home
Secretary in the meeting of the Committee held on 28
th March and 4 th April, 2012. The Home
Secretary submitted as under:-
...This news is entirely incorrectly headlined. There has been no general desertion. What has
happened and what happens every year is that there are certain number of voluntary
retirements which one can apply for and get after 20 years of service and some resignations.
The total number of voluntary retirements and resignations over a period of five years,
comes to about 50,000 for all the forces taken together. The total strength of the Forces
which we have is about 9 lakh. About 50,000 in five years means about 10,000 per year.
V oluntary retirement is a preference. It is not something about somebody wanting to go away
in between. Resignations are purely because people have got better jobs and they are better
educated and manage to get better jobs. Some of them also resign for family and personal
reasons. But the voluntary retirements are 43 to 45 thousand odd and resignations are about
five thousand odd. Now, this is over a five year period. So, the number of resignations over
the entire period in one year in all the forces is about 1000 only and voluntary retirement
is for, say, nine thousand per year. So, the percentage comes to just 1.13 per cent. It is,
in fact, lesser than the separations which one finds today in public sector undertaking,
private sector undertakings. So, this is something which has been incorrectly presented in
the news report. The percentages are not only normal but even they are less than normal.
We have total retirement of about 3.3 per cent per year.
4.1.2 Explaining about the reasons, the Home Secretary submitted as under:–
…The reasons for these are two, mainly one. We have been expanding at a rapid pace.
Every year we have been sanctioning new raising in the forces to provide for the increased
number of the BOPs etc. and to provide for our increased requirements for internal security
duties. Every year, in every force, we raise about five to six new battalions. Over the past
three years, we have raised 51 battalions.

48
4.1.3 Deliberating on the issue, the Home Secretary added further:–
...this year, we are raising 19 battalions, next year also, we are raising 19 battalions. So, that
means 19 thousand vacancies roughly. If you take 3 per cent retirement, over a force of 9
lakh, 30 thousand vacancies would be on account of that. So, that is a normal vacancy
position. So, every year, we recruit as many people also. Last year, we actually advertised 90
thousand posts for constables (GD) and we may manage to get about 70 thousand people.
So, all the posts are advertised and we will make recruitment as soon as possible.
4.1.4 The Ministry of Home Affairs in its subsequent written reply has submitted that the Central
Armed Police Forces (CAPFs) namely BSF, CRPF, CISF, ITBP, SSB and the Central Para Military
Force namely Assam Rifles have a total sanctioned strength of nearly 9 lakh and in these CAPFs
and Assam Rifles, the vacancies arise every year primarily due to the following factors:
a. Wastage/Superannuations.
b. Raising of New Battalions, creation and filling of new posts.
c. General attrition due to V oluntary Retirements, Resignations.
d. Other factors like casualties on duty etc.
e. Vacancies which remained unfilled in the past year.
f. Vacancies arising because of promotions to other ranks.
4.1.5 According to the Ministry, Such vacancies as on date are nearly to the tune of 80,000
including the NGOs and Officers. The vacancies are normally at three levels of entry, viz.
Constables, Assistant Sub-Inspector/Sub-Inspectors and Assistant Commandants. Rest of the ranks
are generally filled by promotion and the majority of vacancies in any given year are at the level
of Constable (General Duty).
4.1.6 The Ministry has apprised that during the year 2011-12, 92,168 vacancies were notified at
the level of Constable (GD) while 29370 vacancies were notified for NGOs, Officers and other
ranks. The Ministry has further apprised that it may be mentioned that while calculating the
vacancies for the year 2011-12, the vacancies for the period January-June, 2012 were added for
calculating the vacancies for the year 2011 at the level of Constable (GD) to reduce the time gap
between the occurrence of the vacancies and filling up the same.
4.1.7 The Ministry has mentioned that for filling up the vacancies at the level of Assistant Sub-
Inspector/Sub-Inspector and Constable (GD), the Staff Selection Commission (SSC) conducts the
written tests and compiles the results whereas the recruitment at the level of Assistant
Commandants is conducted by the Union Public Service Commission (UPSC) and for other
recruitments like General Duty Medical Officers, Specialist Doctors, ASI (Steno), Constable
(Tradesmen), Para-Medical Staff etc. the recruitments are done by the respective CAPFs, either
jointly or separately, through open advertisement of the posts.
4.1.8 According to the Ministry, during the year 2011-12, as per the preliminary results declared
by the Staff Selection Commission for filling 92,168 posts of Constable (GD), 57,373 candidates
have been declared selected and further Review Medical Examinations (RMEs) are in progress to
fill up the remaining vacancies. So far about 19 thousand candidates have cleared the RME. The
Ministry has added that to fill up the vacancies of the State of Jammu and Kashmir, special
recruitment rallies are being conducted in the State during the month of April-May, 2012. The
Ministry has further added that for the recruitments at the level of Assistant Commandants, UPSC
has announced the results for selection of 970 candidates for the 2010 exam and for the
recruitment for the year 2011, to fill up the 656 vacancies, the written exam was held on

49
10.10.2011 and the results of the written exam is likely to be declared on 28.05.2012. The Ministry
has also added that to fill up the 1,572 vacancies at the level of Sub-Inspector, SSC has completed
the written examination/interview and the results are likely to be declared shortly. The Ministry has
informed that for the year 2012-13, the process for recruitment of Constable (GD) and Sub-
Inspector through SSC has already started.
4.1.9 The Ministry has submitted that proper planning, well in advance, is done to fill up the
vacancies in the CAPFs which is a continuous and recurring process and no essential function of
the CAPFs is allowed to suffer on this account.
4.1.10 During the oral evidence of the Home Secretary before the Committee in its sitting held on
28
th March, 2012, one Member sought to know about the number of chances given for shooting/
firing to police personnel. The Home Secretary made the following submission:–
“Regarding inadequate firing by the CPF , it is not correct, Sir. In fact, it has been doing
firing practice regularly according to the laid down norms. All forces under the Ministry
of Home Affairs, their firing is according to the annual range classification norms laid
down which includes CRPF , CISF , SSB and ITBP. CISF is actually 94 per cent, CRPF is
according to schedule and SSB is according to schedule, ITBP is according to schedule,
only BSF is falling short in some ammunition categories, some weapon categories. But I
am sure they will pick up. They have a problem of the long range for firing of mortars.
We will try and work out a solution for that.”
4.1.11 During the sitting of the Committee held on 4
th April, 2012, one member sought to know
whether proper hearing protection and eye protection are provided to the police personnel during
the firing practices done. The Ministry in its written reply has submitted that following measures
are taken.
• Helmet Fibre Glass (FB) is provided to each individual for protection from any
fragmented element during firing. The Helmet is designed to protect the head, ear and
to some extent the eyes of the personnel from any splinter/blunt object.
• During firing practices of other heavy weapons, eye and ear protection gear is available
for the special firing detachments.
• Annual Medical check-up of officers and men have revealed no damage to eyes and
ears of personnel arising out of the routine annual firing practice.
• In addition to above, light weight ballistic protective eye wear @ 30 Nos. Per Bn. is
also authorized to special force units (COBRA Bn.) against grenade blasts.
4.1.12 The Committee notes that allocation for almost all the Central Police Forces has
been enhanced. The Committee is of the view that this enhanced allocation should be
utilized properly in terms of modernizing the weapons, gadgets etc. used by CRPF the
personnel. Besides, the personnel deployed in risk zones should be trained properly for
using hi-tech gadgets and weaponry. The Committee is also of the view that these armed
personnel should be given opportunity for firing practices at regular interval so that they
would be able to hone their skill of firing/shooting.
4.1.13 The Committee expresses its serious concern over large number of vacancies in the
para military forces especially at lower levels which could hamper anti-insurgency, anti-
naxal operations. The Committee expresses its concern that the forces are not able to fill
up the required number of posts inspite of the advertisements made. The recruitment
procedure needs to be relooked so that the vacancies can be filled up fully at regular
intervals particularly at constable level.  The Committee recommends that all vacant posts
may be filled at the earliest.

50
CRPF
4.1.14 The country’s largest paramilitary force CRPF has been allocated Rs.9,307.10 crore in the
2012-13 Budget in comparison to last year’s Rs.8762.53 crore. The proposed increased allocation
is due to enhancement of strength of CRPF personnel and consequently, additional weapons and
equipment would be required. The budget allocation is proposed to be utilized to meet these
requirements. Besides, new authorization(s) and/or replacement of old and obsolete technology
requires budgetary grant.
4.1.15 The actual non-plan expenditure during 2010-11 under Major Head 2055 was to the tune of
Rs.7136.62 crores. Allocation in BE 2011-12 was Rs. 7623.70 crores which was increased to the
tune of Rs. 8434.93 crores at RE 2011-12 stage. The same has now been pegged at Rs. 8967.04
crores in BE 2012-13. The Ministry was asked to give the reasons for variation/increase in
allocation and also on the targets set for the last three years and achieved.
4.1.16 The Ministry has given following reasons for the enhanced allocation.
• In 2009, Government has sanctioned 38 additional Battalions, including 2 Mahila
Battalions in CRPF along with support and supervisory staff to meet internal security
needs. Out of these, 4 Battalions are under raising in 2011-12 and 4 will be raised in
2012-13.
• Additional posts of 486 for Intelligence and 27 posts for Dog School have been
sanctioned to CRPF in 2011-12.
• Apart from above, to strengthen the training capacity, training infrastructure has also
been augmented for CRPF with the establishment of Counter Insurgency and Anti-
Terrorism School. CRPF Academy at Gurgaon has also been sanctioned 269 posts.
• The increased strength as well as enhancement in the rate of DA, risk allowance, cost
of ration as well as increase in increment (@ 3%) to every personnel each year has
added pay to the pay and salary bill, thereby increasing the allocation.
• Besides, the other factors are increase in Risk Allowance for 35 specified districts in
LWE area from existing CI (ops) Modified Field Area Allowance to CI (Ops) Field Area
Allowance at the rate applicable to Army personnel, Payment of training allowance to
personnel of Additional Training Centres and Payment of remuneration to ex-army
personnel appointed for Improvised Explosive Device Ops training.
4.1.17 During the oral evidence of Home Secretary, the Chairman suggested that CRPF jawans
should be posted for at least three years at one place/station.
4.1.18 The Ministry in response thereto made the following submissions:–
• In order to give adequate rest and refief to the CRPF personnel, a proper posting/
transfer policy has been laid out which ensures the movement of Force personnel
between the hard and static/soft postings. The policy has been drawn keeping in mind
the family obligations of the jawans in addition to the Force requirements. Since CRPF
is not a border guarding force, there is no fixed location/point of duty, but the same
changes as per the internal security situation in various parts of the country as well
as the request made by various State Governments/UT administrations as per
developing security scenarios.
• In order to provide adequate rest and recuperation to the CRPF personnel, it has
recently been decided that, as far as possible, the training companies shall not be
deployed for active duties as the main motive of keeping one Company out of every

51
7 Company in a Battalion as a training Company was to give proper rest and
recuperation to the jawans on rotation basis in addition to honing their skills in various
fields.
• Besides, the jawans in CRPF and other CAPFs are eligible for static posting subject
to vacancy and administrative convenience as per the following guidelines:–
a. CRPF formations/institutions/units have been categorized as “Peace” and “Field”
stations for regulating the transfer of the Force personnel.
b. After completion of 8 years field service in case of male; and 4 years field service
in case of female, after basic training, the force personnel are eligible for first
peace station/static posting. Subsequent peace station/static posting can be/is done
after completion of a total of 8 years field service from the completion of last
static posting.
c. For posting to a peace station/static location, preference is given to those having
longest service in the field Battalions.
d. Normal stay of posting in a Group Centre/static institution/peace station is 4
years. This may be extended upto 5 years only in case of training staff posted
in training institution as a special case.
e. After deployment of the Force personnel in the hard areas, like Left Wing
Extremism affected areas, they are generally posted to static postings. Such
jawans are also asked to give their preference of posting on leaving the LWE
areas out of the five options, namely Rapid Action Force/ Duty Battalions/ Group
Centre/Training Institution/Deputation.
f. Personnel having good record of service and free from disciplinary and vigilance
angle, may be posted to near their home towns, 2 years before attaining the age
of superannuation, subject to availability of vacancies.
g. In order to maintain continuity at a place, a maximum of 25% of the strength is
moved every year from one operational theatre to another within a sector.
• In order to keep the Force personnel in their cultural milieu as also for effective and
better utilization, a maximum of 50% of the total personnel posted in a place are from
the local area. In case of other Forces like BSF, as far as possible, recruit Constables
on completion of their basic training are posted to their Frontiers located near their
home states, depending upon the vacancy position.
• Therefore, all efforts are made to help the Force personnel live with their families
intermittently during the static postings /peace stations. The tenure of such peace
postings has been earmarked as 4 years after due consultation with the Force
personnel as also keeping in mind the general good of the maximum number of Force
personnel in CRPF.
4.1.19 The Committee hopes that the Ministry would utilize the enhanced amount allocated
in BE 2012-13 in modernizing the armed personnel in terms of weaponry, training etc. The
Committee takes note of inordinate delay in raising of additional battalions for which
sanction was made in 2009 itself. The Committee is of the view that the battalions proposed
may be raised at the earliest. The Government should also fill up additional posts of 486
for Intelligence, 27 posts for Dog School and 269 posts at CRPF Academy without much
delay.

52
4.1.20 The Committee observes that the Government has established the Counter
Insurgency and Anti-Terrorism School for CRPF personnel. The Committee also notes that
Risk Allowance for CRPF personnel deployed in 35 specified districts in Left Wing Extremist
areas has been increased. The Committee is however given to understand that the frequent
transfer/posting and posting of the CRPF personnel to such areas where they are not able
to communicate due to language problem add to their woes and adversely affect their
working proficiency. The Committee, therefore, recommends that a proper policy should be
formulated on transfer/posting of the CRPF. The Committee further recommends that in
order to give adequate relief to the personnel of all Central Police Forces a posting/transfer
policy should be reflected on ground. The Committee is of the view that personnel working
in CRP should be stationed in a particular place for, at least, three years without
disturbance unless they are hard-pressed to go to some other area because moving from one
place to other in a short span of time causes difficulties not only to them but also to their
families. The Committee is also of the view that these personnel should be posted to the
extent possible in the areas where they know the local language and culture so that there
is no communication gap between the locals and the CRPF personnel.

4.1.24 The Ministry furnished following detailed reasons for the increase in the allocation for
BSF:–
• During 2008-2011, 116 additional battalions have been sanctioned to the CAPFs to
address the security requirement, both internal and along the international border. Out
of this BSF was sanctioned 29 additional battalions in 2009, of which 07are to be
raised in 2012-13.

53
• Apart from the above new battalions, 1691 additional posts have been sanctioned in
BSF in Intelligence Wing, Air Wing, Marine Coy and Engineering Wing.
• To strengthen the training capacity of the forces, Government has taken a policy
decision to increase the training capacity of the CAPFs. In this line Government has
sanctioned augmentation of existing training institutions and creation of new training
institutions. In 2011 BSF has been sanctioned augmentation of training facilities at 09
Subsidiary Training Centres, up-gradation of 2 Basic Training Centres into 2 Subsidiary
Training Centres and BSF Academy. Tekanpur. Hence adequate provision has been
made in the budget to facilitate augmentation of these training institutions.
• The increased strength as well as enhancement in the rate of DA, risk allowance, cost
of ration as well as increase in increment to every personnel each year (@ 3%) has
added pay to the pay & salary bill, thereby increasing the allocation.
4.1.25 The Committee takes note of the enhanced allocation for BSF in BE 2012-13 in
comparison to last year’s allocation in RE 2011-12. The Committee hopes that increased
allocation would be utilized in procuring modern weapons, equipments, gadgets and vehicles
for BSF and/or enhancement of strength of BSF personnel in a time bound manner.  The
Committee recommends that both physical and financial targets should be met.
4.1.26 The task of raising seven additional battalions during 2012-13 out of the 29
sanctioned battalions may be completed as per the proposal. Besides, other sanctioned posts
should also be advertised and filled up at the earliest. The Committee hopes that
augmentation, upgradation of existing training facilities for BSF personnel would be
completed in time bound manner.

4.1.30 The Ministry has stated that after Operation Black Tornado at Mumbai on 26
th Nov 2009,
four Regional Hubs were set up at Mumbai, Hyderabad, Kolkata and Chennai, with strength of 241
personnel at each Hub. The Ministry has further stated that the strength of NSG was also increased
at Manesar, Samalakha and Headquarters by 1081 personnel. Government has also sanctioned two
Regional Centres of NSG with strength of 5150 personnel each (The strength of Regional Hubs
at Kolkata and Hyderabad would be subsumed in Regional Centres).
4.1.31 The attention of the Home Ministry was drawn to the reports of country’s elite counter-
terrorist force NSG, functioning without its top operations commander for almost six months. The
Ministry in its written reply submitted as under:-
“The post of IG (Ops) in NSG remained unfilled for a period of more than 06 months due
to unavailability of suitable candidate. As per the Recruitment Rules, the post of IG(Ops)
in National Security Guard (NSG) is filled on deputation basis from amongst Officers of
the Army holding the rank of Major General. Accordingly MHA requested Ministry of
Defence, on 12.5.11, to provide a panel of suitable officers of the rank of Major Generals
as the incumbent was due to retire on superannuation on 31.07.2011. MoD forwarded a
panel of 03 officers along with requisite documents on 26.05.2011. In the meantime, Maj
Gen R S Pradhan, SM, VSM, AVSM proceeded on premature repatriation on his promotion
in Army on 11.07.2011. After careful examination of the available profiles, the Screening
Committee as well as competent authority observed that officers in the panel had not
enough experience in the relevant area to hold the post of IG (Ops) in NSG . MoD was
again requested on 05.09.2011 to forward a fresh panel of officers. Fresh panel of officers
was forwarded by MoD vide their OM dated 19.09.2011. The panel was sent to NSG on
26.9.2011 for doing a preliminary check regarding suitability for the job and was received
on 4.10.2011. Screening Committee in its meeting held on 24.10.2011 selected Maj Gen S.K.
Chakravorty as the most suitable for appointment as IG (Ops), NSG on deputation basis.
The proposal for appointment of Maj Gen S.K. Chakravorty as IG(Ops)was sent to the
Department of Personnel & Training on 18.11.2011 for obtaining the approval of the
competent authority. DoPT sought certain clarifications which were duly replied by MHA.
Finally, the approval of the competent authority of the Cabinet was communicated to MHA
by Department of Personnel & Training on 13.02.2012 and order for appointment of Maj
Gen S.K. Chakravorty to the post of IG(Ops), NSG was issued on 14.02.2012. There is
already a system in place to ensure filling up of the post well in time. The present case
was an isolated one wherein panel of officers had to be called twice resulting in the post
remaining vacant.”
4.1.32 NSG Force Headquarter is located in Delhi and it has training centre at Manesar in Gurgaon
(Haryana). During the oral evidence before the Committee in its sitting held on 4
th April, 2012, the
Home Secretary was asked to furnish a Report on progress of setting up of NSG Hubs in the
country. The Ministry in its written reply has submitted that the Government has established four
Regional Hubs of the National Security Guard (NSG) at Hyderabad, Mumbai, Chennai and Kolkata
with a strength of 1086 personnel, i.e. 241 personnel in each Regional hubs and 122 personnel for
Admn., support and Coordination. The Government had also sanctioned Rs.186.37crore in June,

55
2009 [Chennai (Rs.54.12 crore), Hyderabad (Rs.37.94 crore), Mumbai (Rs.56.10 crore) and
Kolkata (Rs.38.21 crore)] for construction of pre-fab and permanent structures required for these
hubs. According to the Ministry, these hubs have been operationalised on 30
th June 2009/1 st July,
2009. Construction of the required permanent infrastructure has also been completed.
4.1.33 The Ministry has further informed that the Government has also approved establishment of
NSG Regional Centres at Hyderabad and Kolkata and for this purpose about 600 acres of land has
already been acquired at Hyderabad for which Lay Out Plan (LOP) has been prepared. The
Government has also decided to enhance the strength of Regional hub at Kolkata from 241 to 460.
West Bengal Housing Infrastructure Development Corporation Limited (WBHIDCO) has agreed to
provide 34.315 acres of land to NSG at New Town Kolkata for raising of the additional
infrastructure required for the enhanced strength of this Regional hub and an amount of Rs.34.315
crore has been sanctioned to NSG on 29.3.2012 for making payment to WBHIDCO.
4.1.34 The Ministry has apprised that no new NSG hubs are under construction. However, on
receipt of a request from Government of Gujarat, the Government of India has conveyed “in
principle” approval for setting up of a NSG Regional Hub in Gujarat, subject to the Government
of Gujarat providing land free of cost, at the location found suitable by the NSG.
4.1.35 The Committee hopes that the increased allocation for Office Building under plan
Head would be utilized in proper manner for creation of world class infrastructure of NSG
Hubs at Chennai, Kolkata, Hyderabad and Mumbai and Regional Centre at Hyderabad as
proposed. The physical and financial targets should be achieved and there should not be any
cost/time overruns.
4.1.36 The Committee appreciates that the NSG Personnel are being given allowance of 25%
as NSG Allowance and the Government has proposed an award of Special Duty Medal for
recognition of service in NSG. The Committee recommends that all necessary steps be taken
to ensure that the awards to the deserving NSG Personnel should be started from the
current year itself.
4.1.37 The Committee recommends that the sanctioned/proposed two new regional Centres
at Hyderabad and Kolkata should be established at the earliest. The Committee feels that
in the proposed plan, the whole northern and western part of the country is supposed to be
looked after by the Delhi Headquarters. The Committee suggests that Government may
consider setting up one more regional centre of NSG at appropriate location in northern
India. The Committee takes serious note of the fact that country’s counter-terrorist force
NSG functioned without its top operations commander for almost six months. The
Committee recommends that this kind of situation should not be allowed to emerge in
future, which could have demoralizing effect on the force.

59
almost all heads of account. The Ministry has submitted that allocation of Rs.2225.00 crore under

4.1.54 During the oral evidence before the Committee, some Members raised concerns over
reported mal-practices in purchases in Assam Rifles, particularly in the ‘less than Rs.25 lakh’
category. One Member alleged that below rupees 25 lakh tenders are not floated in the papers.
Some unscrupulous businessmen from Delhi managed all the purchase with the help of some junior
level officers. He further mentioned that during the last three years, all the purchase has been made
from the Delhi-based businessmen and no local person was encouraged.
4.1.55 The Ministry in its written reply has explained as under:–
“No malpractices are there in any purchases carried out by Assam Rifles in tender value
less than Rs.25 lakh category or even above Rs.25 lakh category. In order to curb
malpractices, all purchases for tender value less than Rs.25 lakh are invited either through
open tender inquiry or through limited tender inquiry, with the approval of competent
financial authority. In case of limited tender inquiry only the registered vendors of DG ,
Assam Rifles are invited. The tender inquiry in this case is also published on the Assam
Rifles web site, thereby giving wide publicity to all registered vendors. The opening of
technical bid and price bid are through an independent board of officers to avoid any
chance of malpractices.”
4.1.56 The Committee is constrained to note that the allocated amount in BE 2012-13 to
Assam Rifles for the financial year 2012-13 is not matching with projected demand of the
Ministry. The Committee recommends that the Government may consider enhancing the
amount if required at RE stage. The Committee notes that the Government has approved
restructuring of Assam Rifles. The restructuring envisages creation of 1035 posts, three
Sectors Head Quarters, one IG Head Quarters, one maintenance group and one workshop.
The Committee recommends that the restructuring of Assam Rifles should be completed at
the earliest and in a time bound manner for which a periodic review may be chalked out
to avoid delay.
4.1.57 The Committee expresses its concern over the reported malpractices in purchases
made by Assam Rifles, particularly in the ‘less than Rs.25 lakh’ category which have
benefited some well connected businessmen. Though the Ministry has denied any such
malpractices, the Committee recommends that no scope should be left for any such
complaints. A thorough investigation may be conducted into such complaints.

jackets and Helmets etc. as envisaged. All gadgets, should be of the international standards.
DELHI POLICE
4.2 Delhi Police is the primary law enforcement agency of the National Capital of India. It
upholds the law and provides assistance to the people when necessary and in times of emergency,
disaster and crisis. The function of Delhi Police includes maintaining peace and order in the city;
protect the citizen’s life and property; prevent and detect crime; bring offenders to justice; manage
the traffic; uphold the law and provide policing service.
4.2.1 The allocation made for Delhi Police in RE 2011-12 was Rs. 3290.40 crore which has been
increased to Rs. 3494.83 crore in the BE 2012-13. According to the Ministry, the increase is due
to the provision made for posts likely to be filled during the year 2012-13 and additional provision
for payment of anticipated increase in Dearness Allowance (DA).
4.2.2 The allocation made in BE 2011-12 is Rs.82.00 crore for developing traffic and
communication network in NCR Mega cities and model traffic system which has been reduced to
Rs.20.00 crore in BE 2012-13. The Ministry was asked whether this reduction will adversely affect
traffic system of the city and NCR. The Ministry has explained that Budget Estimates have been
kept at Rs.20.00 crore during 2012-13, which is mainly meant for Cyber Highway component to
be implemented through MTNL and a token provision has been kept for Intelligent Traffic System
(ITS) component. The Ministry has further explained that Delhi Traffic Police will continue to
manage traffic with the existing resources till implementation of the scheme of ITS project and
reduced provision will not adversely affect traffic system of the city and NCR.
4.2.3 The Ministry was asked about the number of complaints for not registering FIRs by the
Police Personnel at the Police Stations in Delhi received during the year 2010, 2011 and 2012,
year-wise. The Ministry was further asked about number of such complaints which were found
to be genuine; the details of action taken against erring personnel and the actions proposed to be
taken to see that the basic right of the victim of crime is protected. The Ministry of Home Affairs

4.2.5 Accor ding to the Ministry, in order to ensure and extend all support to the victim, several
initiatives have been taken by Delhi Police which are as follows:
• All ranks are briefed to take prompt action on complaints received and keep the victims
informed from time to time on the action taken and also to make sure that they are
free from any threat/pressure/duress from the alleged party.
• Women Help Desks have been established in all Police Stations for women
complainants.
• Juvenile Welfare Officers have been appointed in all Police Stations to deal with
juvenile victims of crime as well as children in need of care.
• Rape Crisis Intervention Centres (RCIC) along with NGOs are working in all districts
to provide medical counseling as well as legal counseling to victim of rape and physical
violence. Besides, a Special Police Unit for Women and Children has been established
at Nanak Pura, New Delhi, where cases of domestic violence and complaints of
torture, dowry demands etc. are specifically looked into and provision of professional
counseling and mediation through the Delhi Legal Aid Services is provided. Similarly,
crime against women cells in the district are also functioning to look specifically into
complaints of women at the district level.
• Identity of the victim of crime is not disclosed in cases of minors as well as women
and in sensational cases to protect victims.
• Citizen’s charter of Delhi Police, giving details of redressal mechanism as well as Delhi
Police Help lines has been uploaded on the Delhi Police website and displayed
prominently in all district headquarters. The following important Helplines have been
established by Delhi Police to benefit victims. These helplines are functioning 24X7.
Some of the main helplines are:–

a. Women Helpline.
b. Child Helpline.

d. Anti Stalking Helpline.
e. Anti-Obscene Call Helpline.
f. Inform about Crime Helpline.

4.2.11 According to the Ministry, as regards crime figures, while there was marginal increase in
the total IPC crime reported, crime under various heads like dacoity, murder, riot, kidnapping for
ransom, burglary and theft declined. The Ministry has informed that the yardstick used the world
over to compare crime is in respect of per lakh of population and the total IPC Crime per lakh
of population has seen a steady decline during the last 14 years, from 505.71 in 1998 to 318.47
in 2011. The Ministry has further informed that during the last three years, the crime per lakh of
population remained almost at the same level, with the total IPC crime per lakh of population being
312.60 in 2009 and 318.47 in 2011. The Ministry has apprised that in 1998 the population of Delhi
was 128.3 lakh and total IPC crime was 64882 whereas, while the population rapidly rose to 167.53
lakh in the year 2011 (source of population : Census-2011), the total IPC crime remained only 53353
clearly indicating it was better than 14 years back when population was almost 39 lakh less.

64
4.2.12 The Ministry has informed that the ratio of police-men to population in Delhi in 2011 was
1:200 (approx.) and the population density per police station in Delhi is 1,00,000 – 1,25,000 (approx.).
4.2.13 The Chairman and the Members of the Committee in its sitting held on 4 th April, 2012 in
which Police Commissiner of Delhi was also present along with the Home Secretary, raised the
issue of cases of molestation, eve-teasing and rape of particularly North-East girls in Delhi.
4.2.14 The Police Commissioner of Delhi on the issue made the following submission:–
“...I come to the safety of the North-Eastern girls. Sir, in the year 2010, there was a bad
incident in the month of November and one girl from the North-East, who was dropped by a
BPO vehicle at odd hours, was raped. We arrested five persons. Thereafter, in one year, there
has been only one case of rape of a woman from the North-East. There are only 5-6 cases of
molestation. It is less than one per cent of the total crimes. What we have done is, wherever
North-East women or girls are staying, suppose they are staying in a pocket of 300, we have
deployed a PCR van, there is a picket and there is motorcycle patrolling. There are three women
Deputy Commissioners. In the North Campus, there is a woman officer and in the South Campus
also, there is a woman officer. Every second month, they hold a meeting with the students and
the people from North-East. We are quite conscious of this fact that girls coming particularly
from the North-East have to be provided security. We have also issued a direction under Section
144 Cr.PC saying that media houses, hotels, malls, BPO employers have to drop all these girls
at their respective places of residence. There are as many as 5000 vehicles which ply in Delhi
between 12 o’clock at night till early morning. They ply from Noida and Gurgaon up to Delhi.
In fact, in certain cases, we prosecuted BPO employers when they dropped the girl in the middle
of the road at 2 o’clock in the night. Now, there is a very stringent order and, fortunately, no
incident has taken place. We know what happened in Gurgaon the other day. So, they have to
be dropped at their places of residence. So, we are constantly interacting with the students, with
the Presidents of the North-Eastern Student Associations. Every second month, there is a meeting
and wherever there is a slight gap, we are upgrading. For both North and South Campuses, we
have created police stations which are headed by women officers and about 80 per cent of the
staff is women officers.”
4.2.15 A blast in an Israeli Embassy car in a high-security area Delhi occurred by using sticky
bomb. The Ministry was asked about measures to be adopted to stop this kind of incident involving
foreign diplomats in near future. The Ministry has submitted that the said incident is being
investigated by Delhi Police. The Ministry has further submitted that Security arrangements in VVIP
areas in Delhi, including areas where most of the diplomatic missions and residences of foreign
diplomats are located, are reviewed periodically on the basis of inputs given by the Central Security
Agencies and appropriate advisories are issued to Delhi Police. The Ministry has apprised that the
actual arrangements based on these inputs and advisories, as well as local threat assessments, are
made by Delhi Police and Delhi Police has taken additional measures including extensive patrolling
with Police Control Room Vans, surveillance systems, deployment of pickets at vulnerable points
etc. as also installation of CCTV at prominent markets, important areas, border check posts etc.,
in order to strengthen security.
4.2.16 In recent period Delhi has faced many terrorist attacks in high security areas. In view of
this Installation of CCTV surveillance system at 38 locations in Delhi was proposed to be
undertaken. The Ministry was asked to furnish the locations where CCTVs are proposed to be
installed; by when the CCTV surveillance will be functional there and what steps are proposed to
taken to ensure that this system does not become defunct due to lack of maintenance.
4.2.17 The Ministry has informed that Installation of CCTV Surveillance system at 38 locations
under phase 11(A) is being undertaken by Delhi Police through M/s ECIL. According to the
Ministry, as per agreement between Delhi Police and M/s ECIL, the installation is to be completed
within 150 days from the receipt of permission from the civic authorities viz. NDMC, MCD, DDA,
etc. and these CCTV surveillance systems will be under guarantee/warranty for a period of 3 years

4.2.18 The Ministry was asked whether the present approach of the Ministry of Home Affairs
towards internal security has proved effective during last two years and whether any renewed
approach has been reflected in the current budget proposals. The Ministry in its written reply has
stated that the budget proposals are reflective of the present approach of the Ministry of Home
Affairs towards internal security and the budget proposals deal with both aspects of the approach,
namely, strengthening and expanding existing capabilities and instrumentalities in the realm of
internal security and the creation of new capabilities and instrumentalities to deal with emerging and
future challenges. The Ministry has further stated that the expansion of NIA and the steps for
setting up of NATGRID are examples of developing new organizational capabilities to deal with the
new challenges.
4.2.19 The Committee is concerned over the fact that there are large number of complaints
for not registering FIRs by the Police Personnel at the Police Stations in Delhi. The
Committee apprehends that actual figures may be bigger than the number of complaints
came to light since most of the people especially poor and illiterate ones might have
returned without lodging their complaints which have not been reflected in the figures
during the year 2010, 2011 and 2012. The Committee recommends that there should be a
separate cell of Delhi Police for registering such complaints where representatives of NGOs,
local bodies etc. may be involved so that people trying to lodge such complaints would do
it. The Committee is of the opinion that non-registering of FIR complaint by police
personnel should be viewed as dereliction of duty as well as unbecoming of the police
officers. The Government may consider setting up an authority to look into complaints
against Delhi Police. The Committee is exasperated over the fact that although 877 and 941
such complaints were registered against non-registering of complaints in 2010 and 2011
respectively, however, only 33 and 46 cases were found to be genuine. The Committee is of
the considered view that stringent action is required to be taken against the concerned
officers for non registering of FIRs.
4.2.20 The Committee takes serious view of the fact that 72 police stations out of 180 police
stations are functioning without regular buildings. It is very unfortunate that in the Capital
city of the country, the police stations are functioning without regular buildings. The
Committee recommends that the Government should take immediate steps to provide
regular buildings to these 72 police stations at the earliest.
4.2.21 As regards crime figures, the Committee takes serious note of the increasing cases
of molestation, rape and kidnapping during the last three years. The Committee is of the
view that prevention of crime against women should be a priority issue of the Delhi Police.
The Committee understands that most of the crimes against women go unreported due to
non-cooperating attitude of police personnel in Delhi. In view of this, the Committee
recommends that more and more women officers should be employed at lower as well as
higher level in Delhi Police so that female victims would approach them without hesitation.
4.2.22 The Committee takes note of the fact that the figures given by Delhi Police do not
match the cases reported in media about rape and molestation. Stringent action should be
taken against the culprits involved in harassing girls. The Committee understands that
merely counting the number of incidents does not justify the general perception. The
Committee believes that a mass campaign should be organised by Delhi Police against such
incidents.
4.2.23 The Committee takes serious view of the bomb blast in an Israeli Embassy car in
a high-security area which occurred in Delhi using sticky bomb. The Committee
recommends that proper security arrangements should be made by Delhi Police in
Diplomatic areas so that this kind of incident does not happen in future.

67
4.2.24 The Committee is of the considered view that Installation of CCTV surveillance
system at 38 locations in Delhi should be expedited.
MODERNIZATION OF ST ATE POLICE FORCES (MPF)
4.3 The Ministry of Home Affairs has been implementing a Non-plan Scheme for Modernization
of State police forces since 1969-70 to supplement the efforts of the State Governments in
modernizing their police forces. The Scheme provides assistance to the State Governments, as 50%
grant-in-aid and 50% loans for purchase of vehicles, equipment for forensic science labs, finger
print bureau, police training, weaponry, construction of housing for police personnel, police
stations, training buildings etc. An equal matching contribution was expected from the State
Governments.
4.3.1 The main objective of the scheme is to meet the identified deficiencies in various aspects
of police administration, worked out in 2000 by the Bureau of Police Research and Development
(BPR&D) in a study, according to which a requirement of about Rs.25,000 crore, was needed for
the purpose. Another objective of the scheme is to reduce the dependence of the State
Governments on the Army and Central Para Military Forces to control internal security and the law
and order situation. The above non-Plan Scheme has been under implementation in States since
1969-70 on a cost sharing (50:50) basis.
4.3.2 The Ministry has submitted that the Annual Action Plans under the MPF Scheme are
formulated by States based on the available Central allocations and also depending on the assessed
requirements of State police forces. The naxal affected States have, inter-alia, made provisions for
items such as troop carriers, motor cycles, modern weaponry, BP vehicles, mine-protected
vehicles, BP jackets and helmets, BP patkas, BP morchas, night vision equipment etc. in their MPF
Annual Action Plans for 2011-12, based on their requirements.
4.3.3 The Ministry was asked about State/UT-wise budget allocation so as to help them
modernizing their police forces and whether these allocations were according to the demands of
the States/UTs. The Ministry in its written reply has informed that that the BE provision of Rs.1111
crore for the financial year 2011-12 under the Scheme has been reduced to Rs.800 crore at RE
2011-12 stage.
4.3.4 The Ministry was asked whether the fund meant for modernization of Police Forces would
be sufficient and the status of utilization certificates sent by the States during last three years. The
Ministry has replied that the Budgetary allocation for MPF Scheme is provided in the Demands for
Grants under the following budgetary heads:-
• Major Head “3601” – Grants-in-aid to State Governments (Major Head) – 01 – Non-
Plan Grants (Sub-Major Head) – 01.116– Police – Modernisation of Police Force – 01
– Strengthening of State Police Organizations – 01.00.31 – Grants-in-aid under Grant
No.54 – Police for 2011-12.
• Major Head “2055” – 115 - Modernization of Police Force (Minor Head) – 08 –
Assistance to State Police Organization in kind – 08.00.31 – Grants-in-aid –under
Grant No.54 – Police for 2011-12.
4.3.5 The Ministry has informed that under Major Head 3601, grants-in-aid are released to State
Governments through RBI and Major Head 2055 is operated for release of grants-in-aid for release
to State Police Housing Corporations (towards construction activities) and to Ordnance Factory
Board for providing arms and ammunition to State police forces.
4.3.6 The Ministry has further informed that MPF Scheme has been extended twice during the
last two years ( i.e.) 2010-11 and 2011-12 and A proposal for further continuation of the MPF

68
Scheme for five years with effect from 2012-13 has been placed before the Committee of
Secretaries on 22.3.2012 for consideration.
4.3.7 The Ministry has apprised that the Central allocation approved by the Cabinet under the
MPF Scheme in the year 2005 was Rs.1645 crore. However, the funds made available under the
Scheme during the period 2005-06 to 2010-11 have never reached that limit. The Ministry has
further apprised that Rs.1111.00 crore allocated in the BE 2011-12 has also been reduced to Rs.800
crore in RE 2011-12. According to the Minstry, in the BE 2012-13 provision for the Scheme for
Modernization of State Police Forces is Rs.900 crore (Rs.250 crore under Major Head ‘2055’ and
Rs.650 crore under Major Head ‘3601’). The Ministry feels that this budgetary provision is lesser
than the amount sought for the State police modernization scheme. During the oral evidence before
the Committee in its sitting held on 4
th April, 2012, the Home Secretary submitted as under:–
“After the last five-year plan of modernisation of State police force expired, we had
proposed an extension for another five years. This extension is still under consideration in
consultation with the Ministry of Finance. We have had discussions with the Ministry of
Finance. All those discussions have reached a certain stage. In the meantime, till that
scheme was finalised, they extended it for one year, that is, for the last year and now again,
they have extended it for this year. They have made an interim provision of Rs.900 crore.
I agree that it is not sufficient for the requirements which are there and we have been
saying that we need much more funds. We have conveyed this to the Ministry of Finance
as well as to the Planning Commission. I am quite sure that when the scheme is sanctioned,
we will get more funds. So, the provision of Rs.900 crore is an interim provision. This is
not the final provision. I am quite sanguine; I am quite confident that when the scheme
is sanctioned, the provision will be much more because much more is required. I do believe
that. In fact, when we started off in the year 2000, the provision was Rs.1,000 crores. So,
Rs.900 crore for this year is absolutely inadequate, as far as the modernisation of State
police force is concerned. It is under funded, and, I think, the Committee can make a
recommendation.”
4.3.8 Regarding rendition of Utilization Certificates for last three years, i.e. 2008-09, 2009-10 and
2010-11 by States, the Ministry has mentioned that the States have unspent balances of Rs.47.83
crore for 2008-09 and Rs.194.76 crore for 2009-10 lying with them. According to the Ministry,
the Utilization Certificates for funds released in the year 2010-11 shall become due on 1.4.2012.
The Ministry of Home Affairs has urged the State Governments in various fora to utilize the
unspent Central funds for the intended purpose and furnish UCs to the Ministry of Home Affairs.

4.3.11 According to the Ministry, the posts in State Police Forces remain vacant due to various
reasons such as retirement, resignation, promotion of police personnel, lengthy recruitment process,
etc. The ‘Police’ being a State subject as per VII Schedule to the Constitution of India, it is the
responsibility of the State Governments to fill up the vacancies in police forces and recruitments
to State Police Forces are done by the State Governments themselves and no funds are sanctioned
by the Ministry of Home Affairs for the purpose to the States. The Ministry has apprised that the
State Governments have been advised from time to time in various fora by the Ministry of Home
Affairs to fill up the existing vacancies in the State Police Forces.
