4.3.13 Du ring the oral evidence of the Home Secretary before the Committee, one Member raised
the issue of Policemen not doing shooting/firing practices in the States. The Ministry in its written
reply has submitted that Police being a State subject as per the Seventh Schedule to the
Constitution of India, the question relating to lesser or no firing practice by State police personnel
is not within the purview of Central Government. The Home Ministry has further submitted that
the Central Government, however, provides funds to the States under the Scheme for
Modernization of State Police Forces (MPF Scheme) to help them upgrade the equipment,
weaponry, mobility, security gadgets, etc. The Ministry apprised that as far as Central Armed Police
Forces are concerned, the police personnel regularly undergo fire practice except BSF which is
having some problem in terms of availability of long firing ranges.
4.3.14  During the oral evidence of the Home Secretary before the Committee on 4 th April, 2012,
a Member of the Committee raised the issue of obsolete weapons being produced by the ordinace
factories and used by the police forces. The Home Secretary while replying made following
observations:–
“As far as weapons are concerned, there are two categories. The first one is SLR (Self
Loading Rifles), INSAS Rifle, etc., which are the basic weapon for the infantry in the
Army as well as for our CPMFs. Apart from this, there is special equipment. The
special equipment is something which we buy on a global tender basis. So, we are
buying MP3, MP5 Sub-Machine Guns through the process of global tender. We buy
pistols and we also buy Glock Pistols through open tender. The MP3, MP5 and Glock
Pistols are not only for NSG but are also meant for all our forces. We are buying semi-
automatic weapons from Beretta through global tender. So, it is not that we are
depending only on the Ordinance Factories for our specialized weapons. As far as
INSAS Rifle is concerned, today, there may be better rifles. I cannot say INSAS is the
most modern; it is not. There will be more modern weapons. When we find that our
objectives are not served by INSAS Rifle, we will then think about switching over and
go in for global tender. But, basically, when something is being manufactured in our
country, in a facility which is owned by the Government and if it is something which
meets our requirements, we would rather buy it from within the country than importing
the same. Giving ammunition to the State Police Force is something which we can
propose. But, I can virtually guarantee that the Ministry of Finance will not agree. I
can guarantee. But, nonetheless, I will try to include it as one of the items under the
MPF Scheme. In fact, Sir, when we want to include, along with the procurement of
weapons under MPF , we said that you also allow procurement of some ammunition to
go with the equipment. So, after a lot of discussion, it was allowed. But, for revenue
expenditure, the Ministry of Finance is very, very reluctant to sanction any funds for
revenue expenditure under the MPF Scheme. But, we shall try to do it.”
4.3.15 The Committee takes serious note of the fact that the BE provision for the financial
year 2011-12 under the Scheme for Modernization of State Police Forces (MPF) of Rs.1111
crore was reduced to Rs.800 crore at RE 2011-12 stage and again in the BE 2012-13 pr ovision
for the scheme is Rs. 900 crore. The Committee feels that this lesser budgetary provision
is bound to affect modernization of State police forces. The Ministry apprised the Committee
that the funds are inadequate and they would move for additional funds at RE stage. The
Committee strongly recommends that additional funds sought by the Ministry should be
allocated at RE 2010-11 stage for modernization of State police forces, which is sin quo non
for internal security.

74
4.3.16 The Committee understands that this police modernization scheme plays an
important role in modernizing the police forces of States/UTs. In view of this, the
earmarked provision for the scheme must be utilized fully and optimally by the concerned
States/UTs. The Committee suggests that the State/UT Governments should submit
utilization Certificates in time so that they could avail of the funds from the Centre under
the scheme. The Committee is unhappy to note that States/UTs have unspent balances of
Rs.47.83 crore for 2008-09 and Rs.194.76 crore for 2009-10 lying with them. The Committee
recommends that the Ministry of Home Affairs should take up this matter at Chief
Ministers’ level to impress upon the State Governments to fully utilize the fund under the
scheme in a time bound manner.
4.3.17 The Committee notes the details of State-wise sanctioned and actual strength of total
police force and the police population ratio and expresses its displeasure over the fact that
some States are having vacancies of even more than 50%. The Committee recommends that
the Ministry of Home Affairs should impress upon the State Governments for filling up
vacancies at the earliest. The Committee desires that the Ministry of Home Affairs should
link this issue with provision under scheme of Modernization Police Forces (MPF) and the
fund for States lagging in filling vacancies may be deducted.
4.3.18 The Committee agrees that Police being a State subject as per the Seventh Schedule
to the Constitution of India, the question relating to lesser or no firing practice by State
police personnel is not within the purview of Central Government. However, the Committee
desires that the Ministry of Home Affairs may issue an advisory suggesting all the State/
UT Governments to make their police force personnel undergo fire practices at regular
intervals as in the case of CPFs personnel.
4.3.19 The Committee also recommends that the Ministry should impress upon the States
to procure high-end weapons which could be from Indian Ordinance Factories or from other
resources. The Committee desires that the Home Ministry should place its requirements of
modern weaponry before Defence Ministry which could help improve products of Indian
ordinance factories. The Committee understands that in addition to modern training
facilities to State police forces, they should also be provided with modern arms,
ammunitions, equipment, surveillance devices, better communications facilities, etc.
HOUSING SCHEME
4.4 The provision of housing facilities to CPF personnel has an important bearing on the morale
of the Force. The demand for family accommodation for the force personnel has increased
considerably in recent years, particularly, keeping in view the fact that the Forces are working for
long periods in far flung areas.
4.4.1 The budget has allocated Rs.1,185 crore for construction of 4,000 residential quarters to
accommodate personnel of various forces like CRPF, BSF, ITBP, CISF and SSB personnel. The
Ministry was asked about progress in terms of acquisition of land for proposed construction of
residential quarters. The Ministry has informed that the Government proposes to construct, 6665
numbers of houses during 2012-13, to accommodate personnel of Central Armed Police Forces, for
which provision of Rs.1185.00 crore has been made in the Budget Estimate for 2012-13. Earlier

4.4.4 One Member of the Committee in the meeting held on 28 th March, 2012 raised the issue
of severe shortage of houses for the Central Armed Forces. The Home Secretary clarified the
housing status as under:
“...There was one question about the housing shortage. That is a valid point. It is not
just 4,000 houses only. The sanction is for 6,665 houses during 2012-13. That is not the
only sanction, Sir. Apart from that, we are having a project for construction of houses
under the PPP scheme. About the first phase of the scheme, I had informed the
Committee last time, the tender process is over. After that, before finalization, we wanted
to have the stage audit done. There were some queries and we have answered the part.
We are now ready to go to the Cabinet. The number of houses involved in that is 11,460
in the first lot itself during T welfth Five Year Plan. That will be hopefully coming
through. Apart from that, we are constructing 149 barracks during 2012-13 and its
capacity would be for 20,259 personnel. If second lot of the PPP gets finalised, that will
have another 18,344 Housing units.”
4.4.5 The Ministry further clarifying the status of Housing scheme under Public Private
Partnership model submitted that 1
st and 2 nd lots of the housing project have been approved by
Public Private Partnership Appraisal Committee (PPPAC). For the 1 st lot, after going through
RFQ to RFP, bidders have been shortlisted and for the 2 nd lot, applications have been received
at RFQ stage and evaluated. According to the Ministry, the RFP documents for lot 2 have been
approved by PPPAC in its 46
th Meeting subject to condition that the RFP can be issued only
after Planning Commission commits funds for the 2 nd Lot and a request in this regard has been
sent at the level of Home Minister to Deputy Chairman Planning Commission on 5.10.2011
followed by a reminder on 05.03.2012 by Home Secretary. Response of Planning Commission
was awaited.
4.4.6 The Ministry has stated that the Stage Audit of the project has been carried out by C&AG
and the audit observations have been replied by MHA. Cabinet Note on the project is under
preparation.
4.4.7 According to the Ministry, the decision whether remaining 2 lots will also be taken-up under
Public Private Partnership (PPP) mode or Engineering, Procurement and Construction (EPC) mode
will be taken only after the approval/directions of Cabinet.
4.4.8 The Ministry was also asked whether allocation of Rs.62.00 crore made under Major Head
4055 of Delhi Police for construction of houses, in BE 2012-13 would suffice. The Ministry felt
that the amount was sufficient for construction of Housing Schemes/Projects, projected in Annual
Action Plan 2012-13 under Plan Head (Residential Buildings). The Ministry has informed that Delhi
Police has also initiated the process to construct 5202 staff quarters at Dheerpur and new Police
Headquarters Building at Parliament Street through PPP mode and a token provision of Rs. 1 crore
each has been made for both the projects.

4.4.11 According to the Ministry, the physical targets for 2012-13 will be achieved with the fund
made available for 2012-13. Majority of the works are executed through CPWD. To address the
above issue MHA has initiated a number of steps which are as under:–
• Regular review meetings are being held in MHA at senior officers level to review the
progress of the works assigned to CPWD, where the representatives of the CPWD
from all the Zonal Offices as well as from Hqr. remain present.
• It has been decided that all the CAPFs will hold meeting with CPWD and discuss the
delayed proposals at regular intervals, at least once in a month.
• CAPFs have been directed that instructions may be issued to the field officers to
regularly review the progress of works and discuss the outstanding issues with their
counterparts in CPWD. In case there are difficulties in sorting out obstacles at field
level, the same may be brought to the notice of Force Headquarters.
• CPWD has been requested to update the status on their web-site with the current
progress so that the status of works could be obtained from the web-site.
• The matter has been reviewed at the level of Home Secretary, who has written to
Secretary, Urban Development, expressing MHA’s concern over the delay caused by
CPWD and requested for remedial action.
• Further to address the issue of low housing satisfaction in Central Armed Police
Forces, a Mega Housing Project has been launched for construction of 56298 houses
and 338 Nos. barracks at 224 locations, which is being taken in 38 clusters/4 lots. The
project will be implemented after obtaining the requisite approval of Cabinet.
4.4.12 The Committee takes note that the budget allocation of Rs.1,185 crore for
construction of 6665 residential quarters to accommodate personnel of various forces like
CRPF, BSF, ITBP, CISF and SSB personnel. The Committee also notes that the land has
already been acquired for construction of residential quarters planned for the year 2012-13.
The Committee notes that the tender process is over for construction of 11,460 houses
under PPP scheme. The Committee hopes that the Government would achieve its target in
the current financial year.
4.4.13 The Committee takes note of the fact that in the last three years against the
physical targets of 3570, only 2291 houses for CRPF personnel have been constructed. The
Committee expresses its displeasure over undue delay in the construction of houses for
CRPF personnel. The Committee therefore, strongly recommends that rest of the houses
should be constructed at the earliest and there should not be any time/cost overruns.
4.4.14 The Committee notes the progress made in the matter of construction of
accommodation under PPP model and recommends that all procedural formalities concerning
construction of quarters in the 1
st lot be completed at the earliest so that work on this could
take off. Regarding construction of quarters in 2 nd lot, planning Commission may be
persuaded to commit funds for the purpose at the earliest.

82
4.4.15 The Committee feels that construction of 300 quarters in Dwarka is not sufficient in
view of the scarcity of accommodation for police personnel. The Committee in its earlier
reports had expressed its anguish on the approach adopted by land owning agencies. Several
proposals for allotment of land are pending with them. The Committee therefore,
recommends that the issue of the allotment of land for housing projects of Delhi Police may
be taken up at the Minister level. The Committee also desires that besides GDA, Noida
Authority, possibilities of land allotment may be explored in adjoining areas like
Bahadurgarh, Faridabad, Gurgaon, Sonepat in Haryana and State Government of Haryana
may be approached in this regard.
CRIMINAL INVESTIGATION AND VIGILANCE
4.5 The budgetary allocation of Rs.109.74 crore in BE 2011-12 had been reduced to Rs.75.47
crore in RE stage in 2011-12. For the year 2012-13, a provision of Rs. 78.61 crores has been
made for the purpose. The Ministry has given following reasons behind it:-
• Delay in acquisition of land at Pune, Bhopal and Guwahati for establishment of new
CFSLs and expansion at Chandigarh. The land now has been acquired from respective
State/UT authorities after protracted correspondence. Possession of land at all the
places has been taken.
• There has been delay in construction of CFSL, Kolkata Lab at Rajarhat, Kolkata on the
land acquired from the State Government due to agitation by local people.
• Out of 27 Plan Projects, approved for Eleventh Five Year Plan, 10 were dropped on
account of non viability.
• The delay in recruitment of contractual manpower for the ongoing projects.
• There was a fire in the office building of Directorate of Forensic Science Services
(DFSS) HQs on 23
rd April, 2011 causing extensive damage to records which resulted
in delay in implementation of plan schemes.
4.5.1 According to the Ministry allocation of Rs. 78.61 crores is proposed to be utilized for the
following purposes:–
• Creation of facilities of DNA Data base for identification of unidentified mutilated dead
bodies and missing persons on PP Model.
• Release of grant in aid to States for setting up of 6 Regional Forensic Science
Laboratories (RFSLs) and 52 District Mobile Forensic Units (DMFUs).
• Construction of buildings of CFSLs at Chandigarh, Bhopal, Pune, Kolkata and
Guwahati as well as procurement of machinery and equipment and hiring of
manpower.
• Setting up of Forensic Network among CFSLs and FSLs (initially among CFSLs).
• Implementation of Quality Management and Quality Assurance Programme.
4.5.2 The Ministry has apprised that the proposed allocation for Twelfth Five Year Plan for
CFSLs is Rs.700 crore. Out of which Rs.160 crore has been earmarked for modernization
of existing CFSLs, Rs.220 crore for new CFSLs, Rs.210 crore for building infrastructure and
Rs.10 crore for other schemes and Rs.100 crore is allocated for the States.
4.5.3 The Ministry has given details of steps taken to improve the working of Central Forensic
Laboratories:–

83
• Upgradation of existing divisions and creation of new Forensic Divisions in the existing
Divisions of CFSLs at Chandigarh, Hyderabad and Kolkata, namely, Forensic
Electronics and Engineering (Digital Forensic), Forensic Psychology, Forensic
Intelligence (Data base management) etc.
• Creation of National Training Resource Centre for Cyber Forensics.
• Expansion of Lab accommodation at Kolkata and Chandigarh.
• The action is on to draft a Forensic Regulatory And Development Authority Bill with
a view to to establish a Forensic Sciences Development and Regulatory Authority
(FRDA) and to provide for regulation standardization and accreditation of forensic
science services and certification of forensic science practitioners and for matters
connected therewith or incidental thereto.
• Creation of Networking Facility among the Central, State and Regional Forensic
Science Laboratories.
• Laboratory Accreditation and Quality Assurance Scheme for State and Central Forensic
Science Laboratories.
• Creation of 6 Regional Forensic Science Laboratories and 52 District Mobile Forensic
Units in States.
• New Scheme of Forensic Improvement Grant to implement FRDA.
4.5.4 The Ministry has also informed that in addition, Scientific Performance Audit Committee
(SPAC) has conducted performance audit of all CFSLs and recommended a number of steps for
improvement, upgradation and modernization of CFSLs. According to the Ministry, the report
covers upgradation of capacity and capability in existing divisions of CFSLs, setting up of new
forensic divisions, training of Indian scientists in India and abroad, development of high tech rapid
response forensic team for evidence management, networking, development of SOPs, validation for
uniform reporting, establishment of new high tech CFSL in NCR, creation of more scientific and
administrative manpower for CFSLs and DFSS HQ. The Ministry has apprised that the report is
under consideration and will be considered for implementation in next Five Year Plan.
4.5.5 The Hon’ble Supreme Court of India vide its judgement dated 22.09.2006 in Writ Petition
(Civil) No.310 of 1996 – Prakash Singh and others vs UOI had issued several directions to the
States, UTs and the Central Government on Police Reforms. One of these directions pertains to
separation of investigating police from law and order police. The Ministry was asked whether it has
plan to separate the investigating and law and order function. The Ministry has replied that a copy
of Supreme Court judgment was sent to all State Governments for consideration and appropriate
action. According to the Ministry, vide its subsequent order dated 16.5.2008, the Hon’ble Supreme
Court constituted a Committee under the chairmanship of Shri Justice K.T. Thomas, retired Judge
of Supreme Court, inter alia , to examine the affidavits filed by different States in compliance to
the Court’s directions. The Ministry has further informed that the Terms of Reference for the
Committee, inter-alia, included to examine the affidavits filed by the different States and the Union
Territories in compliance to the Court’s directions with reference to the ground realities; advise the
Respondents wherever the implementation is falling short of the Court’s orders, after considering
the Respondents’ stated difficulties in implementation; bring to the notice of the Court any genuine
problems the Respondents may be having in view of the specific conditions prevailing in a State
or Union Territory etc. The Ministry has further informed that the Committee has submitted its
report to the Hon’ble Supreme Court and a copy of the report has been circulated to all States/
UTs by the Registry of Supreme Court on 4.10.2010. The Hon’ble Court is now examining the
status of implementation of its directions. As per this report a number of States have taken steps

84
to implement this direction of the Supreme Court either through executive orders or through their
respective Police Acts.
4.5.6 According to the Ministry, various Committees/Commissions on Police Reforms, i.e., the
National Police Commission (1977), the Padmanabhaiah Committee on Police Reforms (2000) and
Malimath Committee on Criminal Justice System (2002) in their respective reports recommended
for separation of investigation from law and order. The Ministry has apprised that the
recommendations of the Commission/Committees were sent to State Governments and Union
Territory Administrations from time to time for consideration and appropriate action. The Ministry
has further apprised that as a follow-up of the Prime Minister’s announcement at the Annual
Conference of DsGP in November, 2004, the Ministry of Home Affair constituted a Review
Committee vide  order dated 21.12.2004 to short list those recommendations of all the
Commissions/Committees on Police Reform which had not been implemented or implemented only
partially and to recommend the course of action needed. The Minstry has submitted that this
Committee short-listed 49 recommendations as being crucial to the process of transforming the
police into a professionally competent and service oriented organization and one of these 49
recommendations pertains to separation of investigation from law and order. The Ministry has
submitted that a copy of the recommendations was sent to all State Governments to initiate action
on these recommendations on 5
th May, 2005. According to the Ministry, as per Seventh Schedule
of the Constitution of India ‘Police’ and ‘Law and Order’ are the State subjects and it is the State
Governments which have to implement the police reforms measures. The Ministry feels that the
Centre can at best persuade the States from time to time to bring the requisite reforms in the Police
administration to meet the expectations of the people.
4.5.7 The Committee notes with deep anguish that out of 27 projects of CFSLs which were
approved for Eleventh Five year plan, 10 projects had to be dropped on account of non-
viability. The Committee, denounces this casual approach on the part of the Government
that these projects were found to be deserved for approval upto the level of a Five Year Plan
of the country and suddenly at the end of the Five Year Plan they were found to be
unviable. The Committee feels that the Government should have assessed the viability of
the Plans before taking up. This lackadaisical attitude is not acceptable to the Committee.
4.5.8 The Committee is disappointed to note the plain justification of the Ministry that the
budgetary allocations in 2011-12 at RE stage was reduced due to delay in acquisition of land
at Pune, Bhopal and Guwahati for establishment of new CFSLs and expansion at Chandigarh
CFSLs, delay in recruitment of contractual manpower for ongoing projects. The Committee
understands that land acquisition is the base stage for any project envisaged and the
Government should keep this in mind while planning any project. The Committee,
recommends that proper planning of the projects on CFSLs should be made and concerted
efforts be made to complete projects within a time frame.
4.5.9 The Committee notes that the allocation of Rs. 78.61 crore in BE 2012-13 should be
properly utilized in a time bound manner for creation of facilities of DNA data base,
Regional Forensic Science Laboratories (RFSLs), District Mobile Forensic Units, Network
among CFSLs and FSLs etc. The Committee understands that upgradation of existing
CFSLs, FSLs and creations of new ones would go a long way in revamping our Criminal
Justice System. The Committee further recommends that efforts be made to set up Forensic
Science Development regulatory Authority (FRDA) at the earliest. Consideration of the
report of the Scientific Performance Audit Committee be completed at the earliest for
implementation in the Twelfth Five Year Plan.
4.5.10 The Committee is pleased to note that a number of States have taken steps to
implement the directions of the Hon’ble Supreme Court pertaining to separating Law and

85
Order Function from Investigation by Police. Committee is of the view that maintaining law
and order and investigating cases/complaints are entirely different things. Discussion may
be made with the State Governments on regular basis in this direction for an early
implementation.
EDUCATION, TRAINING AND RESEARCH
4.6 The Ministry was asked to explain variations under Major Heads (2055 & 4055) for
education, training & research which was Rs.2055.00 crore in BE 2011-12 reduced to Rs.945.00
crore in RE 2011-12 and increased to Rs.1600.00 crore in BE 2012-13. The Ministry has submitted
as under:–
“The allocations under ‘Research’ in BE 2011-2012 were Rs.2044 crore. Because of slow
pace of expenditure during 2011-2012 allocations were reduced to Rs.945 crore. As some
projects are expected to be implemented during 2012-2013, the provisions have been
proposed at Rs.1600 crore.”
4.6.1 The Ministry has furnished details of funds earmarked for Central Police Training College
and six zonal branches of Central Detective Training Schools which is as under:–
(i) Funds earmarked for CAPT during the year 2012-13 – Rs. 47.00 crore
(under Major Head 2011 and 4055 – Rs.7 crore &
Rs.40 crore respectively)
(ii) Funds earmarked for six zonal branches of CDTSs* – Rs. 22.00 crore
(under Major Head 2055 and 405 – Rs. 2 crore &
Rs.20 crore respectively)

Government but only two new CDTSs (at Ghaziabad and Jaipur) have been sanctioned under
Eleventh Plan Period to meet the training requirements of Central States and Western States.  The
Ministry has furnished details of the progress of setting up of these two CDTSs which is given
below:–
(i) CTDS Ghaziabad
53 posts have been sanctioned by MHA. Possession of 8.37 acre land at Ghaziabad has
been taken from Ministry of Urban Development on March 9, 2012. Meanwhile, two
training courses have commenced at the premises of adjoining CBI Academy.
(ii) CDTS, Jaipur

(ii) CDTS, Ghaziabad  : It is expected that CDTS, Ghaziabad will become fully functional

86
4.6.4 The Ministry was asked about the measures taken to sensitize police personnel especially
of lower ranks to the problems faced by the Scheduled Castes, Scheduled Tribes, Minorities and
women so as to make policemen people-friendly. The Ministry in its written reply has furnished
details of steps taken which are as under:–
• Ministry of Home Affairs have issued an “Advisory on Crime against Scheduled Castes/
Scheduled Tribes – Measures needed to curb” to all State Governments and UT
Administrations, which has been circulated by Delhi Police also for strict compliance.
• besides, SC/ST (Prevention of Atrocities) Act, 1989 and Protection of Civil Rights
(PCR) Act, 1955 have been included in the syllabi of Basic training/promotional
courses training at PTC, Jharoda Kalan, New Delhi to sensitize the police personnel of
the rank of ASI, SI and Inspector.
• PTC has also included the topics on SC/ST Act in the syllabus of basic courses of
PSIs and DANIPS (Prob).
• The boards containing the instructions to help SC/ST victims have been displayed in
police stations.
• A Special Unit for Women & Children consisting of women police personnel has been
functioning under the charge of a Joint CP. Women police personnel have been posted
as Duty Officer in most of the police stations to facilitate lodging of the complaints
by women, children, weaker sections etc.
• ‘Police’ and ‘Public Order’ are State subjects under the 7
th Schedule (list-II) to the
Constitution of India and, therefore, the State Governments and Union Territory
Administrations are primarily responsible for prevention, detection, registration,
investigation and prosecution of all crimes within their jurisdiction including crimes
against Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes.
4.6.5 The Home Ministry has also furnished a list of advisories issued to prevent crime against
SCs/STs, women and children and to ensure that the Police are sensitive to their special needs. The
list is given below:-
• Advisory on Crime against Scheduled Castes/Scheduled Tribes – Measures needed to
curb – regarding dated 01.04.2010.
• Advisory on Crime against Women – Measures needed to crib – regarding dated
04.09.2009.
• Advisory on Preventing and Combating Human Trafficking in India dated 09.09.2009.
• Advisory on Crime against Children dated 14.07.2010.
• Advisory on Preventing & Combating Cybre Crime against Children dated 04.01.2012.
• Advisory on missing children-measures needed to prevent trafficking and trace the
children-regarding dated 31.01.2012.
4.6.6 The Ministry has furnished details of initiatives taken by the Government to sensitize IPS
probationers to the problems of SC, ST, Minorities and Women at Hyderabad based Sardar
Vallabhbhai Patel National Police Academy. According to the Ministry, the following topics are being
covered during their basic course training:
(a) Investigation of SC, ST cases with particular reference to legal provisions of the
relevant Acts.

87
(b) Crime against women and investigation and important related legal provision.
(c) Communal Harmony and National Integration with issues pertaining to Minorities.
(d) Gender sensitization and sexual harassment at work place.
4.6.7 The Ministry has apprised that the above mentioned topics are covered by the faculty
members of the SVP NPA and the Guest speakers who are invited for delivering the same. The
Ministry has also submitted that seminar on ‘Communal Harmony and National Integration’ was
organized with the issues pertaining to Minorities. Panel discussion on ‘Crime against women and
children’ was also organized.
4.6.8 According to the Ministry, the following topics have been covered to sensitize the IPS
Probationers during the basic course training
(i) Problems of SC, ST, Minorities and Women.
(ii) Communal Riots, Hindu Muslim problems, Communalism & Radicalisation etc.
4.6.9 The Ministry has informed that Seminar on Communal Harmony and National Investigation
has been organized to sensitize them, among other things to the issues pertaining to Minorities.
4.6.10 There were reports of appointing IPS officers directly from Deputy Superintendant of Police
level officers working in State police forces and Central police Organizations as well as from
defence forces. The Ministry was asked whether army has expressed its reservations in the matter
of relieving its officers and whether a case has been filed in a court of law against the proposal.
The Ministry has informed that a Scheme of Limited Competitive Examination (LCE) has been
finalized and UPSC has invited applications for examination to be conducted on 20-22 May, 2012.
It is expected that the first batch of 80 officers would be inducted by August, 2012. The Ministry
has further informed that no communication expressing reservations in the matter of relieving its
army officers has been received in this Ministry from the Ministry of Defence. The Ministry has
apprised that two Writ Petition Nos. 1600/2012 and 1610/2012 have been filed in the Hon’ble High
Court of Delhi at New Delhi.
4.6.11 During the oral evidence of the Home Secretary before the Committee in its sitting held on
4
th April, 2012, one Member of the Committee asked the Home Secretary about details of vacancies
in IPS and action proposed to be taken to meet the shortfall. The Home Secretary in his response
made following remarks.
“..... so far as shortage of IPS officers is concerned, yes, there is shortage of IPS officers.
The last figure, as told by my colleague, is 1,300 officers. One of the reasons for the
shortage is that in-between, in some years, we whittled down the recruitment to about 40
a year, 30 a year. For a huge country like ours, recruiting 30 IPS officers a year was not
advisable. Sir, that is one of the reasons. The number of districts has increased; that is
another reason. Challenges have increased. The requirements of IPS officers have increased.
The cadre revision has happened, and I am told that the shortage is of 1,300 officers.”
