4.6.14  According to the Ministry, to meet the shortfall, following action has been taken
/head2rightIncrease of direct recruitment seats – The number of intake for IPS has been
increased to 150 every year. It is pertinent to mention that in 2002, the total intake
was 56.
/head2rightFor filling the vacancies in the promotion quota, State Governments and UPSC are
being constantly requested to expedite the process of promotion to SPS officers.
/head2rightA third mode of recruitment to IPS has been formalized, namely the Limited
competitive examination, which will induct 80 officers annually.
4.6.15 The Ministry felt that the annual appointment of 230 officers (150+80) annually will wipe
out the DR quota vacancies (existing + expected future vacancies) in the next 8-10 years and this
is after taking into account the past experience that some of the selected officers may not join the
service.
4.6.16 During the meeting of the Committee held on 4
th April, 2012, a Member of the Committee
observed that a lot of people, who join the Police forces, never really wanted to join the Police
forces. He added that they wanted to become IAS Officers and because they did not get IAS, they
opted for the second best service. He suggested that a separate selection process for IPS should
be considered. The Home Secretary in response made following observations:
“...Having a system like the Army for recruiting IPS officers, yes, Sir, we can consider and
we can examine it. But, Sir, there is one thing which the Army does, which I do not know
whether we should do in other areas. When a person joins the Army, he is not generally
allowed or given permission to appear for better opportunities outside. Even for this limited
competitive examination which we have, there is a standing circular which says that only
those officers who are in the low medical category or who have been passed over for
promotion will be allowed to apply. I do not know whether that is a good thing, prohibiting
people from applying for better opportunities. I do not know whether that is a good thing.
..........  We can have that. We can try and recruit only people who have an orientation and
commitment for that. An officer may have an orientation for policing, but, at the same
time, he may want to appear for examination for getting some service which, in his eyes,
is better.”
4.6.17 On the issue, the Home Ministry has also furnished its written reply. The ministry has
submitted that the suggestion of separate selection process for IPS will be examined.
4.6.18 The Committee takes note of the fact that six zonal branches of CDTSs have not
been sanctioned by the Government, but only two new CDTSs (at Ghaziabad and Jaipur)
have been sanctioned under Eleventh Plan period to meet the training requirements of
Central States and Western States. The Committee does not understand as to why six
CDTSs were sanctioned by the Government initially but only two were sanctioned in the
Eleventh Plan period. Even they are also not fully functional. This shows the causal
approach of the Ministry. The Committee condemns this attitude and recommends that only
viable projects should be proposed. Efforts may be made to make the CDTSs already
started, fully functional at the earliest.

89
4.6.19 The Committee understands that the Government should also not only pay attention
on the arms/people training of Police Personnel but also train them for developing good
interaction and communication skills with the public; discipline, attitude, behavior,
responsibility and accountability quotient and motivation & morale levels. The Committee
recommends that the Government should further involve BPR&D which is the premier
police research organization in evolving a curriculum for sensitizing of Police Personnel.
Regular refresher courses may be held in this regard.
4.6.20 The Committee takes serious note of the fact that there is a shortage of 1300 IPS
officers in the country. Though a scheme of limited competitive examination is due to take
place by UPSC to appoint IPS officers directly from State Police Officers and Para-military
Police Officers to overcome this shortage, but this is not ultimate solution. Recruitment
may be done as per requirement in the Civil services examinations.
4.6.21 The Committee is of the view that the examination system for recruiting IPS officers
who head the Police Organisations of the States/UTs and Central Police Forces is not proper.
The Committee is aware of the fact that the UPSC conducts examination for various group
‘A’ services and Indian Police Service is one of them. The people aspiring for IPS, do not
know whether they will get into this or not. In the same way, the people not interested in
IPS, do not know whether they will get IPS or other services. The Committee understands
that the candidates opting for police service have to have/should have a different attitude
which this examination system is not able to guarantee. It has been seen that people who
do not like policing get into IPS. The Committee recommends the Government to explore
the possibility of conducting a separate examination of IPS where the attitude and aptitude
of the candidate must be thoroughly tested besides their mental makeup and inclination for
policing. The Government may impress upon the UPSC to conduct a separate examination
for recruiting IPS officers and SVPNPA may also be consulted in this regard.

90
90
CHPATER-V
OTHER SIGNIFICANT ISSUES
DISASTER MANAGEMENT
5.1 In our country, 58.6% area is earthquake prone, 12% area is floods prone and 68%
cultivable area is drought prone. The Ministry has stated that when a comparison is made for
similar disaster happened in USA and Japan with that of India, it is found that loss of lives in USA
and Japan are much less as compared to that of India. The factor influencing losses is attributed
to the better preparedness in USA and Japan. The Disaster Management Act was enacted in 2005 .
The Ministry has further stated that prevention and mitigation contribute to lasting improvement in
safety and, should therefore be integrated in the disaster management.
5.1.1 On the issue of preparedness of the country in terms of Disaster, the Home Secretary
submitted before the Committee in its sitting held on 4
th April, 2012 as under:–
“...I think, we are still some distance away from full preparedness. We have now started the
National Cyclone Mitigation Project, and approximately an amount of Rs. 70 crores has
been spent on it, but, it is still at the initial stage. Once that project is on the ground, then
we will be better prepared for handling cyclones. The Earthquake Risk Mitigation Project
has still not got off the ground. We need to get it off the ground, and that is something
which we need to do. However, we are better prepared than we were earlier because now
we have the National Disaster Response Force. So, our response capability is there, and that
Force is quite effective. But we have still some distance to go in this. We will send you
a detailed  reply.”
5.1.2  The Disaster Management Act was enacted in 2005. This act provides for the effective
management of disasters and for matters connected therewith or incidental thereto.  The Ministry
in its subsequent written reply has furnished a detailed note on the preparedness of the country
to meet disasters, since the enactment of the Act on the subject.
• Institutional Mechanism – Disaster Management Authorities at National, State and
District level, National Disaster Response Force, National Institute of Disaster
Management etc. have been put in place. The State Governments have the basic
responsibility for undertaking rescue, relief and rehabilitation measures in disaster, by
providing logistic and financial support in case of severe natural calamities. The Central
Government supplements the efforts of the States as and when needed. Other
institutional mechanisms like Civil Defence, Fire Services, Home Guards, National Fire
Service College and National Civil Defence College are also in existence to support the
disaster management activity in the country.
• Financial Arrangements – As provided under the DM Act, National Disaster Response
Fund and State Disaster Response Fund have been established.
• Capacity Development – National Institute of Disaster Management has been set up at
national level. It conducts training, research and development for implementation of
comprehensive human resource development. At State level, Administrative Training
Institutes run their disaster management centers. These are Institutes assisted with

91
financial and technical help from NIDM. The States are provided Capacity
Development grant to strengthen and upgrade the capacity of machinery responsible
for Disaster Management.
• National Disaster Response Force (NDRF) – NDRF has been constituted as specialist
response force for a threatening disaster situation or disaster. It has 10 battalions
across the country, located on the basis of vulnerability to natural disasters. The
States/UTs have also been advised to set up their own Specialist Response Force for
responding to disasters on the lines of NDRF. Many States have started the process
to set up such force and train them for response purposes.
• National Policy – National Policy on DM has been issued. It aims to build a safe and
disaster resilient India by developing a holistic, proactive and multi disaster oriented
technology driven strategy through culture of prevention, mitigation, preparedness and
response.
• Guidelines/Reports – NDMA have released about 15 disaster specific and thematic
guidelines and 10 reports, which have been circulated to all the stakeholders.
• Preparedness and Response: National Emergency Operation Centre (NEOC) in Ministry
of Home Affairs functions 24x7 to monitor disasters or disaster like situation. For
forecast purposes, agencies like the Indian Meteorological Department for Cyclone,
Central Water Commission for flood, Defence Research and Development Organisation
(DRDO) for Avalanche, Indian National Centre for Ocean Information Services
(INCOIS) for Tsunamis etc. have been strengthened and upgraded.
• Advisories are issued to the States/UTs concerned, based on the forecast for keeping
watch on the developing situation and take necessary measures such as evacuation of
vulnerable persons, operation of relief camps, prepositioning of essential commodities
etc. State and district level emergency operation centres are also functional during
disaster situations.
• Prevention and Mitigation – Government of India is implementing several plans for
mitigating disasters such as Flood Management Program by Ministry of Water
Resources, Drought Prone Area Program by Ministry of Agriculture, Integrated Coastal
Zone Management Project by Ministry of Environment and Forests etc. NDMA is
working on the National Flood Risk Mitigation Project, National Landslide Risk
Mitigation Project, National Earthquake Risk Mitigation Project etc. The National
Cyclone Risk Mitigation Project is under implementation with an initial outlay of
Rs. 1496.71 crore.
• Government of India is also implementing externally aided schemes in collaboration
with UNDP and USAID for disaster risk reduction. These aim at reducing vulnerability
to disaster and build capacity of key Indian institutions and to strengthen the institutional
structure to undertake disaster risk reduction activities at various levels including risk
being enhanced due to climate change and develop preparedness for recovery.
• International Cooperation – India is closely working with various bodies of United
Nations (UN) on DRR, and other international and regional organisations such as
ASEAN Regional Forum, Asian Disaster Reduction Centre, GFDRR etc. International
Cooperation are aimed at sharing the best practices, expertise and also for technical
assistance on DRR.
• Way Forward – In the area of prevention, mitigation, response and capacity
development several initiatives, as mentioned above have been taken. However, the

92
man-made disasters are yet to be given similar due attention. Various efforts given
below have been contemplated to strengthen the process of Disaster Risk Reduction
(DRR).
a. Developing a centralized database on different kind of disasters.
b. Strengthening the early warning system and developing communication and
connectivity to the last mile.
c. Modernising the emergency operation centres and to make them work on real time
basis.
d. Mainstreaming DRR into the development and planning process.
e. Strengthening the regulatory mechanism in the Urban Planning.
f. Mainstreaming Corporate Social Responsibility in DRR.
g. Integrating the Climate Change with the process of Disaster Risk Reduction.
5.1.3 The Ministry was asked about details of steps such as making it compulsory for every
institution or building to have an evacuation plan, conducting mock drills at least once a month.
The Ministry has replied that National Disaster Management Authority (NDMA) is running mock
exercises in the States to prepare them in generating efficient response in case of disasters,
pertaining to earthquakes, floods, cyclones, chemical (industrial) disasters, terrorist attacks
including scenarios of gas leakage and firing and urban fire in high rise buildings/malls, with large
scale participation by state leadership, administration and community. The Ministry has further
replied that NDMA helps in training and creation of disaster management facilities in industries and
emphasizes on off-site evacuation plans jointly by the industry and district administration and as
on date 327 exercises have been conducted by NDMA in various parts of country.
5.1.4 According to the Ministry , NIDM has been providing technical assistance to State
Governments/District Administrations and other stakeholders for preparation of Disaster
Management Plans which inter-alia  include steps involved in mitigation, preparedness and response
and relief and recently, a Mock Drill was conducted by Delhi Disaster Management Authority
(DDMA) in collaboration with National Disaster Management Authority (NDMA) on 15
th of
February, 2012. The Ministry has informed that preceding this event a major earthquake
preparedness exercise in Delhi was undertaken and NDMA has been advised to prepare a template
for conducting such Mock Drills across the country.
5.1.5 The Ministry was asked to furnish a concept note on ‘’National Platform on Disaster Risk
Reduction (NPDRR)” along with its objective, mandate and activities undertaken so far. The
Ministry has furnished following details:–
• The National Institute of Disaster Management (NIDM) had been organizing the Indian
Disaster Management Congress (IDMC) since 2006 for collective efforts on knowledge
and research in the areas of disaster management.
• The IDMC was conceived as a platform where scientists, researchers and practitioners
across disciplines and sectors would meet once in two to three years to present the
findings of their research and action, mutual cooperation and road map for the future.
It was, however, observed that the National congress was limited to technical aspects
of disaster management.
• Following the participation of India in the meeting of Global Platform in Geneva in
May 2011, it was envisaged that the Indian Disaster Management Congress (IDMC)

93
and the National Platform be merged together and a single event be organized which
would include academics as well as policy makers and professionals to deliberate on
the subject, exchange the views and to formulate an implementable action plan and a
road map for taking disaster risk reduction (DRR) in the country in a coordinated
manner. The output of the National Platform is expected to help India for its active
participation and engagement with other countries and international bodies.
5.1.6 According to the Ministry, the Government has approved modalities and action plan for
organizing the First National Platform with an objective which is as under:–
Modalities
• The IDMC shall be merged with the proposed National Platform for DRR-a Biennial
event (to be jointly organized by MHA, NDMA and NIDM).
• The First National Platform was to be held in last week of April 2012 and thereafter
biennially which shall be in conformity with the Global Platform. The event in view
of the Parliament being in session is got to be re-fixed on a later date.
• The proposed National Platform is expected to bring together organizations and
individuals in diverse discipline and sectors and assemble, synthesize, manage and
further disseminate academic as well as practical knowledge on disaster management
in all sectors including policy making and development plans of the Government.
Objectives
• To anchor the knowledge and research in the domain of specific disciplines to provide
greater interaction and interface among the disciplines to validate and complement.
• To enlarge the perspectives of research in particular disciplines and provide a holistic
framework.
• To create a pool of resources of scientists, practitioners on DRR.
• To bring together organizations and individuals of diverse disciplines and sectors and
in assembling, synthesizing, managing and further disseminating knowledge, theoretical
as well as practical, on disaster management in diverse sectors.
• To provide a platform where researchers and practitioners shall meet periodically, present
their research papers, debate on the issues, challenges encountered, shortcomings and
opportunities and discuss the areas of mutual cooperation and road maps.
• To encourage national and local implementation, adaptation and creation of ownership
on Hyogo Framework for Action (HFA)
5.1.7 There is a sharp increase in allocation earmarked for Disaster Management which has been
pegged at Rs. 632.48 crore (Rs.150.00 plan and Rs. 482.48 crore non-plan in BE 2012-13 as
compared with RE of 2011-12 at Rs.543.07 crore. The Ministry has stated that the reasons for
sharp increase in allocation can be attributed to the following main reasons:–
• 10 Bns. of National Disaster Response Force have been constituted for the purpose of
specialist response to a threatening disaster situation or disaster, as per provisions of
the Disaster Management Act, 2005 and are located at 10 strategic places across India.
For their office and residential infrastructures, considerable sum of money is required
which will include the cost of land also. Only Rs. 82.70 crore has been provided for
this purpose in BE 2012-13, which is an enhancement of Rs. 52.95 crore over
Rs. 29.75 crore RE of 2011-12.

94
• Building up Specialized Capabilities for Rapid Intervention in case of disaster under The
National Emergency Communication Plan (NECP) Phase-II is under implementation
since last year. Hence for procurement of capital items like motor vehicles, machine
& equipments, more money is required for which a provision of Rs. 26 crore in B.E.
2012-13 has been kept, which is an increase of Rs. 11.95 crore with respect to R.E.
(2011-12) of Rs. 14.05 crore.
• National Institute of Disaster Management (NIDM), established as per provisions of the
Disaster Management Act, 2005, is mandated for planning and promoting training and
research in the area of disaster management, documentation and development of
national level information base relating to disaster management policies, prevention
mechanisms and mitigation measures. Till date, due to non-availability of land, NIDM
could not develop its own building complex and is functioning from the rented
premises of Indian Institute of Public Administration (IIPA). Now, land has been
identified for NIDM by Delhi Development Authority (DDA) and this year we are likely
to pay about Rs. 5.2 crore for the said land. Therefore for development of office and
residential buildings more funds shall be required in next financial year. Hence, Rs. 10
crore has been kept in B.E. 2012-13 which is just Rs. 3 crore in excess to the funds
of Rs. 7 crore provided in R.E. of 2011-12.
• National Disaster Management Programme: B.E.2012-13 has been pegged at Rs.0.66
crore which is an increase of Rs0.30 crore due to provision for Foreign Travel
Expenses which is required to enhance professional capabilities of officials involved
with Disaster Management Activities.
• National Disaster Management Authority (NDMA): Establishment charges: There is
enhancement of Rs 5.34 crore in B.E. 2012-13 over R.E. of Rs. 27.97 crore of 2011-12
which is due to increase in Domestic Travel Expenses, Office expenses, Foreign
Travel Expenses, Publications, Advertisement and Publicity, Professional Services etc.
Enhancements are essential due to increased activity of NDMA.
• National Disaster Response Force (NDRF): In B.E. 2012-13, there is provision of
Rs.276.56 crore under Revenue for NDRF which is an increase of Rs. 29.86 crore
over R.E. of 2011-12. The Budget enhancement is mostly for meeting up of salaries
of NDRF Personnel, Domestic Travel Expenses, Office expenses, Clothing and
Tentage, Professional Services etc.
5.1.8 National Disaster Management Authority, established as per the provisions of the Disaster
Management Act, 2005, with Prime Minister of India as its Chairperson, ex officio , has been
mandated for laying down policies, plans and guidelines for disaster management for ensuring
timely and effective response to disaster. The Ministry has also given details of steps taken or
proposed to be taken for making NDMA functionally more efficient.
• NDMA, being a nascent organisation, started its functioning from rented rooms in
Centaur Hotel and thereafter shifted to NDMA Bhawan during September, 2008.
Though 124 posts were created for this Authority from the level of Secretary
downwards, the posts could not be filled up in totality. However, in recent past, to
facilitate filling up of vacancies in NDMA, Recruitment Rules have been framed for
Group ‘A’ and Group ‘C’ posts including that of Financial Advisor and efforts are
being made for filling up vacancies in NDMA.
• For efficient implementation of National Cyclone Risk Mitigation Project, which is an
externally aided project, and being implemented by NDMA, a Project Director (Joint
Secretary equivalent) has been selected and has taken over the responsibility.

95
• Financial powers for various items have already been delegated to NDMA from time
to time for better functioning.
• Further, to examine and review the Disaster Management Act, 2005, a Task Force has
already been constituted and it is examining/ reviewing the Act threadbare by way of
developing questionnaires for inviting comments of stakeholders which shall be
followed up with workshops at regional and national levels, examining Acts and best
practices in disaster management globally and considering its adoption in Indian
scenario. Those Questionnaire include various questions on the functions, roles,
responsibilities, institutional structure of NDMA, its synergy with MHA & other
institutions of Government of India etc. on which views & comments of various
stakeholders have been invited. Thereafter, the Task Force will suggest necessary
modifications/ amendments, whatever is required in Disaster Management Act, 2005 so
as to make the Act more forceful and implementable in the present day situation. It
may suggest steps/changes in the provision for making NDMA more efficient.
5.1.9 On the issue of providing deputation allowance to the personnel of NDRF, the Ministry has
furnished the following reply:
“The proposal relating to providing deputation allowance to the personnel of NDRF was
forwarded to the Department of Expenditure, Ministry of Finance for approval. The
Ministry of Finance examined the proposal for sanction of deputation allowance to NDRF
personnel. However, it was not accepted by MoF due to the reasons that the deployment of
NDRF has been made on deemed deputation basis for which no deputation allowance is
granted. It further observed that recruitment of appointment to a post outside the normal
field of duty with due reference to the specialized skills or experience which is a pre-
requisite for such an appointment have not been indicated and there is no material to
conclude that the personnel deployed were specially chosen for the task having regard to
any special skills, experience or qualifications possessed by them which justified their
appointment vis-à-vis their colleagues performing similar tasks. Further there is no
recommendation from the nodal Department, DoPT, dealing with the grant of deputation
allowance. DoP&T was also consulted which did not agree with the justification advanced
by NDMA and NDRF . NDMA and NDRF have been advised to prepare a fresh proposal
with proper justification for issues flagged by the Ministry of Finance/ DoPT for revisiting
the issue of deputation allowance.
5.1.10 In view of the vulnerable geo climatic condition of the country, the Committee
understands the need for disaster preparedness at the grass root level. The Committee
takes note of the fact that most of the new porjects such as National Cyclone Mitigation
Project, Earthquake Risk Mitigation Project etc. are at the rudimentary stages and it seems
it would take considerable time for these new projects to take off.
5.1.11 The Committee is constrained to note that a specialized force like National Disaster
Response Force (NDRF) has 10 battalions only for entire country. In a vast country like
India which is vulnerable to natural and man-made disasters, it is needed to raise more
battalions of this force. The Committee feels that merely advising the States/UTs to have
their own Specialist Response Force for responding to disasters will not serve the purpose.
The Committee impresses upon the States/UTs to set up their own specialised force and
train them for disaster preparedness and NDMA should play a pivotal role in helping the
State/UT Governments in this regard.
5.1.12 The Committee, recommends that the offices and residential infrastructures of NDRF
should be put in place at the earliest by enhancing allocation in 2012-13 at RE stage.

96
Regarding provision for deputation allowance to the personnel of NDRF, the Committee is
of the view that the Department of Expenditure, Ministry of Finance may consider this
proposal and approve for this allowance as it would work as a motivating factor for them.
5.1.13 The Committee notes that to strengthen the process Disaster Risk Reduction (DRR),
one of the various efforts contemplated is to mainstreaming corporate social responsibility
in DRR. A mock drill was conducted by Delhi Disaster Management Authority in
collaboration with NDMA and preceding this, a major earth quake preparedness exercise was
also under taken. The Committee welcomes this move and recommends that necessary steps
may be taken to rectify the shortcomings noticed during the mock drills. The Committee
also notes that NDMA is in the process of preparing a template for conducting such mock
drills across the country on the basis of the experience gained. The Committee recommends
that the template may be prepared at the earliest for the benefit of all the State
Governments.
5.1.14 The Committee appreciates that the NIDM has been helping the State/UT
Governments in organising Mock Drill across the country which will go a long way in
disaster mitigation. The Committee commends the initiative taken by the Centre to evolve
the National Platform on Disaster Risk Reduction (NPDRR) which aims at bringing together
organizations and individuals in diverse disciplines and sectors and assemble, synthesize,
manage and further disseminate academic as well as practical knowledge on disaster
management in all sectors including policy making and development plans of the Government.
5.1.15 The Committee notes with displeasure that NIDM is still functioning from a rented
premises of Indian Institute of Public Administration. The Committee is unable to
understand the inordinate delay in the matter of providing land to the Institute. Now that
the land has been identified by Delhi Development Authority, the Government should take
possession of the land at the earliest and start construction of the building without further
delay. The Committee further recommends that all the 124 posts in NDMA be filled up at
the earliest. The Committee also recommends that review of the Disaster Management Act
may be completed at the earliest so as to have a more efficient and implementable
legislation in the country.
SOCIAL SECURITY AND WELFARE REHABILITATION
5.2 Under the Indo-Sri Lanka Agreements, the persons of Indian origin in Sri Lanka granted
Indian Citizenship, are to be repatriated to India and provided relief and rehabilitation assistance.
The budget provision is for providing relief and rehabilitation assistance to these repatriates and also
includes, inter-alia, provision for assistance and loan to Repatriates Cooperative Finance
Development Bank and loans and advances to State Governments and Union Territories involved in
their resettlement. The major part of the provision is are for providing relief assistance to refugees
from Sri Lanka, who are staying in camps, as also for staff expenditure.
5.2.1 The Ministry was asked to explain the reasons for variation i.e. Rs.30.00 crore in BE 2011-12
which was increased to Rs.34.93 crore in RE 2011-12 for Relief & Rehabilitation of Sri Lankan
refugees. The Ministry in its reply stated that the Sri Lankan refugees settled in 114 camps in Tamil
Nadu and one camp in Orissa are provided essential relief assistance on humanitarian grounds. The
Ministry has further stated that the relief assistance includes provision of subsidized rice, clothing
materials, utensils, transportation charges, cash dole etc. According to the Ministry, the State
Governments incur the expenditure in providing relief assistance to the refugees and subsequently
claim reimbursement from Ministry of Home Affairs and on an average, Rs.35-40 crore are
reimbursed on this account annually to the State Governments. The Ministry has explained that due
to budgetary constraints, only Rs.30 crore was provided in BE 2011-12, and to reimburse the

97
pending claims received from Government of Tamil Nadu, a provision of Rs.34.93 crore was made
in RE 2011-12.
5.2.2 The Ministry was asked whether the provision made in BE 2012-13 to the tune of Rs.50.00
crore would be sufficient for Relief & Rehabilitation of Sri Lankan refugees. The Ministry has
replied that the scheme of relief assistance to Sri Lankan refugees was reviewed in February, 2010
in consultation with State Government of Tamil Nadu and an expenditure of Rs. 29,51,21,000 for
infrastructure developments in the camps in Tamil Nadu was approved. The Ministry stated that
on the basis of past trends of expenditure on reimbursement of relief assistance to State
Governments and additional requirement of Rs.29.51 crore for infrastructure development in the
refugee camps of Tamil Nadu, a provision of Rs.69 crore in BE 2012-13 is required. The Ministry
submitted that a provision of Rs.50 crore in BE 2012-13 would be necessary to meet substantial
portion of anticipated reimbursement claim of Government of Tamil Nadu for providing relief
assistance to Sri Lankan refugees.
5.2.3 There has been a declining trend in allocation of funds for relief and rehabilitation of J&K
Migrants. The funds have been reduced from Rs.115.00 crore in BE 2011-12 to Rs.100.00 crore
in BE 2012-13. The Ministry was asked to explain whether the Government is satisfied with the
number of claims settled so far keeping in view the number of affected persons in J&K and how
far the Central Government has been successful in motivating the State Government to launch new
projects/Schemes for the welfare of J & K migrants.
5.2.4  The Ministry in its written reply has submitted that the various items of expenditure on
Relief and Rehabilitation of J&K Migrants are reimbursed by MHA from Security Related
Expenditure (Relief & Rehabilitation) - (SRE–R&R) on receipt of reimbursement claims from the
State Government. According to the Ministry, the items of expenditure relates to cash assistance,
free ration, Surrender Policy, ex-gratia payment to NoKs of J&K Police, construction of 200 flats
at Budgam, PM’s package for return and rehabilitation of Kashmiri Migrants to the Valley etc. The
Ministry mentioned that MHA has already accorded approval to the State Government for
implementation of the PM’s package amounting to Rs.1618.40 crore for Relief & Rehabilitation of
Kashmiri Migrants. The Ministry further mentioned that depending on the progress of
implementation of the Package and receipt of reimbursement claims for the State Government,
additional funds would be provided at R E stage after a review. The Ministry has also stated that
there is no limit on the various sub heads of SRE (R & R), and a reimbursement scheme, additional
funds, if required would be sought at appropriate stages to settle the reimbursement claims of the
State Government. The expenditure incurred by the State Government on various components of
SRE(R&R) is reimbursed to the State Government and as such, the uitilization of allocation depends
on the claims received from the State Government.
5.2.5 The Ministry was asked to explain the reason for not returning of migrants to kashmir and
the measures the Government has taken to ensure home coming of these migrants. The Ministry
was also asked about steps taken or proposed to be taken for rehabilitation of J&K Migrant.
5.2.6 The Ministry has stated that the following measures are being taken in this regard:–
• As a result of the recommendations of the Working Group-I on “Confidence Building
Measures Across Segments of Society in the State” , constituted after the 2
nd Round
Table Conference on J&K held in May 2006, the State Government formulated an
incentive scheme for return and rehabilitation of Kashmiri Migrants to the Valley.
• Accordingly, Hon’ble PM in April 2008 has announced a Package for return and
rehabilitation of Kashmiri Migrants to the Valley. The Package includes components
relating to housing, transit accommodation, continuation of cash relief, employment,
assistance to agriculturists/horticulturists and waiver of interest loans.

98
• The State Government is the implementing authority and has constituted an Apex
Committee for implementation of the PM’s Package.
• The State Government invited applications from migrants who are interested in
returning to the Valley. The State Government has taken up construction of 495 units
of transit accommodations out of which 335 units have been completed.
• This completed accommodation is being utilized by the employees appointed under the
PM’s Package on sharing basis as most of them have preferred to stay in cluster
accommodation. The State Government has also created 3000 posts exclusively for
Kashmiri migrants and so far, appointment orders have been issued in respect of 2169
candidates out of which 1441 have joined in the Valley.
• The State Government has informed that most of the groups representing the Kashmiri
Migrants have reservations in returning to Valley and settle at their native places. As
the numbers of organisations are very large, there has been no concrete consensus
among them for their return. However, their thinking has slightly changed after 1441
candidates joined in the Valley.
• The Government can act as facilitator and ensure that the re-settlement in the Valley
is smooth. The Migrants have to take initiative themselves for returning to Valley and
Government cannot force the Migrants to return. The State Government has reported
that one family has returned to the Valley.
• The State Government suggested revision of the PM’s Package due to price escalation
and to make the package more attractive to the Migrants. The State Government has
informed that the Apex Advisory committee constituted by the State Government for
effective implementation of the Package has endorsed the revised package. The
Revised proposal of the State Government duly approved by the State Cabinet is
awaited. The Central Government will examine the feasibilities of approving the revised
proposal of the State Government.
• The migrants who have been provided employment have returned to the Valley. The
families of the migrants who have been provided employment are joining them
temporarily. The Government is having a regular interaction with various organizations
for facilitating their return.
• The frequency of visits of migrants has increased after more than 1400 candidates
have joined in the Valley. The relations & families of these employees visited Valley and
their native places as well which has given some sort of confidence in them and such
steps may help in future for rehabilitation of these Migrants back to the Valley. The
process is certainly going to help the other migrants, in taking a decision about
returning to the Valley in due course of time.
5.2.7 The Committee is of the view that Relief and Rehabilitation of Sri Lankan refugees
should be given utmost priority and all the necessary relief items should be supplied to the
refugees staying temporarily in 115 camps to improve their conditions. The Committee
takes note of increased allocation to the tune of Rs.50 crore in BE 2012-13 for Sri Lankan
Repatriates and desires that rehabilitation process of these refugees should be expedited for
the relief and improving conditions of Lankan refugees. The Committee also recommends
that additional funds may also be provided, if necessary for taking up infrastructure
developments in the camps in Tamil Nadu.
5.2.8 The Committee notes that revision of the PM Package is underway. The Committee
recommends that the Prime Minister package should be revisited as a whole, to make it
more implementable and attractive.

99
5.2.9 The Committee is pleased to know that the Ministry has accorded approval of the
PM’s package amounting to Rs.1618.40 crore for Relief and Rehabilitation of Kashmiri
Migrants which would help rehabilitation measures  such as housing, transit accommodation,
continuation of cash relief, employment, assistance to agriculturists/horticulturists and
waiver of interest loans aiming for homecoming of J&K Migrants. The Committee is happy
to know that the State Government has invited applications from migrants interested in
returning to the Valley.
5.2.10 However, the Committee recommends that the Home Ministry should impress upon
the State Government for expediting construction of remaining units of transit
accommodation meant for J&K migrants. The Committee also recommends that 3000 posts
exclusively meant for Kashmiri migrants should be filled at the earliest. The Committee
observes that the efforts of State as well as Central Government for the return of Kashmiri
Pandit migrants to Kashmir are proving futile as only one migrant family has returned to
the Valley even after two years of sanctioning of Prime Minister’s package for their return
and rehabilitation. The Committee is of the view that it is the responsibility of the Centre
as well as State Government for creating an atmosphere where these migrants could feel
safe after their return to the valley.
TRANSFER TO UNION TERRITORY GOVERNMENTS
5.3 There has been an increase in BE 2012-13 over RE 2011-12 under Plan Head for Transfer
to UT Governments from Rs.977.31 crore to Rs.1640.89 crore. The Ministry has given following
justification for the increase:–
• The RE 2011-12 for UT of Puducherry under Plan Head was approved at Rs.273.07
crore. The BE 2012-13 under Plan for UT of Puducherry has been approved at
Rs.609.28 crore. The increase of Rs.336.21 cr. has been mainly under Normal Central
Assistance and Additional Central Assistance for Jawaharlal Nehru National Urban
Renewal Mission (JNNURM) projects.
• The RE 2011-12 for UT of NCT of Delhi under Plan Head was approved at Rs.704.24
crore. The BE 2012-13 under Plan Head for NCT of Delhi has been approved at
Rs.1031.61 crore. The increase of Rs.327.37 crore has been mainly on account of
higher allocation under Jawaharlal Nehru National Urban Renewal Mission (JNNURM)
projects.
5.3.1  The Government of Puducherry has a deficit budget and therefore, the Central Government
meets the gap in resources. The quantum of non-plan gap in resources is assessed in the pre-
Budget discussions held in the Ministry of Finance for each financial year. According to the
Ministry, the quantum of non-plan gap in resources in the Budget Estimates for 2012-13 has been
projected by the UT of Puducherry at Rs.1043.71 crore of which the gap in resources approved
by the Government of India for bridging the gap is Rs.585.00 crore which is 56.05%.
5.3.2 The Ministry has informed that in case of National Capital Territory of Delhi, there is no
non-plan gap in resources and therefore no assistance is provided to NCT of Delhi on non-plan
side.
5.3.3 The Jawharlal Nehru National Urban Renewal Mission (JNNURM) is administered by the
Ministry of Urban Development as well by the Ministry of Housing & Urban Poverty Alleviation
in respect of schemes/projects falling under their domain. The Ministry was asked about success
of Jawahar Lal Nehru National Urban Renewal Mission (JNNURM) in UTs of Puduchery and
National Capital Territory of Delhi for which an amount of Rs.95.00 crore and Rs.700.00 crore,
respectively, has been earmarked in BE 2012-13. The Ministry has replied as under:–

100
“The projects approved under JNNURM for Puducherry included Underground Sewerage &
Solid Waste Management in Puducherry; Augmentation of the sources of potable water in
Yanam region; construction of dwelling units for houseless urban poor/slum dwellers along
with infrastructure facilities for better living. With the Central Assistance under JNNURM,
UT of Puducherry could implement mega projects for improving the quality of life of the
citizens of the UT. The projects approved under JNNURM for NCT of Delhi are being
implemented successfully. About 9 projects involving cost of Rs.1090 crore have been
completed; 1500 DTC buses at a cost of Rs.765 crore have been purchased; Rehabilitation
Trunk Sewerage at a cost of Rs.253 crore have been implemented. Other projects are in
progress. Under Basic Services for Urban Poor, 14,000 EWS houses have been completed
and balance 54,000 houses are in progress.”
5.3.4 The Committee takes note of the enhanced allocations to the tune of Rs. 1640.89
crore in BE 2012-13 under Plan Head for Transfer to UT Governments from Rs.977.31
crore in RE 2011-12. The Committee notes that most of the projects under JNNURM in
NCT of Delhi are being implemented successfully. The Committee, however, recommends
that the projects such as Underground Sewerage & Solid Waste Management in
Puducherry; augmentation of the sources of potable water in Yanam region; construction
of dwelling units for houseless urban poor/slum dwellers along with infrastructure
facilities for better living etc. should take off at the earliest which would help improve
the quality of life of people in Puducherry.
5.3.5 The Committee feels that tourism has great potential for the growth of the
economy of the UTs particularly Andaman and Nicobar Islands, Lakshadweep Islands and
Daman and Diu. The Committee has been given to understand that several steps have
been taken by respective UT administrations to boost tourism in their UTs. The Central
Government, on its part also extended special LTC facility to Andaman and Nicobar
Islands for the Government Employees for two years after Tsunami devastation. The
Committee in this connection would like to suggest that such type of LTC facility may be
extended to Andaman and Nicobar Islands again alongwith Lakshadweep and Daman and
Diu UTs.
DAMAN AND DIU
5.4 The Committee considered the Demands-for-Grants (2012-13) of the Ministry of Home
Affairs in its sitting held on 28
th March and 4 th April, 2012. During the meeting, a Member of the
Committee representing Daman and Diu in Lok Sabha raised several issues/queirs concering the
Union Territory.
5.4.1 Request for enhancing the budgetary allocation to the tune of Rs.1000 crore for the
development of Daman & Diu was made by the Hon’ble Member during the meeting. According
to the Ministry Plan allocation for carrying out developmental works in the Union Territory of
Daman & Diu is approved by the Planning Commission and the Plan allocations for the UT during
the last 3 years are as under:
Budget Estimates 2010-11 – Rs. 169.23 crore
Budget Estimates 2011-12 – Rs. 324.95 crore
Budget Estimates 2012-13 – Rs. 568.25 crore
5.4.2 The Ministry pointed out that the Plan allocation for the year 2012-13 is three times more
than the allocation for the year 2010-11 and there has, thus, been substantial increase in the Plan
allocation for the UT during 2011-12 and 2012-13.

101
5.4.3 The Hon’ble Member also requested to raise the financial powers vested with the
Administrator from Rs.20 crore to Rs.50 crore for Plan Schemes and from Rs.10 crore to 20 crore
for Civil Works. In the sitting held on 4
th April, 2012, the Home Secretary responded as under:–
“The financial powers of Administrators are to be increased, Sir. We have increased the
financial powers of Administrators in the year 2010; in some cases, we have increased it
in 2011. In Puducherry, Sir, the delegation of power has been increased to Rs. 50 crores
in 2010. For sanction of works, they have already full powers. In Andaman & Nicobar the
powers were increased from to Rs.25 crores again in July 2010. They have now sent a
proposal for increasing it Rs.50 crores and we want this to be approved. The approval of
sanction of works has been increased to Rs.25 crores in April 2011. In Chandigarh the
power to Administrator has been increased to Rs.50 crores in February 2012. For sanction
of works, we have proposed that it may be increased to Rs.50 crores. We have sent a
proposal to the Ministry of Urban Development. Similarly, for Dadra and Nagar Haveli,
in July 2010, we increased the power to Rs.20 crores. We have now received a proposal for
enhancing the limit to Rs.50 crores. We will have to go for this to the Ministry of U.D.
Approval for sanction of works was increased to Rs.10 crores in 2009, there is a proposal
now to increasing it to Rs.25 crores, which we shall do. For Daman and Diu, in July 2010
we increased the powers from Rs.10 crores to Rs.20 crores. Now we have received a proposal
to increase it to Rs.50 crores. Again we are agreeable to this. We will take up with the
concerned Ministries. Power for sanction of works in Daman and Diu was increased to
Rs.10 crores in 2009, now we have a proposal to increase it to Rs.25 crores which we will
take up. But we are further enhancing in some cases which include Daman and Diu, Dadar
and Nagar Haveli. This issue was raised by the hon. Members from these areas.”
5.4.4 The Ministry in its written reply has further submitted as under:–
“The delegation of financial powers to the Administrator of the Union Territories of Dadra
& Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu for approval of Plan Schemes/Projects was raised from
Rs.10 crore to Rs.20 crore on 05.07.2010. Similarly, the delegation for approval for
sanction of works was raised from Rs.2.40 crore to Rs.10 crore on 26.02.2009. The proposal
received from the UT Administration of Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu for
enhancing the delegation of financial powers for approval of Plan Schemes/Projects upto
Rs.50 crore and that for sanction of works upto Rs.25 crore is under process. The proposal
will be referred to the Ministry of Finance for their approval and ther eafter for concurrence
of the Director General of Works in the Ministry of Urban Development for enhancing the
delegation for sanction of works.”
5.4.5 The Member raised the issue of contaminated water emanating from the factories of the
Vapi G.I.D.C. being released in the Ganga river of Daman. He added that no ban has been imposed
on this release so far and requested to impose a ban on the release of contaminated water in the
Ganga river in the interest of general public. The Ministry has replied that UT Administration of
Daman & Diu has informed that the issue of release of industrial effluents by chemical units in
Vapi had been studied and report had been prepared by NIO (National Institute of Oceanography).
According to the Ministry, the NIO had suggested to lay a pipe line of 7 Kms. inside the Arabian
Sea so that the industrial effluents which will be released by the common effluent treatment plant
set up by the industrial units of Vapi, is discharged directly deep into the sea where it can be
properly diluted. The Ministry also submitted that due to resistance from the fishing community of
Daman, permission to lay this pipe line into the sea has been with held. The Ministry further
submitted that the matter was discussed on 22
nd March 2012 with representative of Central
Pollution Control Board and it was suggested that an Autonomous Management Committee could
be constituted as a registered society to supervise operations of the common effluent treatment

102
plant and the Committee should have representation from Central Pollution Control Board, Gujarat
State Pollution Control Board, Vapi Industrial Association, Fisherman community of both Valsad and
Daman district, Daman Pollution Control Committee and from a National NGO. The Ministry has
further informed that a joint meeting of all stake holders is proposed to be convened by the end
of April for an appropriate decision to break the logjam.
5.4.6  The Member also raised the issue that the process of preparing lay-out for converting CRZ
into CRZ-2 is very slow and also regarding relaxation to Daman and Diu based hotels in CRZ so
as to promote hotel business and provide greater facilities to tourists. The Ministry in its written
reply has stated that UT Administration of Daman and Diu has informed that the Institute of
Remote Sensing, Anna University, Chennai have been engaged on 24
th Feb 2012 for ground level
survey and demarcation of High Tide Line (HTL) and Low Tide Line (LTL) for preparing coastal
zone map showing different zones viz. CRZ-1,CRZ-2, and indicating existing land use. The Ministry
has further stated that the main demand is for relaxation from CRZ regulation by putting Daman
and Diu into CRZ-2 and not in CRZ 1. A proposal will be sent by the UT Administration to
Government of India on receipt of coastal zonal maps from consultants.
5.4.7 Regarding the slow pace of work relating to some new bridges proposed for Daman, the
Ministry, in its written reply, has submitted that UT Administration of Daman & Diu has informed
that seven bridges across various rivers and one signature bridge (cable stayed) connecting Moti
Daman and Nani Daman are under various stages of implementation. According to the Ministry, an
amount of Rs. 58.25 crore has been earmarked in the Annual Plan 2012-13. For this purpose,
Consultants have been appointed, reports after initial surveys have been prepared and alignments
have been finalized in consultation with Hon’ble MPs and other public representatives. The Ministry
has informed that detailed estimates are being prepared by the consultants and tendering for one
bridge has already been done. In case of some bridges, the other side falls in Gujarat. The Ministry
has mentioned that to sort out the issues of land acquisition, a meeting with elected representatives
of neighboring districts of Gujarat along with representatives of Daman was convened on 16
th
February 2012 and further action for acquisition of land is being taken. The Ministry has also
mentioned that two bridges are proposed to be constructed in Diu district and Consultant for these
has been appointed. Efforts are being made to expedite implementation work.
5.4.8 Pertaining to the suggestion that in the Ganga river of Daman, ferry boat may be introduced
for tourists and cruise may also be commissioned from Mumbai to Diu via Daman, to attract a
large number of tourists, the Ministry of Home Affairs stated that UT Administration of Daman &
Diu has informed that the demand for allowing tourist boats in Daman Ganga river will be examined
in the context of available infrastructure. To promote cruise ships based tourism between Daman
& Diu and between Mumbai and Daman, berthing facilities for cruise ships have to be developed
both in Daman & Diu. A project has been included in the Annual Plan and dredging of the river
mouth of Daman Ganga river will be carried out and the resultant silt/sand will be used for
developing a minor port with berthing facility for cruise ships. Similarly, in Diu, dredging of the
sea bed near Vanakbara is proposed for the similar work. Consultants have been appointed for pre
monsoon /post monsoon studies of sea waves structure for both these projects and an amount of
Rs.80 crore has been earmarked in the Annual Plan 2012-13 for this purpose.
5.4.9  There was also a suggestion to establish modern hospitals in Daman and Diu, as well as
to appoint permanent doctors in these hospitals. The Ministry, in its written reply, has stated that
UT Administration of Daman and Diu has informed that the upgradation of the existing hospital at
Marwad with additional 100 beds has been included in the Annual Plan with a budgetary provision
of Rs.10 crore. The Ministry has explained that the issue of recruitment of doctor on regular basis
is pending with the Union Public Service Commission (UPSC) and is being followed up actively
and the Administrator had discussed this issue with the Chairman, UPSC on 4
th April 2012 morning.

103
5.4.10 During the meeting, the issue of non-availability of facilities like MRI, CT Scan, dialysis etc.
in the hospitals of Daman and Diu was raised. The Ministry, stated that UT Administration of
Daman and Diu has informed that MRI, CT scan and diagnosis services are proposed to be
provided at Government Hospital.
5.4.11 Relating to the issue of giving priority to drinking water facilities to Diu, the Ministry, in
its written reply, has submitted as under:–
“UT Administration of Daman and Diu has informed that Water supply Scheme costing
Rs.45.50 crore for Daman by bringing water through pipe line from Madhuban dam was got
approved during the year 2011. Detailed estimates were submitted by consultants on 27
th
February 2012. Technical bids were opened on 3 rd April 2012. Clearance from NHAI and
Railways is needed for crossing of pipeline at Vapi. Second reminder has been sent to them
on 28
th March 2012. The tendering has been done and further action is being taken. The
capacity of water treatment is also simultaneously being enhanced by constructing a water
treatment plant at Dhuneta with a total cost of Rs.13.88 crore. With completion, demand
for water in Daman for the next 20 years can be met.”
5.4.12 Recently electricity tariffs have been increased in Daman and Diu, therefore, a suggestion
was made for not imposing this hike on supply of electricity for domestic, agricultural, etc.
consumption and subsidy in the power tariffs should be provided to domestic and agricultural
users. The Ministry explained that UT Administration of Daman and Diu has informed that the
Power tariffs are being determined by Joint Regulatory Commission of UTs and due to increase
in coal prices, the power tariffs have been increased. The UT Administration is bound to recover
100 % charges paid to power suppliers and amount spent on distribution infrastructure.
5.4.13  During the meeting, the issue for implementing tax holiday for ten years in Daman and Diu
for giving incentive to set up industries was raised. In response, the Ministry stated that UT
Administration of Daman and Diu has informed that the matter will be taken up with Ministry of
Finance, Government of India for taking a decision in the matter.
5.4.14 According to the Committee, non-enforcement of anti-defection law in Daman and Diu has
allowed the winning candidates frequently to change the parties without any hesitation out of sheer
temptation and greed. Therefore, there is a dire need to pass and enforce the Panchayati Raj
Regulation which is pending in the Ministry of Home Affairs since 2010.
5.4.15 Though the Home Secretary during the meeting deliberated on the issue the Ministry in its
subsequent written reply has further stated as below:–
“The Panchayats in the Union Territory of Daman and Diu are governed in accordance
with the provisions of the Daman and Diu Panchayats Regulation, 1965, which was
substantially amended in the year 1994 in pursuance of the Constitution (Seventy-third
Amendment) Act, 1992. The existing Daman and Diu Panchayats Regulation, 1965 as
amended in 1994 does not have the anti-defection provision. With a view to providing
adequate statutory provisions for further strengthening the administrative, financial and
democratic framework for the Panchayat institution, it is proposed to promulgate the
Daman and Diu Panchayat Regulation, 2012 by repealing the existing law. The draft
Daman and Diu Panchayat Regulation, 2012 has been prepared in consultation with the
Ministry of Panchayati Raj and the Ministry of Law and Justice and is broadly on the lines
of Model Panchayat Act circulated by the Ministry of Panchayati Raj. The Fifth Schedule
of the proposed draft Daman and Diu Panchayat Regulation, 2012 contains provision
relating to anti-defection - both for Gram Panchayat and District Panchayat. The said
Schedule is broadly on the lines of the Tenth Schedule of the Constitution. The draft Daman
and Diu Panchayat Regulation, 2012 also envisages constitution of separate District

104
Panchayats for both Daman and Diu districts. The draft Daman and Diu Panchayat
Regulation, 2012 has already been finalized. It will be sent to the Cabinet Secretariat for
placing before the Cabinet for its consideration.”
5.4.16 The Member suggested for regularization of daily wage employees to which, the Ministry
submitted that the issue of regularization of daily wagers had already been considered by the
Ministry in consultation with the Department of Personnel and Training and the Ministry of
Finance. Accordiing to the Ministry, UT Administrations of Daman and Diu and Dadra and Nagar
Haveli were advised to take necessary action for regularization of daily wagers in accordance with
the instructions/guidelines dated 11.12.2006 issued by the Department of Personnel and Training in
pursuance of the judgment of a Constitution Bench of the Supreme Court in Civil appeal No. 3595-
3612/1999 etc. in the case of Secretary State of Karnataka and Ors. Vs. Uma Devi and others.
5.4.17 On the issue of setting up of Solar Power plants in the UT of Daman and Diu, the Ministry
has replied that  UT Administration of Daman and Diu has informed that solar power plants cannot
be set up in Daman and Diu for want of land which is scarce. However a new scheme has been
proposed in Annual Plan to promote solar power by introducing solar power panels in Government
buildings.
5.4.18 On the issue of establishing a Medical College in Daman and Diu, the Ministry has replied
as under:–
“UT Administration has informed that a medical college has been proposed in the 12
th plan
and a token provision of Rs.10 crore for land acquisition has been made in the Annual
Plan. The Collector Daman has been asked to identify the suitable land of about 25-30
acres within a radius of 5 kilometers of existing Government hospital which is also proposed
to be upgraded from 100 beds to 200 beds. Token budget provision of Rs.10 crore has been
made for this purpose also. A new plan scheme has been included for starting coaching
classes to prepare youth of Daman and Diu for national level competitive exams.”
5.4.19 The Committee observed that in the 12
th Five Year Plan there is a proposal for sewerage
plant at both Daman and Diu, which is very much needed. The Ministry stated that UT
Administration has informed that the WAPCOS have been appointed to prepare DPR and estimates
for sewerage system in Daman and the survey work has been completed. According to the
Ministry, the final report is being prepared by them and on receipt of DPR and Estimates, tenders
will be floated by UT Administration.
5.4.20 A suggestion was made for increasing the quota of Kerosene and Diesel to fishermen in Diu
as Fishing industry is the only major means of livelihood in Diu, the Ministry, in its reply, has
submitted that UT Administration of Daman and Diu has taken up the matter with the Ministry of
Petroleum, Government of India to direct OMC (Oil Marketing Companies) to provide additional
quota of kerosene to fishermen of Diu and the additional quota is likely to be released but not at
subsidised rates and will be as per actual prevalent market rates.
5.4.21 On resuming the operations of the passenger aircrafts from the Coast Guard Airport situated
in Daman, the Ministry has commented that the matter has been discussed by UT Administration
of Daman & Diu with the Commandant Coast Guard to start flights of small civilian aircrafts in
Daman and modalities will be worked out.
5.4.22 The Committee notes that the Plan allocation for the year 2012-13 is three times
more than the allocation for the year 2010-11 which is a substantial increase in the Plan
allocation for the UT. The Committee, hopes that with this enhanced allocation, the UT
Government would expedite development activities in the UT.  The Committee also desires
that the Government should raise the financial powers of the Administrator from Rs.20
crore to Rs.50 crore for Plan Schemes and from Rs.10 crore to 20 crore for Civil Works.

105
5.4.23 The Committee also makes the following recommendations:–
(I) The issue of release of industrial effluents by chemical units in Vapi being
released in the Ganga river of Daman should be resolved soon.
(II) The Ministry should look into the demand for relaxation from CRZ regulation
by putting Daman and Diu into CRZ- 2 and not in CRZ-I as it would help
promoting hotel business in Daman & Diu and increase tourists inflow.
However, while doing so environmental and other aspects should also be paid
attention to.
(III) Seven bridges across the rivers and one signature bridge (cable stayed)
connecting Moti Daman and Nani Daman which are at various stages of
construction should be completed in time bound manner.
(IV) T o pr omote tourism cruise ships based tourism between Daman and Diu and
between Mumbai and Daman may be introduced for tourists at the earliest so
that a large number of tourists may be attracted.
(V) The budgetary provision of Rs.10 crore for up gradation of the existing
hospital at Marwad with additional 100 beds should be completed in time
bound manner and the Ministry should impress upon the Union Public Service
Commission (UPSC) for recruitment of doctors. MRI, CT scan and diagnosis
services should be provided at the earliest in the Government Hospital of
Daman & Diu. Steps may be taken to set up a medical college during the 12
th
Five Year Plan.
(VI) Drinking Water supply Scheme costing Rs.45.50 crore for Daman envisages
bringing water through pipe line from Madhuban dam was approved in 2011.
The Committee recommends that all clearances and formalities be completed
and the project completed at the earliest.
(VII) The Ministry should impress upon the Regulatory Commission of UTs to
sympathetically consider the issue of decreasing Power tariffs in Daman &
Diu particularly for domestic and agricultural consumers.
(VIII) The Ministry should impress upon UT administration for expediting the
process of regularization of daily wage employees.
(IX) The Ministry may reconsider the demand of setting up Solar Power plants in
the UT of Daman & Diu and explore possibility of acquisition of land for the
purpose.
(X) The Ministry should impress upon the WAPCOS to prepare DPR and
estimates for sewerage system in Daman so that work on sewerage system
in Daman could be started as soon as possible.
(XI) The Ministry may take up with Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas the
matter of providing additional quota of kerosene to fishermen of Diu at
subsidised rates.
(XII) The Ministry at its level should impress upon the Ministry of Defence and
DG, Coast Guard to start flights of small civilian aircrafts in Daman.
SERVICEABILITY OF HELICOPTERS
5.5 During the oral evidence of the Home Secretary on Demands-for-Grants (2012-13) by the

106
Committee in its sitting held on 4 th April, 2012, the Chairman and the Members of the Committee
stated that 50 per cent availability of helicopters is extremely poor and this must be above 90 per
cent. The Home Secretary on this issue replied as under:–
“...to begin with the last point, one is about the serviceability of the helicopters. It depends
on two things, one is the availability of the spares and the other is about the workshops,
etc. For availability of spares and workshops, for both these we are dependent on the Air
Force. We have three MOUs with the Air Force. The spares etc. are procured from the Air
Force and most of the major repairs are done by the Air Force. But as far as availability
of spares is concerned, it is not that Air Force is not deliberately giving us spares, they
also do not have the spares. This is a problem the Air Force is grappling with. Helicopters
were purchased in 2003. As I said, there has been a disruption in the manufacturing
facilities of this type of helicopters and availability of spares for the Air Force itself is very
low. When I compared it with the Air Force, I had only pointed out that this is something
which is not limited only to the Ministry of Home Affairs, this is something which is being
faced by us as a country because we are dependent on a country which broke up and where
manufacturing facilities were dispersed. Some helicopters are actually 2002-2003 vintage. In
some cases they have been closed down and in other places they are near closing down.
The Ministry of Defence is procuring some of the additional helicopters and we have asked
them to add our requirements to their procurement.”
5.5.1 The Ministry further explaining the position made following written submissions:–
• 6 MI-17 1V helicopters were procured by Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA) through
the Ministry of Defence (MoD) in 2003. These helicopters are operated under the
Military Registration, i.e. as per the approved norms of Indian Air Force. At the time
of procurement of these helicopters, an assurance was taken from MoD that Indian Air
Force (IAF)/ MoD will provide the required support for operation and maintenance of
this fleet in terms of pilots, technical manpower, spares backup and repair facilities.
Accordingly, as per the agreed terms and conditions, three Memorandum of
Understandings (MoUs) were signed between MHA and MoD, after induction of this
fleet, for providing the required support.
• Though, IAF/MoD are making their best efforts to provide the service as per the terms
and conditions of the MoUs, however, some time they are not able to extend the
required support in terms of pilots, technical manpower, spares backup and repair
facilities due to their own operational constraints. The pilots as well as technical
manpower to handle this fleet is not available in the open market and has to be
necessarily sourced from IAF/MoD. As per the MoUs, the spares of this fleet are
sourced by MHA through the IAF/MoD. IAF/MoD have a much larger fleet, and as a
result they are able to negotiate better terms and conditions as well as price with the
OEMs. However, after the breakup of the erstwhile USSR, IAF/MoD are also finding
it difficult to source spares from different CIS countries manufacturing different parts.
•  Also due to their own increased volume of repair works because of the ageing fleet,
the Indian Air Force sometimes is not able to accord the required priority deserved by
the fleet of BSF. Though, the repair facilities have been established by IAF, they are
yet to establish the repair facilities of certain major components at their “3 Base Repair
Depot, IAF Chandigarh”. As a result, such components are still required to be sent to
the Original Equipment Manufacturer (OEM) overseas which takes lot of time.
•  Now, with the introduction of the Service Bulletin issued on 24
th December 2009 by
‘Military Moscow Helicopter’ plant for Mi-17 series helicopters, the Service life and

107
Service time between the two overhaul of Mi-17 series helicopters have been extended
in respect of certain components. Therefore, though the serviceability has been
improved to some extent, however the same is affected to an extent due to the non-
availability of the spares/components whose life has not been extended by the said
service bulletin. Also the procurement of these spares from Original Equipment
Manufacturer/Authorised agent have a long lead time.
•  As a result, presently 2 MI-17 1V helicopters (Z-4102 & Z-4104), out of a fleet of
6 with MHA, are in unserviceable condition i.e. Aircraft on Ground (AOG) due to non-
availability of the critical components/spares, including Aero Engines and Main Rotor
Blades. The list of deficient major critical spares/components for the two helicopters
are given below :–
Z-4102 has been grounded for want of following major components :–
(i) Aero Engines Quantity – 2
(ii) APU Quantity – 1
(iii) M ain Rotor Blade Quantity – 1 set
(iv) GPS Trimble Quantity – 1
(v) Control panel of FDR Quantity – 1
(vi) Radio Altimeter Quantity – 1
(vii) Main Rotor Hub Quantity – 1
(viii) Hydraulic boosters Quantity – 1 set
(ix) MGB needs to be extended from 1500 hrs to 2000 hours by Russian
Specialists under the contract of IAF.
• Out of the remaining 4 MI-17 1V helicopters, generally at least 3 remain serviceable,
while one at times remain under the scheduled maintenance. All four helicopters are
likely to become serviceable within 2 weeks. Concerted efforts are on to further
improve the serviceability status of this fleet. It may be mentioned that the overall Rate
of Effort of BSF fleet is comparable to/better than their IAF/MoD counter parts.
• The efforts made so far to improve the serviceability of this fleet are as under:–
/head2rightFor Operation & Maintenance of this fleet 266 additional posts have been
sanctioned on 12
th May 2011.
/head2rightNew Recruitment Rules for Group ‘A have been ‘notified on 11 th Nov. 2011
and Group B & C notified in Jan. 2012.
/head2rightThe recruitment process to fill up all posts on priority is in progress so as
to fill up the vacant posts in a time bound manner.
/head2rightWherever, IAF/MoD is not able to provide the required critical components
and spares, the process of procurement of these directly from the Original
Equipment Manufacturers/Authorised agents has been initiated in a time bound
manner.

108
/head2rightThe matter is being vigorously pursued with MoD by MHA for providing
adequate number of qualified technical personnel at the earliest for the
operation & maintenance of this fleet. MoD has also been requested to accord
priority in repair and overhaul of the critical components/equipments
deposited by BSF in 3 BRD, IAF Chandigarh.
5.5.2 During the said sitting, some Members raised the issue of growing number of fatal
accidents of helicopters which is much larger when compared to other countries. The Home
Secretary made following reply:–
“...unfortunately, the accidents that happened, involving prominent personalities, were
generally with Western-origin helicopters. I am not aware of accidents involving Mi-17IV
Helicopters. We have six such Mi-17IV helicopters. At this point, what we need to rethink
is this. First is, these helicopters are old. We have bought them in 2003. Modern technology
has progressed much thereafter. The second one which we have been thinking about and
which is occupying our minds is the fact that after sales the spares support from Russia has
been a problem. So, this is something which we have to consider when we procure the next
lot of helicopters. I am quite sure that the Ministry of Defence will also consider this. What
we have done this time is, we have asked the Ministry of Defence to club our requirements
with theirs. This time, I believe, the procurement which they are in the process of finalizing
is a Russian-origin helicopter. That is something on which I do not have authoritative
statement from the Ministry of Home Affairs. But, they have an open system of procurement
where they float tenders, different manufacturers submit their tenders from different parts of
the world, including the Western manufactures and they might have or might not have
competed. But, that is something which I still do not have any authoritative information
on this. But, with regard to helicopter accidents, for example, the accident involving the
Chief Minister of Arunachal Pradesh, the helicopter Acureuil AS-350 B-3 from the
Eurocopter (France) is involved.
5.5.3 The Committee is constrained to note that Helicopters are not functional due to
scarcity of spares. Such a situation should not have been allowed. Ideally India should
diversify its supplies in terms of purchase of helicopters. The Committee, therefore,
recommends that the Home Ministry should impress upon the Ministry of Defence to supply
adequate number of helicopters to it so that it never faces problem of non-availability of
helicopters alongwith a guarantee of spares.
5.5.4 The Committee takes serious view of the fact that despite MoU signed between MHA
and MoD, the Airforce is not able to extend the required support in terms of pilots,
technical manpower, spares backup and repair facilities to the Ministry of Home Affairs due
to their own operational constraints. When MoU was signed, the Ministry of Defence should
honour this without fail. The Ministry of Defence should plug these operational constraints.
CIVIL DEFENCE
5.6 Plan allocation for civil defence (3601) has been decreased to Rs.74.39 crore in BE 2012-
13 from Rs. 137.52 crore at RE stage in 2011-12. The Ministry explaining the reasons for this,
stated that there are two Centrally Sponsored Schemes, being implemented by the Directorate of
Civil Defence.
(i) Revamping of Civil Defence set up in the Country at a cost of Rs.100 crore; and
(ii) Strengthening of Fire and Emer gency Services in the Country at a cost of Rs.200 crore.

109
5.6.1 According to the Ministry, these two schemes are to be completed during the period from
2009-10 to 2011-12 and both the schemes have two components namely Grant in Aid to the States
(Head of Accounts 3601) and to meet the DGCD expenditure on publicity, capacity building etc.
to run the scheme. The Ministry has explained that due to slow progress of the scheme and
insufficient budgetary allocation the scheme could not be completed within the time schedule. The
Ministry has further explained that the scheme will be completed in the next financial year and
balance amount required to complete the scheme has only been provided in the Demand for Grant
for the year 2012-13.
5.6.2 The Ministry has furnished details of steps taken under the centrally sponsored scheme of
Revamping the Civil Defence set up by the Ministry for revitalization of Civil Defence during the
previous year which is as follows:–
• As against strengthening of physical infrastructure of existing State Training Institutes
in 17 States, renovation/ upgradation of physical infrastructure has since been
completed in 4 States and the process for procurement of equipments commenced in
11 States.
• As regards creation of New Training Institutes in 10 States, land already allotted in all
States except Chandigarh where new Training Institute is not feasible.
• For creation of 100 multi hazard districts, all States have already identified their
location for physical infrastructure and process for procurement of equipments also
commenced in most of the States.
• As regards pilot project for involving civil defence in internal security matters in 40
towns, training of 214 master trainers completed and 4280 CD volunteers also trained
as envisaged in the project.
• As regards training camps/exercise and demonstrations, firm commitments has already
been received from 14 States for contribution of matching funds on the basis of 50:50
between the Central Government and State Govt and funds also released to some of
the States.
5.6.3 Home Guards are basically a voluntary organization whose members are drawn from the
public. There is no Central Government legislation. Home Guards are raised/trained and deployed
under the Home Guards Acts/Rules of the respective States/Union Territories. Home Guards serve
as an auxiliary to the Police and generally help in maintenance of law & order except crime
investigation. It is for the State Government to utilize the service of Home Guards.
5.6.4  According to the Ministry, the Central Government reimburses 50% of the cost of raising,
training and equipping of Home Guards for North Eastern States excluding Assam and 25% of such
costs for all other States. The Ministry has informed that the duty allowance is reimbursed by
specified rates when Home Guards are called upon for duty. The Ministry has further informed that
as the rates for reimbursement are fixed, the demand for grants is made on the basis of number
of claims for reimbursement and the claims as received from the State Governments after being
duly audited at the State level. The Ministry has submitted that the allocation by Central
Government for this purpose in the last 7 years have been found adequate and met the requirement
of reimbursement to the States.
5.6.5 The Ministry has furnished details of funds allocated in BE, RE and actually utilized during
last seven years which is as under:–
