5.6.6 Police is a State subject as per the VII Schedule of the Constitution of India. The Ministry
has furnished information regarding various Community Policing Models being implemented in
different States of the country which are given below:
• Delhi Police – Neighbourhood Watch Scheme, Victim’s Assistance Programme
(Pratidhi), Rape Crisis Intervention Centres( RCIC), DOST etc.,
• Assam Police – Project Aashwas, Project Prahari and Nagarik Committees and
Community Liaison Groups,
• Madhya Pradesh – Parivar Paramarsh Kendra, Nagar Suraksha Samities, Gram Raksha
Samities.
• Bihar – Sambhav, Child Welfare Committee,
• Tamil Nadu – Friends of Police,
• Andhra Pradesh – Maithri,
• Kerala – Janamaithri Suraksha Project,
• Rajasthan – Jansabhagita,
• West Bengal – Resistance Groups,
• U.P. – Yuva Kalyan Yojna etc.
5.6.7 The Ministry has informed that a meeting of the Consultative Committee of Ministry of
Home Affairs on “Community Policing” was held on 25
th November, 2011, wherein Hon’ble
Members expressed their views on the issue and offered their suggestions. The Ministry has
further informed that Hon’ble Home Minister while replying to the suggestions of Members drew
the attention of the Members to the serious difficulties being faced by States in implementing the
community policing initiatives. The Home Minister further stated that the foundation of
community policing is the beat constable and there are large numbers of vacancies in the
constabulary across the country. The Home Minister further stated that the idea of a separate
‘Community Police Force’ is attractive and BPR&D was asked to prepare a concept paper in
consultation with Delhi Police.
5.6.8 According to the Ministry, the draft Model Police Act drafted by Expert Committee in 2005
was circulated to various States on 31
st October, 2006, for appropriate consideration as Police is
a State subject. The said Model Act inter alia  contains a number of provisions on community

111
policing like Village Police System (Section 68), Duties and responsibilities of Village Guard (Section
76), Village Defence Parties (Sections 77-78), Community Liaison Group (Sections 85-86),
Community participation in policing in Metropolitan Areas (Section 102). So far 14 States namely,
(i) Assam (ii) Bihar, (iii) Chhattisgarh, (iv) Gujarat, (v) Haryana (vi) Himachal Pradesh, (vii) Kerala,
(viii) Meghalaya, (ix) Punjab (x) Rajasthan, (xi) Sikkim, (xii) Tripura, (xiii) Uttarakhand, and
(xiv) Mizoram have either enacted new Police Act or amended the existing Police Act. The Ministry
has informed that by and large these States have made provision for Community Policing in their
respective Acts.
5.6.9 The Ministry has submitted that the project ‘Soft Skill Training for Police Personnel’ was
developed by National Police Mission and two training programmes were conducted at Punjab
Police Academy, Phillaur in December, 2010. The Ministry has further submitted that so far 3 ToTs
(Training of Trainers) and 7 Regular Courses have been conducted at Ludhiana and 250 Officers
have been trained. Besides, training Programmes have been conducted for Delhi Police also. The
Ministry has apprised that it is proposed to upscale the project to entire country after the conduct
of impact study. The Ministry has further apprised that another project on Police Community
Partnership and Overarching Model of Community Policing is under consideration for approval of
Expenditure Finance Committee (received on 23.3.2012). The Ministry has mentioned that National
Police Mission has demanded a Plan Budget for NPM related projects. The Ministry also stated that
in order to incentivize the implementation of Police Reforms by States, a Reserve Fund of 5% of
Modernization of Police Force Scheme’s annual allocation is proposed to be set aside for
disbursement to States who achieve benchmarked standards.
5.6.10 The Committee takes a serious note of the fact that allocation for civil defence
(3601) has now been drastically decreased to Rs.74.39 crore in BE 2012-13 from Rs. 137.52
crore at RE stage in 2011-12, which needs to be seriously looked into. Though the Ministry
has tried to explain the reasons, the Committee is not convinced with those explanations.
The Committee desires that the two schemes namely, revamping of Civil Defence set up in
the country and Strengthening of Fire and Emergency Services in the country may be
completed in the next financial year as claimed by the Ministry. The Committee also
recommends that the Government may expedite projects such as strengthening of physical
infrastructure in remaining States, creation of New Training Institutes in 10 States, pilot
project for involving civil defence in internal security matters in 40 towns.
5.6.11 The Committee appreciates Community Policing Models being implemented in
various States of the country. The Committee is of the view that the other State/UT
Governments should also emulate this model. The Committee, however, desires that the
BPR&D should prepare a concept paper on Community Policing.
MODERNISATION OF PRISONS ADMINISTRATION
5.7 ‘Prisons’ is a State subject under List-II of the Seventh Schedule to the Constitution of
India. The management and administration of Prisons falls exclusively in the domain of the State
Governments, and is governed by the Prisons Act, 1894 and the Prison Manuals of the respective
State Governments. Thus, States have the primary role, responsibility and authority to change the
current prison laws, rules and regulations.
5.7.1 Considering the “appalling condition” of prisons in the States, the Union Home Ministry has
initiated the process of formulating the second phase of the scheme for modernisation of prison
Administration. During the oral evidence of the Home Secretary on Demands-for-Grants (2012-13),
one Member of the Committee raised the issue of prison modernization. The Home Secretary in
response made following observations.

112
“The second phase of prison modernization, again, is something which we had proposed.
We proposed it last time. I would like the Committee’s support on this. We have again
moved it. We believe that it is necessary. We need to help the States to modernize prisons.
In many States, there is still a lot of overcrowding. In many States, the condition is very
bad and the States just do not have enough funds to modernize.”
5.7.2 The Committee understands that the State/UT Government cannot go beyond a limit
in modernizing their prisons due to financial constraints. Keeping in view the appalling
condition of various jails in the country, the Committee is of the view that the Second
Phase of the scheme of modernisation of prison administration should be given approval at
the earliest because a lot of work is yet to be done through the scheme.  The Committee,
therefore, strongly recommends that the Planning Commission and the Department of
Expenditure should give attention to this and approve the proposal of the Ministry of Home
Affairs for Second Phase of scheme of modernization of prisons at the earliest.

113
113
OBSERV ATIONS/CONCLUSIONS AND RECOMMENDATIONS
OF THE COMMITTEE — AT A GLANCE
TRENDS IN DEMANDS FOR GRANTS (2012-13)
The Committee notes that the Plan allocation has been proposed to be increased by
9.88% in BE 2012-13 as compared to last year’s BE while Non-Plan allocation has increased
by 12.87%. Though there is increased focus on the Police Housing Scheme and creation of
infrastructure for CAPFs, the Committee feels that the Housing Scheme for Central Police
Forces should be implemented and completed immediately. The Committee understands that
undue delay in land acquisition for housing projects should be addressed on priority. The
Committee also understands that Police personnel, who are mostly deployed in violence
infested regions and dealing with naxalism, terrorism and insurgency throughout the year
should be provided with proper housing. (Para 2.21.1)
The Committee takes note of the variation in the projected demand of the Ministry
under Demand No. 54 and allocation made in BE 2012-13 especially under Head ‘Police’. The
Committee is of the considered view that the allocation under Grant No. 54 (Police) may
be enhanced at RE stage as per the requirement of the Ministry so that the schemes may
not be affected. (Para 2.21.4)
The Committee appreciates introduction of expenditure management and feedback
mechanism in the Ministry to monitor the projects undertaken. The Committee
recommends that the Ministry should further enhance this mechanism to check leakages.
The Committee feels that the officials of the Ministry should be provided training on
internal audit, information system audit and fraud examination in acclaimed institutions.
While giving enhanced financial delegation to field organizations, efforts should also be
made to ensure that there are no leakages in the expenditure and delays in
implementation. (Para 2.21.8)
LEFT-WING EXTREMISM
The Committee observes that Left Wing Extremism (LWE) has emerged as the
biggest threat to the internal security of our country. Though, the causalities have come
down in 2011 the figure was showing upward trend till 2010. The Committee notes that the
Government has approved extension of Security Related Expenditure (SRE) Scheme for
LWE-affected States for another 5 years beyond 31
st March, 2012. The Government should
regularly watch implementation of the scheme. (Para 3.2.15)
The Committee is aware of the fact that the naxal affected regions are
underdeveloped areas with less infrastructure facilities. The Committee is of considered
view that schemes such as Integrated Action Plan (IAP) for Selected Tribal and Backward
Districts for accelerated development and Security Related Expenditure (SRE) Scheme
should be implemented at a fast pace in the naxal affected areas. The Committees desires
that the Government should make efforts for implementing the flagship programmes in a
proper manner to ensure that the targeted groups are benefited. The Committee believes
that providing employment opportunities to disgruntled youths, good governance and proper
implementation of major development schemes in the areas dominated by the Maoists, can
check their growth. Development is the panacea for the ills of LWE areas. All actions

114
should be directed towards development. The Government, therefore, should work in tandem
with NGOs, local bodies, peoples representatives to spread awareness among the masses
against the outdated ideology of Maoists which is undemocratic and which believes that
power comes through barrel of the gun. (Para 3.2.16)
The Government should also explore the channels/agencies such as NGOs/Social
Activists who can build pressure on the Maoists to eschew violence and come to the talking
table and join the mainstream. The Committee feels that the schemes implemented by the
Government are not yet yielding the desired results. Government may consider
strengthening security forces of States on the lines of greyhounds in Andhra Pradesh. Joint
elite forces can also be an alternative. (Para 3.2.17)
 The Committee views that although the multi-pronged development strategy is
required to check spread of Left Wing Extremism, the Government should also provide
required training to the Central Police Forces as well as state police forces and modernize
them. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the police personnel should be trained
properly before sending them to the naxal affected areas. The Committee also recommends
that these personnel should be equipped with all necessary modern weapons and gadgets.
(Para 3.2.18)
NORTH EAST
The Committee notes that the Government of India has initiated talks with some
erstwhile underground insurgent groups which could help establish peace in the region. The
Committee also takes note of the fact that the Government of India has initiated talks with
some friendly neighbouring countries to check these insurgent groups which had earlier
become safe haven for them. But the steps taken have not yet yielded the desired results.
Though, there is a separate Ministry for development of North East region, the insurgent
activities are unabated. The Committee feels that there needs to be a change in the
strategy adopted to deal with North East insurgency and militancy. Steps are required to be
taken to bring the people into the mainstream of the society. (Para 3.3.8)
The Committee is of the view that counter-insurgency strategies in the North-East
should be multi-pronged. Developmental Ministries of the Government of India in tandem
with concerned State Governments of North-East can play effective role in countering these
insurgent groups. The Committee believes that fast pace of economic development in the
region can be an effective tool to bring misguided people into the mainstream. Besides, the
Committee believes that the police establishment, the army and paramilitary forces engaged
in counter-insurgency operations in the region should be given special training. In all trade
discussions with the countries bordering North East States, a clause should be included on
closure of training camps for the insurgent groups being run on their land. (Para 3.3.9)
TERRORISM IN J AND K
The Committee feels that the Government should not be complacent with the
situation in Jammu and Kashmir which has improved in recent period in terms of the
number of accidents and the number of casualties. The Committee cautions that the
Government should keep vigil on the terrorist infrastructure functioning in “troubled
neighbourhood”. (Para 3.4.5)
The Committee is of the view that the people of Jammu and Kashmir should be
made to understand about negative consequences of terrorism and the Government of India
should initiate more developmental measures in the State to bring back the misguided

115
people who are misled into the mainstream. The Committee further believes that tourism
is the backbone of the economy of the State which has the potential for huge employment
to the population. The Committee, therefore, feels that efforts be made to boost tourism
in the State. More development projects and industries should be taken up in the State
giving it a development orientation. (Para 3.4.6)
COUNTERFEIT CURRENCY (FICN)
The Committee takes serious view of the fact that the country is flooded with fake
currency notes and large amount of Fake Indian Currency Notes (FICN) are being seized.
The Committee is concerned to note that despite the fact that the Ministry is working in
tandem with various other government agencies to thwart the circulation of FICNs, the
circulation of FICN continues unabated. The Committee recommends that all porous borders
should be plugged to prevent smuggling of FICNs. (Para 3.5.3)
The Committee understands that all border checkposts and major international
airports should be modernized to check the smugglers of FICNs. The Committee in this
connection, recommends that the Reserve Bank of India should constantly upgrade security
features of Indian currency and the people should be made aware on these security features
to enable them to easily distinguish fake from real notes. The Committee recommends that
note sorting machines should be installed at all bank branches to detect fake currency notes
as soon as they enter the banking channel. Since, Nepal and Bangladesh are main transit
points for smuggling of FICNs into India, the Committee feels that India should help train
Nepalese and Bangladeshi customs officials in identifying and keeping watch over FICNs.
The Committee also recommends that India should pursue these countries for the
installation of hi-tech luggage scanners at their major airports and land check posts so that
the baggages of passengers are thoroughly checked for counterfeit currency. (Para 3.5.4)
NARCOTICS
The Committee is of the view that  t rafficking of Narcotics, as an organised cross
border crime has now become a threat to internal security of India because of its diabolic
alliance with terrorist groups. Because these drugs are the major source for funding
terrorism and insurgency in the country, the Committee understands that smuggling of
narcotics is a matter of serious concern. The Committee is aware of the fact that the
economy of Pak-Afghan frontier is dependent on the production of poppy and cannabis. The
issue of drug trafficking needs to be taken up in bilateral and multilateral fora and
discussions. The Government should take more serious steps in this regard. (Para 3.6.4)
NATIONAL INVESTIGATION AGENCY
The Committee is of the view that NIA should work in close co-ordination with other
intelligence agencies such as Intelligence Bureau, Central Bureau of Investigation and
Enforcement Directorate. The Committee understands that without strengthening
capabilities of local police and building local intelligence resources, the country cannot fight
against terrorism. The Committee recommends that the cases entrusted to the NIA should
be dealt with at fast pace. The Committee also recommends that the office infrastructure
for NIA offices should be put in place as soon as possible. (Para 3.7.5)
NATIONAL INTELLIGENCE GRID (NATGRID)
National Intelligence Grid (NATGRID), is the first of its kind project, which will link

116
various databases between user (intelligence and investigative agencies) and provider
organisations (such as telecom companies) to enhance our counter terrorism capabilities.
The Committee desires that all physical and financial targets fixed in the current year may
be achieved. The project should take off as envisaged without any time and cost overruns.
(Para 3.8.3)
NATIONAL COUNTER TERRORISM CENTRE (NCTC)
The Committee takes note of the fact that the Chief Ministers of several States have
objected to the proposed constitution of NCTC without consulting them. According to the
State Governments, the proposed NCTC would infringe on the federal structure of the
Constitution. The Committee is also given to understand that in a recent meeting of the
Chief Ministers, this issue also was slated for discussion and some Chief Ministers objected
to it being included in the larger agenda and desired that it be discussed separately
threadbare. The issue is now reportedly slated for discussion on 5 May, 2012. In view of the
objections raised by several Chief Ministers, the Committee is of the view that the matter
may be discussed threadbare with the Chief Ministers in the next meeting and arrive at a
consensus before taking a final decision in the matter. (Para 3.9.4)
COASTAL SECURITY
In view of the 26/11 terror attack on Mumbai where attackers entered the city
through the sea route, the Committee understands the necessity of coastal security
mechanism. Keeping in view that India has a vast coastline of more than seven thousand
kms, the Coast Guard, the State police and the Navy have major responsibility for coastal
security the country. The Committee therefore, recommends that foolproof arrangements
may be made for coordination and synergy among different agencies. (Para 3.10.8)
 The Committee recommends that the Government should step up coastal areas
vigilance by strengthening infrastructure for patrolling and surveillance of the coastal areas.
Executive and technical posts should be filled in at the earliest. The Committee desires that
Coastal Security Scheme, Phase-II should achieve its targets in given time period.
Necessary legal frame work such as amendment to the Merchant Shipping Act, 1958 may
be completed at the earliest. (Para 3.10.9)
The Committee impresses upon the Ministry to complete issuance of Bio-metric ID
cards to fishermen as per the target. There should not be any delay in this regard.
Appropriate technology for Automatic Identification system may be selected immediately
after the study of the pilot project. Fast Interceptor Crafts (FICs) may also be procured at
the earliest in a transparent manner. (Para 3.10.10)
BORDER MANAGEMENT
The Committee feels that though cases of infiltration from across the border and
number of persons apprehended have decreased in recent period, the Government should
not feel complacent. India is already burdened with a large number of infiltrators. The
Committee recommends that security at patches vulnerable to cattle smuggling identified by
BSF should be tightened so that smuggling of the cattle heads from India could be checked.
The Committee also strongly recommends that remaining works of fencing and flood
lighting in border areas may be completed as per targets. The Committee also recommends
that issues such as acquisition of land etc. should be resolved as soon as possible to avoid
delaying fencing and floodlighting works. The Committee also recommends that shifting of

117
the border fencing within 150 yards along Indo-Bangla Border may be completed soon.
(Para 3.11.23)
In view of the fact that India has 15,106.7 km. of land border, the Committee
believes that securing it against hostile interests and taking measures to counter threats
such as illegal migration, smuggling, infiltration by terrorists, smuggling of arms, narcotics
and FICNs while facilitating legitimate trade and commerce across the border should be the
main objective of effective border management which is vital for internal security. The
Committee also recommends that construction of 1377 km road on Indo-Nepal Border
should be undertaken in a time bound manner. (Para 3.11.24)
The Committee has noted that various works are under progress in all the proposed
ICPs. Several targeted dates have been given for completion of various ICPs. The
Committee recommends that there should not be any cost and time overruns. All the ICPs
should be completed and they start functioning as per the targets fixed. (Para 3.11.25)
CENTRAL POLICE FORCES
The Committee notes that allocation for almost all the Central Police Forces has
been enhanced. The Committee is of the view that this enhanced allocation should be
utilized properly in terms of modernizing the weapons, gadgets etc. used by CRPF the
personnel. Besides, the personnel deployed in risk zones should be trained properly for
using hi-tech gadgets and weaponry. The Committee is also of the view that these armed
personnel should be given opportunity for firing practices at regular interval so that they
would be able to hone their skill of firing/shooting. (Para 4.1.12)
The Committee expresses its serious concern over large number of vacancies in the
para military forces especially at lower levels which could hamper anti-insurgency, anti-
naxal operations. The Committee expresses its concern that the forces are not able to fill
up the required number of posts inspite of the advertisements made. The recruitment
procedure needs to be relooked so that the vacancies can be filled up fully at regular
intervals particularly at constable level.  The Committee recommends that all vacant posts
may be filled at the earliest. (Para 4.1.13)
CRPF
The Committee hopes that the Ministry would utilize the enhanced amount allocated
in BE 2012-13 in modernizing the armed personnel in terms of weaponry, training etc. The
Committee takes note of inordinate delay in raising of additional battalions for which
sanction was made in 2009 itself. The Committee is of the view that the battalions proposed
may be raised at the earliest. The Government should also fill up additional posts of 486
for Intelligence, 27 posts for Dog School and 269 posts at CRPF Academy without much
delay. (Para 4.1.19)
The Committee observes that the Government has established the Counter
Insurgency and Anti-Terrorism School for CRPF personnel. The Committee also notes that
Risk Allowance for CRPF personnel deployed in 35 specified districts in Left Wing Extremist
areas has been increased. The Committee is however given to understand that the frequent
transfer/posting and posting of the CRPF personnel to such areas where they are not able
to communicate due to language problem add to their woes and adversely affect their
working proficiency. The Committee, therefore, recommends that a proper policy should be
formulated on transfer/posting of the CRPF. The Committee further recommends that in
order to give adequate relief to the personnel of all Central Police Forces a posting/transfer

118
policy should be reflected on ground. The Committee is of the view that personnel working
in CRP should be stationed in a particular place for, at least, three years without
disturbance unless they are hard-pressed to go to some other area because moving from one
place to other in a short span of time causes difficulties not only to them but also to their
families. The Committee is also of the view that these personnel should be posted to the
extent possible in the areas where they know the local language and culture so that there
is no communication gap between the locals and the CRPF personnel. (Para 4.1.20)
BSF
The Committee takes note of the enhanced allocation for BSF in BE 2012-13 in
comparison to last year’s allocation in RE 2011-12. The Committee hopes that increased
allocation would be utilized in procuring modern weapons, equipments, gadgets and
vehicles for BSF and/or enhancement of strength of BSF personnel in a time bound
manner. The Committee recommends that both physical and financial targets should be
met. (Para 4.1.25)
The task of raising seven additional battalions during 2012-13 out of the 29
sanctioned battalions may be completed as per the proposal. Besides, other sanctioned posts
should also be advertised and filled up at the earliest. The Committee hopes that
augmentation, upgradation of existing training facilities for BSF personnel would be
completed in time bound manner. (Para 4.1.26)
NSG
The Committee hopes that the increased allocation for Office Building under plan
Head would be utilized in proper manner for creation of world class infrastructure of NSG
Hubs at Chennai, Kolkata, Hyderabad and Mumbai and Regional Centre at Hyderabad as
proposed. The physical and financial targets should be achieved and there should not be any
cost/time overruns. (Para 4.1.35)
The Committee appreciates that the NSG Personnel are being given allowance of
25% as NSG Allowance and the Government has proposed an award of Special Duty Medal
for recognition of service in NSG. The Committee recommends that all necessary steps be
taken to ensure that the awards to the deserving NSG Personnel should be started from the
current year itself. (Para 4.1.36)
The Committee recommends that the sanctioned/proposed two new regional Centres
at Hyderabad and Kolkata should be established at the earliest. The Committee feels that
in the proposed plan, the whole northern and western part of the country is supposed to be
looked after by the Delhi Headquarters. The Committee suggests that Government may
consider setting up one more regional centre of NSG at appropriate location in northern
India. The Committee takes serious note of the fact that country’s counter-terrorist force
NSG functioned without its top operations commander for almost six months. The
Committee recommends that this kind of situation should not be allowed to emerge in
future which could have demoralizing effect on the force. (Para 4.1.37)
ITBP
The Committee takes note of the fact that the amount provided in BE 2012-13 for
procurement of modern and latest weapons will not be sufficient and the allocation made
for arms and ammunition (Normal) will be utilized. The Ministry may ensure that this
scheme may not suffer due to transfer to modrnisation. As the allocated amount considered

119
by the Ministry is insufficient, the Committee recommends that the Government should
enhance the amount under this head at RE stage, if necessary. (Para 4.1.42)
The Committee recommends that proposed new battalions, Frontier Head Quarters
and Sector Head Quarters and proposed training Centres may set up in time bound manner.
All the physical and financial targets should be achieved. (Para 4.1.43)
CISF
The Committee notes that the two reserve battalions which were sanctioned in 2010
are still under raising. The Committee finds that further delay in raising battalions would
stretch existing manpower of CISF. Keeping in view the fact that the CISF has been
assigned security responsibilities of all major airports and sensitive units of the country, the
Committee recommends that the battalions be raised without further delay. Further
procurement of arms and ammunitions for which Rs. 37.90 crores has been provided may
be done without any delay. (Para 4.1.48)
ASSAM RIFLES
The Committee is constrained to note that the allocated amount in BE 2012-13 to
Assam Rifles for the financial year 2012-13 is not matching with projected demand of the
Ministry. The Committee recommends that the Government may consider enhancing the
amount if required at RE stage. The Committee notes that the Government has approved
restructuring of Assam Rifles. The restructuring envisages creation of 1035 posts, three
Sectors Head Quarters, one IG Head Quarters, one maintenance group and one workshop.
The Committee recommends that the restructuring of Assam Rifles should be completed at
the earliest and in a time bound manner for which a periodic review may be chalked out
to avoid delay. (Para 4.1.56)
The Committee expresses its concern over the reported malpractices in purchases
made by Assam Rifles, particularly in the ‘less than Rs.25 lakh’ category which have
benefited some well connected businessmen. Though the Ministry has denied any such
malpractices, the Committee recommends that no scope should be left for any such
complaints. A thorough investigation may be conducted into such complaints. (Para 4.1.57)
SSB
The Committee hopes that the target of additional battalions proposed to be raised in
2012-16 would be adhered. The SSB personnel may be equipped with modern weapons,
force multiplier/surveillance/navigational/communication equipments, bullet proofing of
vehicles, induction of Light Armoured Troop Carrier, latest personal protective gears like
BP jackets and Helmets etc. as envisaged. All gadgets should be of the international
standards. (Para 4.1.62)
DELHI POLICE
The Committee is concerned over the fact that there are large number of complaints
for not registering FIRs by the Police Personnel at the Police Stations in Delhi. The
Committee apprehends that actual figures may be bigger than the number of complaints
came to light since most of the people especially poor and illiterate ones might have
returned without lodging their complaints which have not been reflected in the figures
during the year 2010, 2011 and 2012. The Committee recommends that there should be a

120
separate cell of Delhi Police for registering such complaints where representatives of NGOs,
local bodies etc. may be involved so that people trying to lodge such complaints would do
it. The Committee is of the opinion that non-registering of FIR complaint by Police
personnel should be viewed as dereliction of duty as well as unbecoming of the police
officers. The Government may consider setting up an authority to look into complaints
against Delhi Police. The Committee is exasperated over the fact that although 877 and 941
such complaints were registered against non-registering of complaints in 2010 and 2011
respectively, however, only 33 and 46 cases were found to be genuine. The Committee is of
the considered view that stringent action is required to be taken against the concerned
officers for non registering of FIRs. (Para 4.2.19)
The Committee takes serious view of the fact that 72 police stations out of 180 police
stations are functioning without regular buildings. It is very unfortunate that in the Capital
city of the country, the police stations are functioning without regular buildings. The
Committee recommends that the Government should take immediate steps to provide
regular buildings to these 72 police stations at the earliest. (Para 4.2.20)
As regards crime figures, the Committee takes serious note of the increasing cases
of molestation, rape and kidnapping during the last three years. The Committee is of the
view that prevention of crime against women should be a priority issue of the Delhi Police.
The Committee understands that most of the crimes against women go unreported due to
non-cooperating attitude of police personnel in Delhi. In view of this, the Committee
recommends that more and more women officers should be employed at lower as well as
higher level in Delhi Police so that female victims would approach them without hesitation.
(Para 4.2.21)
The Committee, takes note of the fact that the figures given by Delhi Police do not
match the cases reported in media about rape and molestation. Stringent action should be
taken against the culprits involved in harassing girls. The Committee understands that
merely counting the number of incidents does not justify the general perception. The
Committee believes that a mass campaign should be organised by Delhi Police against such
incidents. (Para 4.2.22)
The Committee takes note of the fact that the figures given by Delhi Police do not
match the cases reported in media about rape and molestation. Stringent action should be
taken against the culprits involved in harassing girls. The Committee understands that
merely counting the number of incidents does not justify the general perception. The
Committee believes that a mass campaign should be organised by Delhi Police against such
incidents. (Para 4.2.23)
The Committee takes serious view of the bomb blast in an Israeli Embassy car in
a high-security area which occurred in Delhi using sticky bomb. The Committee
recommends that proper security arrangements should be made by Delhi Police in
Diplomatic areas so that this kind of incident does not happen in future. (Para 4.2.24)
The Committee is of the considered view that Installation of CCTV surveillance
system at 38 locations in Delhi should be expedited. (Para 4.2.25)
MODERNIZATION OF ST ATE POLICE FORCES (MPF)
The Committee takes serious note of the fact that the BE provision for the
financial year 2011-12 under the Scheme for Modernization of State Police Forces (MPF) of

121
Rs.1111 crore was reduced to Rs.800 crore at RE 2011-12 stage and again in the BE
2012-13 provision for the scheme is Rs. 900 crore. The Committee feels that this lesser
budgetary provision is bound to affect modernization of State police forces. The Ministry
apprised the Committee that the funds are inadequate and they would move for additional
funds at RE stage. The Committee strongly recommends that additional funds sought by the
Ministry should be allocated at RE 2010-11 stage for modernization of State police forces,
which is sin quo non  for internal security. (Para 4.3.15)
The Committee understands that this police modernization scheme plays an
important role in modernizing the police forces of States/UTs. In view of this, the
earmarked provision for the scheme must be utilized fully and optimally by the concerned
States/UTs. The Committee suggests that the State/UT Governments should submit
utilization Certificates in time so that they could avail of the funds from the Centre under
the scheme. The Committee is unhappy to note that States/UTs have unspent balances of
Rs.47.83 crore for 2008-09 and Rs.194.76 crore for 2009-10 lying with them. The Committee
recommends that the Ministry of Home Affairs should take up this matter at Chief
Ministers’ level to impress upon the State Governments to fully utilize the fund under the
scheme in a time bound manner. (Para 4.3.16)
The Committee notes the details of State-wise sanctioned and actual strength of total
police force and the police population ratio and expresses its displeasure over the fact that
some States are having vacancies of even more than 50%. The Committee recommends that
the Ministry of Home Affairs should impress upon the State Governments for filling up
vacancies at the earliest. The Committee desires that the Ministry of Home Affairs should
link this issue with provision under scheme of Modernization Police Forces (MPF) and the
fund for States lagging in filling vacancies may be deducted. (Para 4.3.17)
The Committee agrees that Police being a State subject as per the Seventh Schedule
to the Constitution of India, the question relating to lesser or no firing practice by State
police personnel is not within the purview of Central Government. However, the Committee
desires that the Ministry of Home Affairs may issue an advisory suggesting all the State/
UT Governments to make their police force personnel undergo fire practices at regular
intervals as in the case of CPFs personnel. (Para 4.3.18)
The Committee also recommends that the Ministry should impress upon the States
to procure high-end weapons which could be from Indian Ordinance Factories or from other
resources. The Committee desires that the Home Ministry should place its requirements of
modern weaponry before Defence Ministry which could help improve products of Indian
ordinance factories. The Committee understands that in addition to modern training
facilities to State police forces, they should also be provided with modern arms,
ammunitions, equipment, surveillance devices, better communications facilities, etc.
(Para 4.3.19)
HOUSING SCHEME
The Committee takes note that the budget allocation of Rs.1,185 crore for
construction of 6665 residential quarters to accommodate personnel of various forces like
CRPF, BSF, ITBP, CISF and SSB personnel. The Committee also notes that the land has
already been acquired for construction of residential quarters planned for the year 2012-13.
The Committee notes that the tender process is over for construction of 11,460 houses
under PPP scheme. The Committee hopes that the Government would achieve its target in
the current financial year. (Para 4.4.12)

122
The Committee takes note of the fact that in the last three years against the
physical targets of 3570, only 2291 houses for CRPF personnel have been constructed. The
Committee expresses its displeasure over undue delay in the construction of houses for
CRPF personnel. The Committee therefore, strongly recommends that rest of the houses
should be constructed at the earliest and there should not be any time/cost overruns.
(Para 4.4.13)
The Committee notes the progress made in the matter of construction of
accommodation under PPP model and recommends that all procedural formalities concerning
construction of quarters in the 1
st lot be completed at the earliest so that work on this could
take off. Regarding construction of quarters in 2 nd lot, planning Commission may be
persuaded to commit funds for the purpose at the earliest. (Para 4.4.14)
The Committee feels that construction of 300 quarters in Dwarka is not sufficient in
view of the scarcity of accommodation for police personnel. The Committee in its earlier
reports had expressed its anguish on the approach adopted by land owning agencies. Several
proposals for allotment of land are pending with them. The Committee therefore,
recommends that the issue of the allotment of land for housing projects of Delhi Police may
be taken up at the Minister level. The Committee also desires that besides GDA, Noida
Authority, possibilities of land allotment may be explored in adjoining areas like
Bahadurgarh, Faridabad, Gurgaon, Sonepat in Haryana and State Government of Haryana
may be approached in this regard. (Para 4.4.15)
CRIMINAL INVESTIGATION AND VIGILANCE
The Committee notes with deep anguish that out of 27 projects of CFSLs which were
approved for Eleventh Five year plan, 10 projects had to be dropped on account of non-
viability. The Committee denounces this casual approach on the part of the Government
that these projects were found to be deserved for approval upto the level of a Five Year Plan
of the country and suddenly at the end of the Five Year Plan they were found to be
unviable. The Committee feels that the Government should have assessed the viability of
the Plans before taking up. This lackadaisical attitude is not acceptable to the Committee.
(Para 4.5.7)
The Committee is disappointed to note the plain justification of the Ministry that the
budgetary allocations in 2011-12 at RE stage was reduced due to delay in acquisition of land
at Pune, Bhopal and Guwahati for establishment of new CFSLs and expansion at Chandigarh
CFSLs, delay in recruitment of contractual manpower for ongoing projects. The Committee
understands that land acquisition is the base stage for any project envisaged and the
Government should keep this in mind while planning any project. The Committee,
recommends that proper planning of the projects on CFSLs should be made and concerted
efforts be made to complete projects within a time frame. (Para 4.5.8)
The Committee notes that the allocation of Rs. 78.61 crore in BE 2012-13 should be
properly utilized in a time bound manner for creation of facilities of DNA data base,
Regional Forensic Science Laboratories (RFSLs), District Mobile Forensic Units, Network
among CFSLs and FSLs etc. The Committee understands that upgradation of existing
CFSLs, FSLs and creations of new ones would go a long way in revamping our Criminal
Justice System. The Committee further recommends that efforts be made to set up Forensic
Science Development regulatory Authority (FRDA) at the earliest. Consideration of the
report of the Scientific Performance Audit Committee be completed at the earliest for
implementation in the Twelfth Five Year Plan. (Para 4.5.9)

123
The Committee is pleased to note that a number of States have taken steps to
implement the directions of the Hon’ble Supreme Court pertaining to separating Law and
Order Function from Investigation by Police. Committee is of the view that maintaining law
and order and investigating cases/complaints are entirely different things. Discussion may
be made with the State Governments on regular basis in this direction for an early
implementation. (Para 4.5.10)
EDUCATION, TRAINING AND RESEARCH
The Committee takes note of the fact that six zonal branches of CDTSs have not
been sanctioned by the Government, but only two new CDTSs (at Ghaziabad and Jaipur)
have been sanctioned under Eleventh Plan period to meet the training requirements of
Central States and Western States. The Committee does not understand as to why six
CDTSs were sanctioned by the Government initially but only two were sanctioned in the
Eleventh Plan period. Even they are also not fully functional. This shows the causal
approach of the Ministry. The Committee condemns this attitude and recommends that only
viable projects should be proposed. Efforts may be made to make the CDTSs already
started, fully functional at the earliest. (Para 4.6.18)
The Committee understands that the Government should also not only pay attention
on the arms/people training of Police Personnel but also train them for developing good
interaction and communication skills with the public; discipline, attitude, behavior,
responsibility and accountability quotient and motivation and morale levels. The Committee
recommends that the Government should further involve BPR&D which is the premier
police research organization in evolving a curriculum for sensitizing of Police Personnel.
Regular refresher courses may be held in this regard. (Para 4.6.19)
The Committee takes serious note of the fact that there is a shortage of 1300 IPS
officers in the country. Though a scheme of limited competitive examination is due to take
place by UPSC to appoint IPS officers directly from State Police Officers and Para-military
Police Officers to overcome this shortage, but this is not ultimate solution. Recruitment
may be done as per requirement in the Civil services examinations. (Para 4.6.20)
The Committee is of the view that the examination system for recruiting IPS
officers who head the Police Organisations of the States/UTs and Central Police Forces is
not proper. The Committee is aware of the fact that the UPSC conducts examination for
various group ‘A’ services and Indian Police Service is one of them. The people aspiring
for IPS, do not know whether they will get into this or not. In the same way, the people
not interested in IPS, do not know whether they will get IPS or other services. The
Committee understands that the candidates opting for police service have to have/should
have a different attitude which this examination system is not able to guarantee. It has
been seen that people who do not like policing get into IPS. The Committee recommends
the Government to explore the possibility of conducting a separate examination of IPS
where the attitude and aptitude of the candidate must be thoroughly tested besides their
mental makeup and inclination for policing. The Government may impress upon the UPSC
to conduct a separate examination for recruiting IPS officers and SVPNPA may also be
consulted in this regard. (Para 4.6.21)
DISASTER MANAGEMENT
In view of the vulnerable geo climatic condition of the country, the Committee
understands the need for disaster preparedness at the grass root level. The Committee
takes note of the fact that most of the new porjects such as National Cyclone Mitigation

124
Project, Earthquake Risk Mitigation Project etc. are at the rudimentary stages and it seems
it would take considerable time for these new projects to take off. (Para 5.1.10)
The Committee is constrained to note that a specialized force like National Disaster
Response Force (NDRF) has 10 battalions only for entire country. In a vast country like
India which is vulnerable to natural and man-made disasters, it is needed to raise more
battalions of this force. The Committee feels that merely advising the States/UTs to have
their own Specialist Response Force for responding to disasters will not serve the purpose.
The Committee impresses upon the States/UTs to set up their own specialised force and
train them for disaster preparedness and NDMA should play a pivotal role in helping the
State/UT Governments in this regard. (Para 5.1.11)
The Committee, recommends that the offices and residential infrastructures of NDRF
should be put in place at the earliest by enhancing allocation in 2012-13 at RE stage.
Regarding provision for deputation allowance to the personnel of NDRF, the Committee is
of the view that the Department of Expenditure, Ministry of Finance may consider this
proposal and approve for this allowance as it would work as a motivating factor for them.
(Para 5.1.12)
The Committee notes that to strengthen the process Disaster Risk Reduction (DRR),
one of the various efforts contemplated is to mainstreaming corporate social responsibility
in DRR. A mock drill was conducted by Delhi Disaster Management Authority in
collaboration with NDMA and preceding this, a major earth quake preparedness exercise was
also under taken. The Committee welcomes this move and recommends that necessary steps
may be taken to rectify the shortcomings noticed during the mock drills. The Committee
also notes that NDMA is in the process of preparing a template for conducting such mock
drills across the country on the basis of the experience gained. The Committee recommends
that the template may be prepared at the earliest for the benefit of all the State
Governments. (Para 5.1.13)
The Committee appreciates that the NIDM has been helping the State/UT
Governments in organising Mock Drill across the country which will go a long way in
disaster mitigation. The Committee commends the initiative taken by the Centre to evolve
the National Platform on Disaster Risk Reduction (NPDRR) which aims at bringing together
organizations and individuals in diverse disciplines and sectors and assemble, synthesize,
manage and further disseminate academic as well as practical knowledge on disaster
management in all sectors including policy making and development plans of the
Government. (Para 5.1.14)
The Committee notes with displeasure that NIDM is still functioning from a rented
premises of Indian Institute of Public Administration. The Committee is unable to
understand the inordinate delay in the matter of providing land to the Institute. Now that
the land has been identified by Delhi Development Authority, the Government should take
possession of the land at the earliest and start construction of the building without further
delay. The Committee further recommends that all the 124 posts in NDMA be filled up at
the earliest. The Committee also recommends that review of the Disaster Management Act
may be completed at the earliest so as to have a more efficient and implementable
legislation in the country. (Para 5.1.15)
SOCIAL SECURITY AND WELFARE REHABILITATION
The Committee is of the view that Relief and Rehabilitation of Sri Lankan refugees

125
should be given utmost priority and all the necessary relief items should be supplied to the
refugees staying temporarily in 115 camps to improve their conditions. The Committee
takes note of increased allocation to the tune of Rs.50 crore in BE 2012-13 for Sri Lankan
Repatriates and desires that rehabilitation process of these refugees should be expedited for
the relief and improving conditions of Lankan refugees. The Committee also recommends
that additional funds may also be provided, if necessary for taking up infrastructure
developments in the camps in Tamil Nadu. (Para 5.2.7)
The Committee notes that revision of the PM Package is underway. The Committee
recommends that the Prime Minister package should be revisited as a whole, to make it
more implementable and attractive. (Para 5.2.8)
The Committee is pleased to know that the Ministry has accorded approval of the
PM’s package amounting to Rs.1618.40 crore for Relief and Rehabilitation of Kashmiri
Migrants which would help rehabilitation measures  such as housing, transit accommodation,
continuation of cash relief, employment, assistance to agriculturists/horticulturists and
waiver of interest loans aiming for homecoming of J&K Migrants. The Committee is happy
to know that the State Government has invited applications from migrants interested in
returning to the Valley. (Para 5.2.9)
However, the Committee recommends that the Home Ministry should impress upon
the State Government for expediting construction of remaining units of transit
accommodation meant for J&K migrants. The Committee also recommends that 3000 posts
exclusively meant for Kashmiri migrants should be filled at the earliest. The Committee
observes that the efforts of State as well as Central Government for the return of Kashmiri
Pandit migrants to Kashmir are proving futile as only one migrant family has returned to
the Valley even after two years of sanctioning of Prime Minister’s package for their return
and rehabilitation. The Committee is of the view that it is the responsibility of the Centre
as well as State Government for creating an atmosphere where these migrants could feel
safe after their return to the valley. (Para 5.2.10)
TRANSFER TO UNION TERRITORY GOVERNMENTS
The Committee takes note of the enhanced allocations to the tune of Rs. 1640.89
crore in BE 2012-13 under Plan Head for Transfer to UT Governments from Rs.977.31 crore
in RE 2011-12. The Committee notes that most of the projects under JNNURM in NCT of
Delhi are being implemented successfully. The Committee, however, recommends that the
projects such as Underground Sewerage and Solid Waste Management in Puducherry;
augmentation of the sources of potable water in Y anam region; construction of dwelling
units for houseless urban poor/slum dwellers along with infrastructure facilities for better
living etc. should take off at the earliest which would help improve the quality of life of
people in Puducherry. (Para 5.3.4)
The Committee feels that tourism has great potential for the growth of the economy
of the UTs particularly Andaman and Nicobar Islands, Lakshadweep Islands and Daman and
Diu. The Committee has been given to understand that several steps have been taken by
respective UT administrations to boost tourism in their UTs. The Central Government, on
its part also extended special LTC facility to Andaman and Nicobar Islands for the
Government Employees for two years after Tsunami devastation. The Committee in this
connection would like to suggest that such type of LTC facility may be extended to
Andaman and Nicobar Islands again alongwith Lakshadweep and Daman and Diu UTs.
(Para 5.3.5)

126
DAMAN AND DIU
The Committee notes that the Plan allocation for the year 2012-13 is three times
more than the allocation for the year 2010-11 which is a substantial increase in the Plan
allocation for the UT. The Committee, hopes that with this enhanced allocation, the UT
Government would expedite development activities in the UT.  The Committee also desires
that the Government should raise the financial powers of the Administrator from Rs.20
crore to Rs.50 crore for Plan Schemes and from Rs.10 crore to 20 crore for Civil Works.
(Para 5.4.22)
The Committee also makes the following recommendations:–
I. The issue of release of industrial effluents by chemical units in Vapi being released
in the Ganga river of Daman should be resolved soon.
II. The Ministry should look into the demand for relaxation from CRZ regulation by
putting Daman and Diu into CRZ- 2 and not in CRZ-I as it would help promoting
hotel business in Daman & Diu and increase tourists inflow. However, while doing so
environmental and other aspects should also be paid attention to.
III. Seven bridges across the rivers and one signature bridge (cable stayed) connecting
Moti Daman and Nani Daman which are at various stages of construction should be
completed in time bound manner.
IV. To promote tourism cruise ships based tourism between Daman and Diu and between
Mumbai and Daman may be introduced for tourists at the earliest so that a large
number of tourists may be attracted.
V. The budgetary provision of Rs.10 crore for up gradation of the existing hospital at
Marwad with additional 100 beds should be completed in time bound manner and the
Ministry should impress upon the Union Public Service Commission (UPSC) for
recruitment of doctors. MRI, CT scan and diagnosis services should be provided at
the earliest in the Government Hospital of Daman and Diu. Steps may be taken to
set up a medical college during the Twelfth Five Year Plan.
VI. Drinking Water supply Scheme costing Rs.45.50 crore for Daman envisages bringing
water through pipe line from Madhuban dam was approved in 2011. The Committee
recommends that all clearances and formalities be completed and the project
completed at the earliest.
VII. The Ministry should impress upon the Regulatory Commission of UTs to
sympathetically consider the issue of decreasing Power tariffs in Daman & Diu
particularly for domestic and agricultural consumers.
VIII. The Ministry should impress upon UT administration for expediting the process of
regularization of daily wage employees.
IX. The Ministry may reconsider the demand of setting up Solar Power plants in the UT
of Daman and Diu and explore possibility of acquisition of land for the purpose.
X. The Ministry should impress upon the W APCOS to prepare DPR and estimates for
sewerage system in Daman so that work on sewerage system in Daman could be
started as soon as possible.
XI. The Ministry may take up with Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas the matter
of providing additional quota of kerosene to fishermen of Diu at subsidised rates.

127
XII. The Ministry at its level should impress upon the Ministry of Defence and DG , Coast
Guard to start flights of small civilian aircrafts in Daman. (Para 5.4.23)
SERVICEABILITY OF HELICOPTERS
The Committee is constrained to note that Helicopters are not functional due to
scarcity of spares. Such as situation should not have been allowed. Ideally India should
diversify its supplies in terms of purchase of helicopters. The Committee, therefore,
recommends that the Home Ministry should impress upon the Ministry of Defence to supply
adequate number of helicopters to it so that it never faces problem of non-availability of
helicopters alongwith a guarantee of spares. (Para 5.5.3)
The Committee takes serious view of the fact that despite MoU signed between MHA
and MoD, the Airforce is not able to extend the required support in terms of pilots,
technical manpower, spares backup and repair facilities to the Ministry of Home Affairs due
to their own operational constraints. When MoU was signed, the Ministry of Defence should
honour this without fail. The Ministry of Defence should plug these operational constraints.
(Para 5.5.4)
CIVIL DEFENCE
The Committee takes a serious note of the fact that allocation for civil defence
(3601) has now been drastically decreased to Rs.74.39 crore in BE 2012-13 from Rs. 137.52
crore at RE stage in 2011-12, which needs to be seriously looked into. Though the Ministry
has tried to explain the reasons, the Committee is not convinced with those explanations.
The Committee desires that the two schemes namely, revamping of Civil Defence set up in
the country and Strengthening of Fire and Emergency Services in the country may be
completed in the next financial year as claimed by the Ministry. The Committee also
recommends that the Government may expedite projects such as strengthening of physical
infrastructure in remaining States, creation of New Training Institutes in 10 States, pilot
project for involving civil defence in internal security matters in 40 towns. (Para 5.6.10)
The Committee appreciates Community Policing Models being implemented in
various States of the country. The Committee is of the view that the other State/UT
Governments should also emulate this model. The Committee, however, desires that the
BPR&D should prepare a concept paper on Community Policing. (Para 5.6.11)
MODERNISATION OF PRISONS ADMINISTRATION
The Committee understands that the State/UT Government cannot go beyond a
limit in modernizing their prisons due to financial constraints. Keeping in view the
appalling condition of various jails in the country, the Committee is of the view that the
Second Phase of the scheme of modernisation of prison administration should be given
approval at the earliest because a lot of work is yet to be done through the scheme.  The
Committee, therefore, strongly recommends that the Planning Commission and the
Department of Expenditure should give attention to this and approve the proposal of the
Ministry of Home Affairs for Second Phase of scheme of modernization of prisons at the
earliest. (Para 5.7.2)

3.0 The Chairman welcomed the Members and apprised them of the agenda for the day. He
stated that the Home Secretary alongwith has been invited to make a presentation on Demands for
Grants (2012-13) of the Ministry of Home Affairs.
(At this stage witnesses called in)
4.0 Thereafter, the Chairman asked the Home Secretary to make presentation on the Demands
for Grants of the Ministry of Home Affairs for the year 2012-13 and also asked him to focus on
both the budgetary aspects and the issues concerning the major programmes and policies of the
Departments, Divisions and Organisations under the Ministry. He also asked him to focus on
programmes/projects where the targets could not be achieved or where the schemes were delayed.
4.1 The Chairman also asked the Home Secretary to update the Members on the major issues
concerning the Home Ministry such as ‘Left Wing Extremism’, ‘Cross-Border Terrorism’,
‘Circulation of High Quality Counterfeit Currency in the Country’, ‘Status of Census 2011 and
National Population Register (NPR)’, ‘Achievements of National Investigation Agency’, ‘Assistance
to the States for Modernisation of Police Forces’ and Law and Order situation in Delhi among other
aspects while making presentation on the Budget Estimates of MHA.
4.2 The Home Secretary then briefed the Committee about the general internal security situation
in the country particularly in J & K and North East. He underlined the bilateral initiatives taken with
neighbouring countries to counter insurgent groups operating from foreign soils. He also briefed the
Committee about the problems coming in the way of checking the growth of naxalims in the
country.

134
4.3 Thereafter, the Chief Controller of Accounts, MHA made a Powerpoint presentation on the
Demands for Grants (2012-13) of the Ministry of Home Affairs.
5.0 The Chairman and Members then sought clarifications on the following issues:–
• Reports of around 45,000 officers, staff deserting Central Police Forces. Factors
responsible for this desertion.
• Growth of Naxalism in the country and the Government’s strategy to check it.
• Obsolete weapons being produced by the ordinace factories and used by the police
forces
• Severe shortage of housing accommodation for the Central Police Forces and Delhi
Police.
• Slow pace of Border Fencing.
• CAG report of six MI-17 helicopters worth about Rs.125 crores procured in an
unplanned manner which are lying idle.
• Non-enforcement of anti-defection law in Daman & Diu which allowed the winning
candidates frequently to change the parties, and the need to pass and enforce the
Panchayati Raj Regulations which is pending in the Ministry of Home Affairs since
2010.
• Raising the financial powers vested with the Administrators of UTs which could help
overall development.
6.1 The queries were replied by the Home Secretary and other officers of the Ministry. The
Chairman then asked the Home Secretary to send detailed note/replies to the queries which were
not replied in the meeting. It was felt that Members may like to seek further information on the
detailed note/replies to the queries and therefore the Committee decided to further hear the Home
Secretary in its next meeting. The Chairman directed the Home Secretary to come for discussion
in the subsequent meeting to be held on 4th April 2012.
7.0 A verbatim record of the proceedings of the meeting was kept.
8.0 The Committee then adjourned at 4.20 
P.M. to meet again at 11.00 A.M. on 4 th April, 2012.

2.0 The Chairman welcomed the Members to the sitting of the Committee and apprised them
of the agenda for the day. He recalled that in the last sitting held on 28
th March, 2012, the
Committee had heard the presentation of the Ministry on Demands for Grants (2012-13). He added
that the discussion was not concluded that day and it was decided to further hear the Home
Secretary.
2.1 He further added that on the basis of discussion held in its previous sitting, written replies
of the Ministry of Home Affairs on questionnaires prepared by the Secretariat and other papers
relating to Demands for Grants (2012-13) of the Ministry have been circulated to the Members.
He requested Hon’ble Members to seek fresh clarifications from the Home Secretary and other
officers. They would first respond to the queries made by Members in its previous sitting,
thereafter, fresh clarifications could be sought.
(At this stage witnesses were called in)
3.0 The Chairman asked the Home Secretary to first respond to the questions raised by
Members in the previous sitting of the Committee, and then, the Members would seek
clarifications.
3.1 The Home Secretary then replied to the queries/issues raised in the previous sitting of the
Committee.
3.2 Thereafter, the Chairman and Members then sought clarifications on the following issues:–
• Trafficking of women, cows, fake currency and arms near the Sundarbans on the Indo-
Bangladesh border and incidents of molestation of women living in border areas by
security forces.
• Status of construction of roads on Indo-Nepal Border.
• Obsolete weapons being produced by our ordinance factories and used by the police
forces.
• Availability of proper hearing protection and eye protection to the police personnel
during the firing practices done.
• Increasing incidents of molestation, eve-teasing and rape of women particularly North-
East girls in Delhi.
• Reported mal-practices in purchases in Assam Rifles, particularly in the ‘less than Rs.25
lakh’ category.
• Vacancy in IPS and action proposed to be taken to meet the shortfall.
• Vacancy in Central Armed Paramilitary Forces.

138
• People joining IPS want to become IAS Officers and because they do not get IAS, they
opt for the second best service, therefore, need for a separate selection process for IPS
with the purpose to select IPS Officer commensurating to the attitude and physical
fitness required for the service.
• Formulation of the second phase of the scheme for modernisation of prison
Administration.
• progress regarding the case of Hafiz Saed and the initiatives taken by the Ministry of
Home Affairs in this regard.
• Less practice session given for shooting/firing to the personnel of Central Police
organizations as well as state police forces.
• 50 per cent availability of helicopters which is extremely poor and this must be above
90 per cent.
• Growing number of fatal accidents of helicopters which is much larger when compared
to other countries.
• Functioning of police stations in Delhi. Police-population ratio in Delhi. Police stations
being run in temporary accommodation like tents, etc. in Delhi and the plans to place
them in regular buildings. The crime situation in the last three years, item-wise like
murder, dacoity, looting, rapes, bank robberies, etc., of the last three years.
• Achievement made with regard to disaster preparedness. Deputation allowance to NDRF
Personnel.
• Any proposal to extend NSG hubs to other parts of the country.
• Any review done in the recent past to review coastal security situation in the country.
• Frequent transfer/posting and posting of the CRPF personnel to such areas where they
are not able to communicate due to language problem add to their woes and adversely
affect their working proficiency.
• Issues concerning UT of Daman and Diu.
4.0 The queries were replied by the Home Secretary and other officers of the Ministry. The
Chairman then asked the Home Secretary to send detailed note/replies to the queries which were
not replied in the meeting by 9
th of April, 2012.
5.0 A verbatim record of the proceedings of the meeting was kept.
6.0 The Committee then adjourned at 4.50 P.M.
